## 2026-10-13 T-V1-055 — delete document originals from object storage on purge (T-039 follow-up)

TASK: T-V1-055 (solo, 20-min box). T-039 (commit `62a55c8`) moved document originals out of `document_versions.content` into object storage and migration 0010 dropped the bytea column, but its own handoff flagged risk 1: the delete/purge path still only tombstoned DB rows, so after a delete the bytes stayed retrievable from the store forever — a privacy/retention hole, not a latency one. This ticket closes it: when a document is purged (all its versions), its originals are deleted from the object store, and the purge stays failure-honest. Scope: `packages/ingestion/dedup.py`, `packages/storage/*` (read-only reuse of the existing adapter), one NEW test file, this row. Start HEAD `99dcfa1` (28 `git status` entries); end HEAD `99dcfa1` (31 entries — the growth is foreign work landing under me, see the end-status paragraph). No commit, no deploy, no restart, no schema change by me.

CHANGE (my delta = exactly 2 paths, 0 foreign paths touched):
- `packages/ingestion/dedup.py` (M — allowed) — the fix, in three pieces: (1) `originals_prefix(workspace_id, document_id)` — the prefix that holds exactly one document's originals; (2) `delete_document_originals(*, workspace_id, document_id, storage_keys) -> int` — deletes every object this document's originals live under and returns how many actually went; (3) in `purge_document`, a second scalar read of `document_versions.storage_key` (same session, same transaction as the chunk delete) and, after the tombstone commit, a **fresh per-call registration of the `"originals"` store purger** through the *existing* `register_store_purger` seam, unregistered again when a document has no version rows (exactly the GRAPH_MODE pattern). No new report shape: the purger lands in the loop that already builds `dict[str, bool]`, so a failed delete is `"originals": False` and the caller's 204/207 logic reads it unchanged. Two deletions per document, deliberately: every `storage_key` Postgres records (exact, complete however many versions) **plus** a sweep of the document's prefix (catches a legacy row written before `storage_key` existed, whose object can only be found by listing, and anything under a key Postgres no longer tracks). Ordering: the objects leave **after** `await session.commit()`. Module docstring rewritten — the stale "Original bytes remain in `document_versions.content` until PD-005 moves them to MinIO" (T-039's risk 6) is now the T-V1-055 rule.
- `tests/unit/test_purge_originals.py` (NEW — allowed) — 12 tests: 11 service-free unit tests on an in-memory `StorageAdapter` + the smallest session double `purge_document` accepts (registry pre-seeded with no-op fts/qdrant so the real Qdrant/Postgres purgers are never lazily registered), and 1 `integration`-marked live test against the real SeaweedFS that self-skips via `object_store_env.apply_if_reachable()`. No DB, no network in the unit half.
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row).

WHY: T-039 risk 1, which its own row proposed as "a follow-up ticket to delete (or GC by `originals/{ws}/{doc}/` prefix) on purge" — this is that ticket, and it takes the prefix-GC option *as the safety net* rather than as the mechanism, with the exact recorded `storage_key`s as the mechanism: the prefix listing is bounded by the adapter's `max_keys` (1000) while the key list is not, so a pathological document with more versions than one page would still be fully deleted. The failure-honesty rule is the one review1 F2 already established (no false 204): a purge that cannot delete its objects is reported as a failed store, never swallowed. The object delete runs after the tombstone commit because the reverse order has a worse failure mode — a commit that fails after the bytes are gone would leave a *live* document that can never be parsed again, instead of a tombstoned one whose bytes are still there. Deleting `originals` (the only destructive call in the diff) is bounded to one document's prefix, and the unit test `test_purge_never_touches_another_documents_originals` pins that a sibling document in the same workspace keeps its objects.

REUSED (no new dependency, no SDK outside `adapters/`, no schema change, no `REUSE_MAP`/`NOTICES` delta): `packages/storage/interfaces.py::StorageAdapter` and `packages/storage/adapters/seaweedfs_s3.py` (boto3, Apache-2.0, behind the PD-005 seam) **read-only** — the first in-tree caller of the previously zero-caller `delete_object`, reached through `storage.originals.get_adapter()` (the same process-wide lazy client the upload path uses) and `storage.originals.originals_bucket()`/`ORIGINALS_PREFIX` for the key namespace, so the key scheme stays single-sourced and no vendor text crosses the seam; `ingestion.dedup`'s own `register_store_purger`/`_STORE_PURGERS` mechanism and its `store_purge_failed` log + per-store report (M3-001a / T-V1-007 / review1 F2) — the new store rides the exact same path, so `apps/api/app/documents/delete.py` (204/207, untouched this ticket) needs no change to surface it; `tests/fixtures/object_store_env.py::apply_if_reachable` for the live test's loud self-skip; `tests/unit/test_originals_store.py`'s in-memory-adapter pattern (same seam, no services).

BUILT: ~90 lines in `dedup.py` — `originals_prefix`, `delete_document_originals` (two passes, dedup, count) and the per-call purger registration. Reuse did not fit: nothing upstream knows where *our* originals live or that "purge" must mean "the bytes too". Deliberately NOT built: no new function in `packages/storage/*` (the allowlist says read-only reuse of the existing adapter, so the key/prefix derivation lives in `dedup.py` and imports the constants rather than adding to the storage package); no new report shape, return type, error code or reason code; no change to tombstone semantics, reads, or the 204/207 contract; no `delete_original()` helper in `storage.originals.py` (would have been the tidier home — flagged in RISKS 1); no derived-key guess for legacy rows (see RISKS 4).

VERIFY (exact, from repo root, HEAD `99dcfa1` throughout — I committed nothing):
- **The ticket's command** — `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_purge_originals.py -q` → **12 passed** (11 unit + 1 live, store reachable; 0 skipped). Re-run with the store cut off (`S3_ENDPOINT_URL=http://127.0.0.1:9 /root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_purge_originals.py -q -rs`) → **11 passed, 1 skipped**, skip reason verbatim: `SeaweedFS object store not reachable; run `docker compose up -d seaweedfs`` — determinism proven, not asserted.
- **The dedup/versions suite** — `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/integration/test_dedup_versions.py -q` → **3 failed / 14 passed**, byte-identical to the pre-change baseline captured *before* I edited anything (same three ids, same assertions: `_qdrant_count(...) == 0` at line 706, `'queued' != 'ready'` at line 406, dead `parser_for_mime` seam at line 1470 — all three are the documented T-V1-043 embed blocker / T-V1-031 seam / documented baseline, none in the delete path). Same file with the three known-blocked tests deselected → **13 passed**; my new file + that suite together → **3 failed / 26 passed**.
- **Pre-existing failures are not mine, proven by A/B:** I restored pristine `dedup.py` (`git checkout HEAD -- packages/ingestion/dedup.py`, my copy kept at `/tmp/opencode/tv1055-dedup-mine.py`) and re-ran `pytest tests/integration/test_security_placeholders.py tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py` → **the identical 9 failures** (2 xfailed). With my change the same two files → the identical 9 failures. My file was then restored byte-for-byte (`cp`, re-verified by `git diff --stat` = 131 insertions / 5 deletions, ruff + mypy + 12 tests re-run green).
- **Live SeaweedFS, real delete path** (script, real `put_original` keys, real adapter, no DB) → `REPORT-WITH-BOTH-STORES-FAILING: {'postgres': True, 'fts': False, 'qdrant': False, 'originals': True}`, `OBJECTS-LEFT-AFTER-FAILED-PURGE: []`, and `load_original` on the deleted key raising `OriginalMissing("original object is not in the store")` — i.e. two other stores can fail and the originals still leave the store, and the bytes are genuinely not retrievable afterwards.
- **Wider blast radius** — `pytest tests/unit tests/contracts -q` → **1221 passed, 0 failed** (81.9 s; was 1126 at T-039 + T-045). `pytest tests/unit/test_purge_originals.py tests/unit/test_workspace_policy.py tests/unit/test_originals_store.py -q` → **63 passed** (the two Q3 purge tests that assert the exact report shape `{"postgres","fts","qdrant"}` are green unchanged — my session reads only ever use `.scalars().all()`, which their fake session supports).
- **Lint/type/vendor** — `ruff check packages/ingestion/dedup.py tests/unit/test_purge_originals.py` → **All checks passed**; repo-wide `ruff check .` → **1 finding, 0 in my paths** (`tests/unit/test_ingest_pipeline.py:159` E501, pre-existing and documented); `mypy packages/ingestion/dedup.py tests/unit/test_purge_originals.py` → **Success: no issues found in 2 source files**; repo `mypy` → **Success: no issues found in 158 source files**; `bash scripts/vendor_guard_pd005.sh` → **VENDOR GUARD PASSED**.
- **In-tree callers of the adapter's `delete_object`:** was 0 outside the adapter itself (T-039 review) → now `packages/ingestion/dedup.py`, reached via `storage.originals.get_adapter()`. `grep -c "delete_object" packages/ingestion/dedup.py` → 1.

RISKS: (1) **The tidy home for this code is one file I was told not to write to:** `packages/storage/originals.py` is the documented single place that "turns a document version into an object key", and `delete_document_originals` + the prefix derivation arguably belong there next to `put_original`/`load_original`. The allowlist says `packages/storage/*` is read-only reuse, so I kept the derivation in `dedup.py` and imported `ORIGINALS_PREFIX`/`originals_bucket`/`get_adapter` — one small follow-up for that file's owner to move it in, with the unit tests as the safety net. (2) **`apps/api/app/documents/delete.py` has no second purge surface** (grep: its only call into this path is `purge_document`, line 71), so the 204/207 contract picks the new store up for free — but it is worth a reviewer's eye, since a document whose originals cannot be deleted now returns 207 where it used to return a clean 204. (3) **New dependency on the object store for every delete:** if the store is unreachable, every purge of a document with version rows reports `originals: False` → 207 instead of 204. That is the honest answer (the bytes really might still be there) and fts/qdrant are usually down with it, but it does make an object-store outage visibly change delete responses; an operator guide for re-purging after a 207 would be the useful follow-up. (4) **Legacy rows are handled by listing, not by guessing:** a version row with `storage_key IS NULL` has an object only if something wrote one outside the T-039 path, and I refuse to reconstruct its key (the extension came from a filename Postgres never recorded) — the prefix sweep is what catches it. This is a deliberate choice: guessing keys would delete nothing extra today (0009 backfilled every row that had bytes and 0010 moved the dual-copy stragglers, so a NULL key means "never had bytes") and a wrong guess is a silent no-op. (5) **The sweep is bounded by the adapter's `max_keys` (1000)**; the exact `storage_key` pass is what covers a document with more versions than one listing page, so the two passes are complementary, not redundant. (6) **Bucket existence is assumed, not created:** nothing in the repo calls `create_bucket`, so a stack whose bucket is missing reports `originals: False` on every delete even though there are no bytes — honest and greppable, but noisy; `S3_BUCKET` misconfiguration now shows up as deletes returning 207. (7) `supersede_other_versions` (T-V1-017) still leaves a superseded version's original in the store, by design: it is a different document generation, not a delete, and the next full purge of the document takes the whole prefix. If retention policy says a superseded version's bytes must not outlive its supersession, that is a separate ticket (and would need the same failure-honest treatment). (8) This ticket's own live probe wrote and deleted objects under random `uuid4` workspace/document keys in the compose bucket — nothing else was touched, and `GET` on those keys now returns `NoSuchKey`.

Start→end `git status --short`: **28 → 31 entries**. **My delta: 2 paths** — ` M packages/ingestion/dedup.py`, `?? tests/unit/test_purge_originals.py` (plus this already-dirty `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` row). **Foreign paths touched by me: 0** — including the tree-discipline list from the ticket: `packages/answer/*`, `packages/workflow_engine/adapters/langflow_adapter.py` + `tests/unit/test_workflow_langflow_adapter.py` (unknown agent), `scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py`, `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/fixtures/*` all still exactly as I found them (the last two went *clean* mid-ticket because a foreign commit landed, not because I edited them). Foreign and NOT mine, present at start and untouched: `M packages/answer/{adapters/postgres_lookup.py,hydration.py,interfaces.py}`, `M tests/unit/test_answer_engine.py`, `M handoffs/{COORDINATOR,MUSER-LOOP-STATE,REW1-REVIEW,REW2-REVIEW}-HANDOFF.md`, `?? handoffs/*` reports, `?? tests/unit/test_bulk_chunk_lookup.py`, `?? tests/fixtures/workflows/`. Foreign, appeared mid-ticket and NOT mine: `M apps/api/app/adapters/readiness.py`, `M apps/worker/jobs/ingest_worker.py`, `M docker-compose.yml`, `M handoffs/DEV21-V4-HANDOFF.md`, `?? tests/{integration/test_worker_liveness_live,unit/test_worker_liveness_probe,unit/test_workflow_langflow_fixture}.py`. **No commit, no deploy, no restart, no compose command, no schema change, no subagent by me.**

## 2026-10-13 T-V1-058 — the purge's fts/qdrant purgers were never registered (one FTS row survived a delete)

TASK: T-V1-058 (solo, ~20 min). `test_delete_purges_fts_and_qdrant_and_is_unretrievable` reported `fts 1 / qdrant 0` after a delete: exactly one `lexical_docs` row survived the purge. Classify it — (a) a **sturggler** (the test does not await READY, so an FTS write lands after the purge; fix in the test) or (b) a **real purge gap** (the fts purger deletes with the wrong scope, or a superseded version's row is left behind). **Answer: (b) — a real purge gap, and the fix is product code, not the test.** Loudly, because it is mine: this was a regression I shipped in **T-V1-055** (committed by museR as `33d3c09`, 06:54:28+0200) and it stayed invisible until T-V1-043 put real bge-m3 embeddings back online and the test stopped failing at its Qdrant *precondition* before reaching the post-delete assertions. **No test change was needed or made** — `tests/integration/test_dedup_versions.py` is untouched; my delta is 1 product file + 1 new unit test file.

CHANGE (my delta = exactly 2 paths, 0 foreign paths touched):
- `packages/ingestion/dedup.py` (M — allowed) — a **move**, not new logic: the `originals` purger registration block that T-V1-055 placed immediately after `report = {"postgres": True}` now sits **after** the `if not _STORE_PURGERS:` block that lazily registers the default fts/qdrant purgers (and after the GRAPH_MODE re-evaluation, immediately before the `for name, purger in _STORE_PURGERS.items()` loop). Same closure, same per-call key freshness, same `pop("originals")` on a version-less document, same per-store failure path — only its position changed, plus a load-bearing comment at the new site explaining why the order is part of the contract. Nothing else in the file moved (`git diff` = one block relocated, +38/−36 lines, all of the delta comment).
- `tests/unit/test_purge_default_stores.py` (NEW — allowed, "a new unit test if the fix is product code") — 5 tests, service-free: (1) `test_originals_is_registered_after_the_default_stores` — a record-only spy on `register_store_purger` over a virgin registry asserts the order is exactly `['fts', 'qdrant', 'originals']`; (2) `test_a_version_less_purge_registers_the_defaults_and_no_originals`; (3) `test_a_version_less_purge_drops_a_stale_originals_purger`; (4) `test_a_first_purge_reaches_all_three_stores_and_scopes_by_document` — the real loop with `PostgresLexicalStore`/`QdrantVectorStore` patched at the modules the purge imports them from, asserting `report == {"postgres": True, "fts": True, "qdrant": True, "originals": True}` **and** that both deletes carry the *document* scope (`filter.document_ids == [str(document_id)]`, `workspace_ids == [str(workspace_id)]`) — the ticket's "version-scoped where the row is document-scoped" hypothesis, disproven by construction; (5) `test_a_second_purge_in_the_same_process_still_purges_every_store` — the registry persists across purges, and the second purge still reaches fts+anonymous qdrant+originals (that is the state the API process is in, and the state the survivor appeared in).
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row).

WHY: the mechanism, proven below. `purge_document` registers the default fts/qdrant purgers inside `if not _STORE_PURGERS:` — a "first purge in this process" guard. T-V1-055 added `originals` and registered it *before* that guard, so on any process that had not purged yet the registry was non-empty by the time the guard ran: fts and qdrant were **never registered**, the purge loop only found `originals`, and the report was `{'postgres': True, 'originals': True}` — a clean **204** for a document whose lexical row and vector point were still there. That is worse than the leak itself: the per-store report was the thing that was supposed to keep the purge honest, and it reported a store set that had silently lost two members. Moving the registration after the guard restores the invariant that whatever a purge registers for one document can never prevent the default stores from being registered. Not (a), and not a scope bug: the surviving row's `workspace_id` **matched** the filter the purger builds (`build_purge_filter` → `document_ids=[doc]`, `workspace_ids=[ws]`), and the row was written 0.66 s **before** the tombstone, so a re-purge-after-straggler test would have "fixed" the symptom and left the real hole in place — the test needed no change and got none.

REUSED: nothing new — no dependency, no SDK, no schema change, no new error code or report shape; the fix is a re-ordering inside `ingestion.dedup`'s existing registration seam (`register_store_purger` / `_STORE_PURGERS` / `build_purge_filter` / `PostgresLexicalStore.delete(query_filter=…)` / `QdrantVectorStore.delete(collection, query_filter)`), all as-is. The new unit test reuses the T-V1-055 file's in-memory `StorageAdapter` + session-double pattern and `tests/fixtures/object_store_env.py` conventions; fts/qdrant are replaced by recording doubles at the modules the purge imports them from, so no unit test reaches Postgres, Qdrant or the object store.

BUILT: 5 unit tests (~230 lines) and a 38-line comment. No product logic was written: the entire fix is the position of an existing block. Deliberately NOT built: no re-design of the lazy-registration pattern (see RISKS 2), no backfill/re-purge script for the residue below (flagged instead — see RISKS 1), no change to `purge_document`'s ordering of DB-work (the originals still leave after the tombstone commit), no change to the 204/207 contract or to any test in `tests/integration/`.

VERIFY (exact, from repo root; HEAD moved under me from `99dcfa1` to `33d3c09` (T-V1-055) → `f241cb6` (T-V1-056) → `e108c87` (T-V1-043 bge-m3 deploy) — all foreign/museR commits, none by me):
- **Classification, from the DB.** `/root/ko-venv/bin/python /tmp/opencode/tv1058-probe.py` (outside the repo; replays the test's upload→`process_one`→DELETE and dumps the survivor) → `fts_before_delete(doomed): 1`, `delete_status: 204`, `SURVIVORS after purge: 1` with `{'id': 'aa1c860d-…', 'workspace_id': 'c10cbc08-…', 'document_id': '97f8abc1-…'(the doomed document), 'document_version_id': 'b28fa7cc-…'(the doomed version), 'chunk_id': 'aa1c860d-…'(== row id), 'language': 'en', 'content': 'tombstone probe 6f16f35a…'}`, `workspace_id match: 1` (the delete filter **would** have matched → not a scope bug). Timeline: doomed job `finished_at = 2026-10-13 05:10:59.742984+00:00` vs `documents.deleted_at = 2026-10-13 05:11:00.400637+00:00` → the FTS write landed **0.66 s before** the tombstone → **not a straggler**. `/root/ko-venv/bin/python /tmp/opencode/tv1058-probe2.py` (fresh process, direct `purge_document`, one version row + one FTS row) → `registry BEFORE: ()`, **`REPORT: {'postgres': True, 'originals': True}`**, `registry AFTER: ('originals',)`, `fts rows after: 1`; live Qdrant after the same purge → `doomed document points still in qdrant: 1`. So both the lexical and the vector half leaked while the report said 204.
- **The fix, A/B proven.** `git checkout HEAD -- packages/ingestion/dedup.py` (pre-fix) + `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_purge_default_stores.py -q` → **3 failed / 2 passed**, incl. `Right contains 2 more items: {'fts': True, 'qdrant': True}`. Restored the fixed file (`cp`, re-verified by `grep -c T-V1-058` = 2) → **5 passed**.
- **The ticket's command** — `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/integration/test_dedup_versions.py -q`: **before 5 failed / 12 passed → after 4 failed / 13 passed**, and the same 4 on a second consecutive run (13 passed both times). `test_delete_purges_fts_and_qdrant_and_is_unretrievable` is now green. The 4 survivors fail with **byte-identical assertions** before and after (`grep -E '^E ' ` diff): `'INTERNAL_ERROR' == 'SUPERSESSION_CLEANUP_RETRY'` (×1), `'queued' == 'ready'` (×2), `assert False is True` (×1); the baseline's `assert 1 == 0` — the FTS survivor this ticket is about — is gone. None of the 4 are in the delete path.
- **Live end-to-end after the fix** (probe 1, real stack) → `SURVIVORS after purge: 0`, `fts_count(doomed) after purge: 0`, `keyword hits for the doomed marker: []`, `fts_count(live) after purge: 1` (the control document untouched), originals still deleted (`store.deleted == [key]` in the unit equivalent).

(3) **A silent report is worse than a loud one.** While broken, the API answered 204 with `stores: {"postgres": true, "originals": true}` — the report can only be as honest as what got registered, so a *partial* registry must be treated as a bug class, not just a store failure; worth a follow-up that asserts the registry contains fts+anonymous qdrant before the loop runs (cheap, and it turns this whole class into a red test). ## 2026-10-13 T-V1-063 — CLOSED WITHOUT CHANGE (museR re-scope; root cause was dev2's T-V1-059)

TASK: T-V1-063 (solo, ~25 min) — diagnose two job-lifecycle integration failures (`test_identical_reupload_skips_parse_and_reuses_version`, `test_supersession_cleanup_failure_is_retried_visibly`) and decide expectation-vs-fence-vs-harness before touching anything. **Verdict: (a) product bug — and not one this ticket needed to fix.** By museR's re-scope, T-V1-063 is closed with **no change from me**: the cause was the T-V1-050 `MissingGreenlet` publish regression, fixed by dev2's T-V1-059 (commit `2e4170a`). My two target tests pass **unchanged**, as does the whole file.

CHANGE (my delta = exactly 1 path, the handoff row; 0 product paths, 0 test paths):
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row). Nothing else. I did not edit either test, did not adjust any expectation, and made **no** product change: what I had written mid-diagnosis in `apps/worker/jobs/ingest_pipeline.py` (a defensive `await session.refresh(job)` after the CAS) was **removed** once dev2's fix landed in the shared tree — with `synchronize_session="fetch"` inside the UPDATE, no attribute is left expired and the extra SELECT per publish was pure duplication of a better fix. `git diff` on that file is now exactly dev2's change, unchanged by me.

WHY: the diagnosis (the part that mattered), for the record. Both failures were the *same* bug wearing two costumes, and the tests were honest. Evidence gathered from the live DB and probes: every job that reached the publish step ended `status=queued/queued, error_code=INTERNAL_ERROR, error_message=MissingGreenlet: greenlet_spawn has not been called…` (56 occurrences in a single suite run); the doomed job in test 1 had written its chunks, FTS rows, embeddings and Qdrant points (`store_write_verified … "store": "qdrant"`) and then died *at the publish*, so the re-upload saw a job that was not READY and re-parsed instead of short-circuiting (`'queued' == 'ready'`); the supersession test's v2 job likewise surfaced INTERNAL_ERROR where it expected `SUPERSESSION_CLEANUP_RETRY`. Timeline killed the "sturggler" hypothesis: job `finished_at 05:10:59.74` vs tombstone `05:11:00.40`, and the surviving row's columns scoped exactly to the document/version with a matching `workspace_id`. The minimal repro, isolated to one construct: `session.expire(job)` + a **sync** read of the expired attribute (T-V1-050's CAS expires the row; the pipeline then reads `str(job.id)` synchronously in `_log_stage`'s log extra) → MissingGreenlet. It was invisible until T-V1-043 put real bge-m3 back online, because jobs used to die at the embedding stage *before* ever reaching the publish. **Lesson for the row (per museR): both failures went green with zero test edits — a wrong expectation usually does not do that. Prove product-vs-expectation first, from the DB and the job rows, before editing any assertion.**

REUSED: nothing added — no dependency, no SDK, no schema change, no new code. The evidence came from the existing seams: `ingestion_jobs`' own `status`/`error_code`/`error_message` columns, the pipeline's `ingest_stage`/`store_write_verified` logs, `ingestion.dedup.purge_document` for the delete-in-flight injection the tests already use, and `tests/fixtures/object_store_env.py` for the probe env. Diagnostics ran outside the repo (`/tmp/opencode/tv1063-probe{,2,3,4}.py`).

BUILT: nothing that ships. Diagnostic probes only (dumped the survivor row + timeline; ran `process_job`/`process_one` directly to get real tracebacks; wrapped the pool checkout and the session factory to see where the sync IO came from), and one A/B that is now moot: reverting the pipeline to HEAD reproduced all four failures, restoring the fix turned them green — which is how I know the single missing greenlet-context refresh was the whole story.

## 2026-10-13 T-V1-060 — BLOCKED by the live stack (infinity restart loop): 2 real defects fixed, NO switch performed, NO P0

TASK: T-V1-060 (solo, ~40 min box). Post-T-043 recovery: build a fresh single-identity `knowledge` from a full rebuild and switch the live name over to it via T-045's verified switch, because the live collection mixes legacy padded-384 evidence with real 1024-d evidence. **Outcome: BLOCKED before the build, and the block is the stack, not the code.** Steps 1 (dry-run) ran as far as the provider allowed; steps 2–5 (build/verify/switch/smoke) did **not** run. Nothing was switched, renamed or deleted: `knowledge` still resolves and serves, so there is **no P0**. Two genuine defects in my allowed files were found and fixed on the way, both of which made the documented recovery command unrunnable.

CHANGE (my delta = 2 paths, 0 foreign paths touched — plus this row):
- `scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py` (M — allowed) — the CLI `--batch-size` default was **100**, which is one synchronous `/embeddings` POST per batch; the documented recovery command (`python scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py --collection knowledge --replacement knowledge_v2`, no flags) therefore died on its very first batch with `httpx.ReadTimeout` before writing anything. Default is now **32**, with the measurements in the comment (8 texts ≈ 9 s, 16 ≈ 8 s, 32 varies 9–30 s, 100 > 30 s) so the choice is auditable rather than a magic number. Corpus cost is 75 requests instead of 24.
- `packages/retrieval/adapters/infinity_embed.py` (M — **adapter, allowed "if a real defect blocks the run", flagged loudly**) — `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` was **30.0**, which is not a plausible ceiling for bge-m3 at 1024-d: a legitimate batch of 32 chunk texts measured 9–30 s, so any slow-but-successful embed became a retryable `EmbeddingProviderError`. This is not a reindex-only problem: the **live worker** embeds a whole chunk batch per document through the same `embed_documents` call (`ingest_pipeline.py:912`), so the same 30 s ceiling can fail an ordinary ingest. Raised to **120.0**, with the measurements and the blast radius in the comment. No test pins the old value (grepped: no test references `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`).
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row). **Nothing else** — no collection was created, no points written, no alias changed, no script/test behaviour change beyond the default.

WHY: the goal is a single-identity `knowledge`, and the evidence for the hazard is exact. Full identity + padding scan of the live collection (1997 points, 1024-d, all unstamped): **1542 points are legacy 384-of-1024 zero-padded** (`dense[384:]` all zeros) and **455 are real bge-m3 1024-d** — so retrieval is comparing junk against real evidence in one collection, and no point carries the identity stamp that would let T-014 keep anything in place. `inspect_collection` therefore reports an unknown-model index and the in-place route must refuse, which is why the recovery is fresh-collection + verified switch. Source of truth for the rebuild: `chunks` = **2371** rows (`lexical_docs` = 2411, the surplus being the stale tombstoned-document rows T-V1-058 documented), so the fresh collection's expected point count is the chunk count, and `promote_replacement(expected_points=…)` will refuse anything short of it.

REUSED: nothing added — no dependency, no SDK, no config. The recovery is the documented procedure the script itself prints (`--replacement` → `promote_replacement`, T-V1-015's read-only-then-switch); the identity stamp is `provider_identity()`; the zero-manufactured guarantee is `validate_vector()` plus the store boundary, unchanged. My adapter change is a constant + comment only.

BUILT: nothing new. Two constants (`default=32`, `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=120.0`) and their comments. Deliberately NOT built: no retry loop in the reindex (a failed batch already leaves the replacement incomplete and `promote_replacement` refuses it — safe by construction); no change to the switch, the identity stamping, or any collection.

VERIFY (exact, from repo root; HEAD `0721869` at the end, foreign commits landed over my ticket window — `e108c87`→…→`0721869`; nothing committed by me):
- **Dry-run (step 1), the command from the ticket** — `PYTHONPATH=packages:apps/api python scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py --dry-run --collection knowledge_v2` →
  `provider: InfinityEmbeddingProvider base_url=http://localhost:7997 model=BAAI/bge-m3 revision=BAAI/bge-m3` ·
  `dims: configured=1024 observed=1024 nonzero=1024 adaptation=ALLOW_DIMENSION_ADAPTATION=false` ·
  `identity recorded on write: {'embedding_provider': 'InfinityEmbeddingProvider', 'embedding_model': 'BAAI/bge-m3', 'embedding_revision': 'BAAI/bge-m3', 'embedding_dimension': 1024}` ·
  `collection 'knowledge_v2': exists=False points=0 dims=None checked=0/0 models=[] revisions=[]` ·
  `plan: action=build — collection does not exist yet` ·
  `counts: live_chunks=2374 batches=75 batch_size=32 existing_collection_points=0` ·
  `[DRY RUN] would index batch 0-32 (32 records, 0 writes)` → then the provider died (see RISKS). **Zero writes confirmed** (`knowledge_v2` still does not exist; no FTS insert; no upsert).
- **Before state, measured** — `knowledge`: 1997 points, dims 1024, 1542 padded-384 / 455 dense-1024 / **0 identity stamps**, `aliases=[]`. `points_count` at the end of my ticket: 2015 (the live index keeps being written by the live worker; no alias, no rename, no delete by me).
- **Lint/type on the two touched files** — `ruff check scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py packages/retrieval/adapters/infinity_embed.py` → **All checks passed**; `mypy` on both → **Success: no issues found in 2 source files**; `pytest tests/unit/test_reindex.py -q` → **14 passed** (the tests drive `reindex(...)` with an explicit `batch_size`, so none of them pinned the CLI default); `pytest tests/unit/test_reindex.py tests/unit/test_originals_store.py tests/unit/test_purge_default_stores.py tests/unit/test_purge_originals.py -q` → **50 passed**.
- **Steps 2–5 did NOT run** — no build, no identity scan of a fresh collection, no switch, no search smoke. That is the whole of what remains.
- **No P0, proved** — `knowledge` still resolves and serves (Qdrant `GET /collections/knowledge` → 2015 points, 1024-d; `aliases=[]`); qdrant up; api Up (healthy), `/health` → `{"status":"ok"}`; worker heartbeat renewing (0.9 s ago).

RISKS: (1) **THE BLOCKER — infinity is in a restart loop and cannot serve.** `docker inspect knowledge-os-infinity-rerank-1` → `Status=running Restarts=22 ExitCode=0 OOMKilled=false`, "Up 43 seconds" and climbing; every cycle loads `BAAI/bge-m3` (the model IS the right one, `INFINITY_MODEL_ID: BAAI/bge-m3;cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L6-v2` from the committed compose file) and then exits with `ERROR infinity_emb cli.py:393: Error: No command given. Please use infinity with the 'v2' command. This is deprecated since 0.0.32. You are on 0.0.77.` — i.e. the container's effective command line is `infinity_emb` with **no `v2` subcommand** (`Entrypoint=[infinity_emb]`, `Cmd=[]`), which that image rejects and exits 0 on, and `restart: unless-stopped` re-runs it forever. It never binds :7997, so `GET /health` → connection reset and `/ready` → `"infinity": {"reachable": false, "error": "ConnectError: All connection attempts failed"}` (every other dependency green). **This is not something I can fix: repairing it needs a compose command/restart, which the ticket forbids** ("no restarts; compose commands only for read-only diagnostics"), and it is not a code defect in my allowlist — it is the stack's command line, and the fix is an operator one (a `command:`/`v2` subcommand for the infinity service, then a recreate). Until it is fixed the whole ingest path is down, not just my reindex. (2) **Host is RAM-starved, which is what likely tipped the container over**: `free -m` → total 7935 / available **277 MB**, swap fully used; four foreign `t021_crash_worker.py` test processes hold ~200 MB each and several `opencode`/`hermes` processes are live. Anyone about to run heavy work should know the box has no headroom. (3) **Timing of the breakage**: my first attempt (default batch 100) still got a 200 for the probe embed and then hit a plain `httpx.ReadTimeout` on the 100-text batch — so infinity was alive at 07:10 and unreachable by 07:16, before I ever ran a batch that could have stressed it. My requests were ≤64 texts; nothing I ran could rewrite a container's command line. I flag it so the coordinator can attribute it correctly rather than to this ticket. (4) **What remains, exactly**: once infinity serves, run `PYTHONPATH=packages:apps/api python scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py --collection knowledge --replacement knowledge_v2` (now with the safe default batch), expect the identity scan to refuse the in-place route on `knowledge` (unknown model, as designed) and build ~2374 identity-stamped points into `knowledge_v2`, then let the script call `promote_replacement(live="knowledge", replacement="knowledge_v2", expected_points=<written>)` — which verifies existence/dims/count before any alias change — and finally smoke one real keyword+vector search through the stack. (5) No commit, no deploy, no restart, no compose command beyond read-only `docker ps`/`inspect`/`logs`, no schema change, no subagent by me.

Start→end `git status --short`: **24 → 27 entries**. **My delta: 2 paths** — ` M packages/retrieval/adapters/infinity_embed.py`, ` M scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py` (plus this handoff row, already dirty at start). **Foreign paths touched by me: 0** — the tree-discipline list stayed exactly as found: `packages/workflow_engine/adapters/langflow_adapter.py`, `tests/unit/test_workflow_langflow_adapter.py`, `tests/unit/test_workflow_langflow_fixture.py`, `tests/fixtures/workflows/`, and every `handoffs/*` file. Foreign, present at start and untouched: `M handoffs/{COORDINATOR,DEV21-V4,MUSER-LOOP-STATE,REW1-REVIEW,REW2-REVIEW}-HANDOFF.md`. ## 2026-10-13 T-V1-060 (continued) — BLOCKED again, this time by the provider WEDGING under load: knowledge untouched, no switch, one real product fix landed

TASK: T-V1-060 resumed from step 2 after museR repaired infinity (commit `2e63bd7` pinned `0.0.77` + `command: ['v2','--host','0.0.0.0','--port','7997']`). **Result: the rebuild is BLOCKED by a second, different provider failure. `knowledge` was never switched, renamed or deleted and still serves (2022 points, `aliases=[]`) — no P0.** What landed: one real product fix in the script (a bounded per-batch embed retry, with tests). What did NOT land: the build, the identity verification of a fresh collection, the alias switch and the search smoke.

CHANGE (my delta = 2 paths, 0 foreign paths touched — plus this row; my earlier two fixes for this ticket are committed by museR as `2e63bd7`):
- `scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py` (M — allowed) — the batch loop embedded each batch through a bare `embed_provider.embed_documents(texts)`, so **one provider hiccup aborted a 40-minute rebuild** after minutes of successful work. New `embed_batch_with_retry(provider, texts)`: `_EMBED_BATCH_ATTEMPTS = 4`, `_EMBED_BATCH_BACKOFF_SECONDS = (15.0, 30.0, 45.0)`, retrying only the provider call (embedding is pure — no store write happens until it returns), and raising `EmbeddingProviderError` once the budget is spent so the run stops loudly. The **dimension probe** now uses the same helper (it is one more `POST /embeddings` and was aborting before a single batch was indexed). Budget sized to ride out one model reload (~35 s here) — the comment records both measured failure modes.
- `tests/unit/test_reindex.py` (M — allowed) — 4 new tests: retry-then-success (asserts one failure, one retry, exactly one 15 s backoff), exhaustion-then-raise (the budget is a constant, not retry-until-success), the budget constants including `sum(backoff) >= 35.0` ("must cover a full model reload before the last attempt"), and an **end-to-end no-switch-on-failed-batch** test (probe succeeds, batches never do → `reindex()` raises and no store ever calls `promote_replacement`). The two pre-existing retry-timing assertions were updated to the new budget, not weakened. 14 → **18 passed**.
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row). Nothing else — no collection was left behind, no alias changed, no adapter edited this round.

WHY: I did not respond to the second failure by raising the timeout again. Both failures are *provider availability*, not corpus shape, and the evidence says so: (1) **latency tail** — 11 consecutive batches of 32 ordinary texts completed in 9-30 s, then batch 352-384 (min/median/max text length **11 / 30 / 1279 chars**; corpus maximum 1933 chars; **zero** chunks over 2000) went unanswered past the 120 s client timeout, while the server answered `200 OK` for the requests immediately after — slow, not hung, and nothing to split; (2) **process restart** — during the retry build infinity went `RestartCount 0 → 1` (`ExitCode=0`, `OOMKilled=false`) and needed ~45 s to load both models again, so a 5 s+10 s budget expired while the server was still starting, and the client saw `ReadError` on the in-flight request. Fragmenting chunks or raising the global ceiling would have hidden both behind a slower run; a bounded retry of the one impure-free call makes the rebuild survive a transient provider outage while still refusing to continue indefinitely.

REUSED: nothing added — no dependency, no SDK, no config change. The retry reuses the provider's own typed error (`EmbeddingProviderError`) so only provider faults are retried, and the existing refuse machinery is unchanged: an exhausted budget raises, the run stops, and `promote_replacement`'s read-only verification refuses the half-built replacement.

BUILT: ~35 lines (one helper, two constants, the probe call site, and their comments) + 4 tests. Deliberately NOT built: no retry in the shared adapter — that would change live ingest semantics and belongs to its own ticket; no per-chunk splitting (nothing is oversized); no unbounded retry.

VERIFY (exact, from repo root; HEAD `2e63bd7` throughout, nothing committed by me):
- `ruff check scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py tests/unit/test_reindex.py` → **All checks passed**; `mypy scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py` → **Success**; `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_reindex.py -q` → **18 passed** (14 pre-existing + 4 new).
- **Build attempt 1 (08:21-08:37)** — `PYTHONPATH=packages:apps/api python scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py --collection knowledge --replacement knowledge_v2`: `counts: live_chunks=2388 batches=75 batch_size=32 existing_collection_points=0`, 11 batches indexed, then `EmbeddingProviderError: ... ReadTimeout` at batch 352-384 → **crashed at 352 points**. `knowledge` untouched (2015 → 2015).
- **Build attempt 2 (08:41-08:51, with retry)** — same command: 9 batches, 2 retries logged, then `EmbeddingProviderError: ... ReadError` → crashed at **288 points**. `knowledge` untouched (2015 → 2022, written by the live worker, not by me).
- **Build attempt 3 (08:54-, with retry + restart-sized budget)** — same command: **0 batches**, 3 retries, all `ReadError`, because by then the server was already wedged.
- **The provider defect (for the operator, not fixable in my allowlist)** — after the restart, infinity logged nothing after `08:50:34` (its own benchmark line: `batch_size=32`, `avg tokens per sentence=2`, `inference 5012.89 ms`, `embeddings/sec 6.37`) while `Status=running`, `Restarting=false`, process alive at ~99 % CPU and 1.55 GB RSS — and it refuses connections *instantly* (`curl /health` empty; a direct 1-text embed fails in 0.0-0.3 s with `ReadError`). So the `v2` server survives model load but **stops accepting connections under sustained embedding load**: the HTTP listener wedges while the process spins. It is not a client-side or text-shape problem, and `docker-compose.yml`/the image config are outside this ticket's allowlist and my no-restart rule.
- **No P0, and the box is left as found** — after deleting the half-built replacements per the script's own recovery step 2: `knowledge = 2022`, `knowledge_v2 exists = False`, `aliases = []`. Nothing of mine ever resolved or un-resolved the live name.

RISKS: (1) **The rebuild cannot complete on this stack until infinity can sustain embedding load** — it has now failed two rebuilds in two different ways (latency tail, process restart) and is currently wedged. The next attempt should start with a health probe (`/health` **and** a 1-text embed) rather than trusting the container's `Status=running`; the script's probe would abort early, but only after burning its retry budget. (2) **The mixed-identity hazard is still live**: `knowledge` still holds ~1550 legacy zero-padded 384-of-1024 points next to ~470 real 1024-d ones, all unstamped, so retrieval keeps comparing junk against real evidence until the switch lands. (3) **`supersede`/new-chunk drift**: the corpus grew 2371 → 2388 chunks during the attempts (the live worker keeps writing), so a future rebuild must read its count at build time — `promote_replacement` compares against what the script actually wrote, which is the right contract. (4) **Retry semantics**: retrying a batch is safe because nothing is written until the embed returns; if a batch ever succeeded server-side but timed out client-side, the extra call is still pure. (5) No commit, no deploy, no restart, no compose command beyond read-only `docker ps`/`inspect`/`logs`, no schema change, no subagent by me; no leaked `t021_crash_worker.py` workers (I checked with a pattern that excludes my own command line).

Start→end `git status --short`: **28 → 27 entries**. **My delta: 2 paths** — ` M scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py`, ` M tests/unit/test_reindex.py` (plus this handoff row, already dirty at start). **Foreign paths touched by me: 0** — `packages/workflow_engine/adapters/langflow_adapter.py`, `tests/unit/test_workflow_langflow_adapter.py`, `tests/unit/test_workflow_langflow_fixture.py`, `tests/fixtures/workflows/` and every other `handoffs/*` file are as I found them. Foreign, present at start and untouched: `M handoffs/{COORDINATOR,DEV21-V4,MUSER-LOOP-STATE,REW1-REVIEW,REW2-REVIEW}-HANDOFF.md`. `git_connector.py` / `test_git_source_acquisition.py` (dirty at the previous ticket end) were committed by museR during this window — not by me.

## 2026-10-13 T-V1-069 — FORENSICS: the 373 non-authentic vectors were written by the integration suites' stubbed embedder (test path, not product)

TASK: T-V1-069 — measurement-only forensics: name the exact code path + condition that persisted the 373 vectors that are not embeddings of their text, and the 17 orphan chunk_ids, before any re-index. **Verdict: candidate (c).** They were written by the **integration test harness's stubbed embedder**, running through the real pipeline and the real Qdrant adapter into the **live `knowledge` collection**. No product code path writes a non-embedding. Nothing was fixed, nothing was written to `knowledge`.

FINDINGS
- **(a) pre-F5 zero-padding path — ELIMINATED (different population).** Measured over the whole collection: the legacy family is **1542** points whose `[384:1024]` tail is entirely zero, of which most are *fully* zero vectors (‖v‖ = 0.000000, median 0) and only 274 are unit-norm. The 373 are the **opposite**: no zero tail, all 1024 components equal, ‖v‖ = 1.000000. Two disjoint populations — the 373 cannot be the padding path, exactly as the census suspected.
- **(b) failed-embed fallback that writes a placeholder anyway — ELIMINATED (code).** `apps/worker/jobs/ingest_pipeline.py` has exactly **one** vector write (`qdrant.upsert`, line 949) and it is reachable only after `embed_documents` returns: the `except` at lines 912-920 logs `embedding_failed` and raises `IngestPipelineError("EMBED_UNAVAILABLE", retryable=True)` **before** any record is built. No placeholder/zero-fill write path exists.
- **(c) test/seed path — THE WRITER (DB + code).** The 373 are byte-identical to each other: `dense = [0.031249996] × 1024` — one distinct value, `0.03125 = 1/√1024` in every component, ‖v‖ = **1.000000**, and pairwise cosine among them = **1.000000**. That is a *flat unit vector* (a constant vector normalized to unit length), not an embedding: `cos(stored, fresh bge-m3 of the same text)` = **−0.0238 / −0.0221 / −0.0232** on three samples, reproducing dev2's empty band, while today's server returns 1024 distinct unit values for the same texts. Attribution by document (Postgres `chunks → document_versions → documents`): every chunk of the eleven integration-test documents that install a stubbed embedder is in the 373 — `stale.txt` 39/39, `after-claim.txt` 22/22, `parse.txt` 19/19, `fence-del.txt` 19/19, `embed.txt` 18/18, `index.txt` 18/18, `publication.txt` 17/17, `fenced-replace.txt` 15/15, `fenced-delete.txt` 10/10, `fence.txt` 44/46, `fence-live.txt` 20/22 (**356/367, and zero authentic vectors among them**); the other 17 are further integration documents (`doomed.txt`, `doc.txt`, `live.txt`, `x.txt`, `same.txt`, `direct.bin`, `stable-ids.txt`, `note.txt`, `del.txt`, `detail.txt`, `ready.txt`, `poll.txt`). The condition: those suites monkeypatch `infinity_embed.InfinityEmbeddingProvider` with a class returning a **constant** vector and then let the job publish through the real pipeline and the real store, which writes to the hardcoded live collection `coll = "knowledge"` (`ingest_pipeline.py:906`). Stub sites: `tests/integration/test_dedup_versions.py:330 _install_stub_embedder` (used by 4 tests), the repeat-jobs stub at `:431`, the fence stub at `:1328` (+ subclass `:1347`), and `tests/fixtures/scripts/t021_crash_worker.py:118 _install_embedder`.
  - **Named open detail (not guessed):** the *committed* stubs return `[0.001] * 1024` (‖v‖ = 0.032) and **no product code normalizes** — the pipeline passes `vec` verbatim (line 934), `QdrantVectorStore.upsert` writes `r.vector` unchanged (line 510), `validate_record_vector` only rejects, and the infinity adapter only validates and optionally pads; `git log --all -S` finds no normalizer in the history either. **No point in the collection has ‖v‖ ≈ 0.032** — every real-labelled point is unit-norm — and today's server is healthy under heavy padding, identical inputs and empty strings. So the 0.001 → 1/32 step happened either in an **uncommitted working-tree form of the stub** at write time (invisible to `git log -S`), or **inside the served model/inference stack** during the Oct 12–13 instability window (repeated restarts, the bge-m3 swap, the `v2` pin, the thread cap). Both are test-infra causes; neither is reachable from committed product code today. Settling it needs one authorized scratch-collection repro (below) — it does not change the verdict that the harness is the writer.
- **(d) the 17 orphans — not reproducible as stated, and the real number is larger.** Re-running dev2's exact orphan computation over his 475 real-labelled ids now gives **0** (the collection churned: 2015 → 2039 points today), so those 17 were a transient snapshot. Over **all 2039 points**: **112** points have a `chunk_id` with no `chunks` row, all in integration-test documents — 95 padded-legacy, 10 uniform (stub), 7 real-dense. All 75 of their documents still exist in Postgres (only 7 tombstoned: `doomed.txt`, deleted 06:15–06:44 UTC today), all 112 version rows still exist, and **66 belong to superseded versions**. The dominant mechanism is therefore the **supersession leak**: `dedup.supersede_other_versions` deletes the older version's `chunks` + FTS rows in-transaction and *then* calls the best-effort `_cleanup_superseded_vectors` (dedup.py:214); if that call fails and its queued retry never lands, the points outlive their chunk rows. The 7 tombstoned ones match the T-V1-055 purge-gap window already fixed in T-V1-058 (fts/qdrant purgers never registered → the delete left points behind). The inverse gap also exists and was measured: **485 chunks have no Qdrant point** (`doc.txt` 154, `same.txt` 80, `direct.bin` 62, `live.txt` 51, `doomed.txt` 51, `stable-ids.txt` 50, …) — jobs that never reached the vector write plus purged-then-recreated versions.

WHY it matters for the rebuild: a re-index **would reproduce the 373**, because the same suites re-upload their own documents and publish through the same live collection. The rebuild fixes the *mixed identity*; the write path that manufactures non-embeddings has to land first or the next census finds them again.

REUSED (nothing rebuilt): dev2's artifacts as instructed — `/tmp/opencode/tv1061b_authenticity.json` (the 475 scored `cos` values; the 373 are the `<= 0.95` set, and I re-derived its distribution rather than re-running the census) and `/tmp/opencode/tv1061b_authentic.py` / `tv1061b_census.py` (method). My own read-only probes: `/tmp/opencode/tv1069-profile.py` → `tv1069_nonauthentic_profile.json` (vector identity, payload fingerprints, per-document attribution). Postgres reads only (`psql`-equivalent via SQLAlchemy); Qdrant reads only (`scroll` with `with_vector`). No ingests, deletes, upserts, or collection changes on `knowledge`.

BUILT: **nothing.** This ticket produced measurement, a verdict per candidate, and three filed fix tickets. No product code, no test code, no schema change, no commit.

FILED (not implemented here — per the ticket):
1. **T-V1-070 — integration suites must never write to the live collection.** *File:* `tests/integration/test_dedup_versions.py` (`_install_stub_embedder` :330, stub :431, :1328, :1347) and `tests/fixtures/scripts/t021_crash_worker.py` (`_install_embedder` :118), plus `apps/worker/jobs/ingest_pipeline.py:906` (`coll = "knowledge"` hardcoded). *Condition:* a stubbed provider's constant vector is published into the production collection. *Fix shape:* make the collection name injectable (env `VECTOR_COLLECTION`, default `knowledge`) so every suite runs against `knowledge_test_<uuid>`, and add a harness guard that fails an integration run which targets a pre-existing collection. Blocks the rebuild, or the rebuild reproduces the population.
2. **T-V1-071 — supersession vector-cleanup leak (the 66 superseded-version orphans).** *File:* `packages/ingestion/dedup.py:196-217` (`supersede_other_versions` → `_cleanup_superseded_vectors`). *Condition:* chunk/FTS rows are deleted in-transaction while the vector cleanup is best-effort; a failed call only queues a retry (`SUPERSESSION_CLEANUP_RETRY/FAILED`) that may never land. *Fix shape:* make the pending cleanup durable (a sweeper-consumed marker) or add a periodic orphan sweep for points with no chunk row.
3. **T-V1-072 — reconciliation + orphan hygiene.** *Files:* a read-only reconciliation probe (points ⇄ chunk rows ⇄ tombstone) and, behind an explicit operator flag, a prune for points whose chunk row is gone; it would have caught 112 orphan points and 485 point-less chunks before this census.

VERIFY (exact, from repo root; HEAD `43fa5a7`, nothing committed, no writes to `knowledge`):
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/python /tmp/opencode/tv1069-profile.py` → 373/373 matched; `distinct first-16-dim signatures: 1`; `pairwise cos among 40 of them: min=1.000000 median=1.000000 max=1.000000`; `norm min/median/max = 1.000000`; `non-zero dims in [384:1024]: 640` (no zero tail); payload keys `{workspace_id, content, metadata, document_id, document_version_id, chunk_id, language}` on all 373; `point ids distinct: 373`, `chunk_id == id` on all 373; 373 distinct documents and 373 distinct versions.
- Raw REST `points/scroll` on one of them → `dense[0:6] = [0.031249996] × 6`, `distinct values in dense: 1`, sparse `[1.0, 1.0]` — the byte-level confirmation that it is a flat unit vector.
- Postgres attribution query (chunks → document_versions → documents) → the per-document table quoted in (c): **356/367** chunks of the eleven stub-using documents are in the 373, **0 authentic**.
- Population separation: `PADDED-384 legacy: n=1542 norm median=0.000000 (unit-norm only 274)` vs `REAL 1024-d: n=495 norm min/median/max = 1.000000` → (a) eliminated, and unit-norm is the *real* server's own convention (so unit-norm alone is not the tell — the identical-value test is).
- Fresh re-embed of three of the 373's own texts: `distinct=1024` each, `cos(stored, fresh) = −0.023782 / −0.022084 / −0.023164`.
- Orphan census over all points → **112** orphan points (95 padded / 10 uniform / 7 dense), **66** on superseded versions, 7 documents tombstoned; inverse **485** chunks with no point; dev2's 17 not reproducible (0 orphans over his 475 ids now).
- Code evidence: one vector write in the pipeline (`:949`) behind the `EMBED_UNAVAILABLE` raise (`:912-920`); `upsert` writes `r.vector` verbatim (`qdrant_store.py:510`); `validate_record_vector` rejects only; stub sites at the four test locations + `t021_crash_worker.py:118`; supersession order at `dedup.py:196-217`.

RISKS: (1) **The rebuild alone is not enough** — without T-V1-070 the suites keep writing non-embeddings into `knowledge`, so the next census repeats this ticket. (2) **One documented ambiguity remains** in (c): the exact 0.001 → 1/32 step is not reproducible from committed code (no normalizer exists in product or history, and no 0.032-norm vector is in the collection). It is test-infra either way; settling it needs one *authorized* scratch-collection repro — upsert one `[0.001]*1024` vector into a throwaway collection and see what Qdrant stores — which I did not run because this ticket is read-only. (3) The 7 tombstoned-document orphans sit in the T-V1-055 purge window and should disappear on the next full purge/rebuild; the 66 superseded-version orphans will persist until T-V1-071. (4) 485 point-less chunks mean retrieval is also *missing* rows, so the corpus is wrong in both directions and the identity census alone does not measure completeness. (5) No commit, no deploy, no restart, no compose command beyond read-only `docker ps`/`inspect`, no write of any kind to `knowledge`, no subagent; probes live in `/tmp/opencode/` outside the repo.

Start→end `git status --short`: **24 → 24, byte-identical snapshots** (`diff` clean). **My delta: 1 path** — `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row, the file was already dirty at start). **Foreign paths touched by me: 0** — the tree-discipline list and every `handoffs/*` file are exactly as found.

## 2026-10-13 T-V1-071 — the vector collection is injectable; integration suites no longer write live `knowledge`

TASK: T-V1-071 (solo, ~35 min) — T-V1-069's rebuild blocker, filed by me as T-V1-070 and renumbered by the coordinator (same content): make the vector collection name injectable with `knowledge` as the production default, move every integration suite that runs the real pipeline onto a scratch collection, and **STEP 0 first**: settle the T-069 `0.001 → 1/32` ambiguity with an authorized scratch-collection repro. **Metric (integration suites writing live `knowledge`): 0, proven by count stability.**

STEP 0 RESULT — **Qdrant itself normalizes the vector, so the stub explanation is confirmed numerically.** Upserted `[0.001] * 1024` through the real `QdrantVectorStore` into a throwaway collection `t1071_probe_*` (authorized: scratch only; created, read back, deleted — never `knowledge`) and read it back: `len=1024 distinct=1 first=0.031249996 norm=0.999999872`, `stored == stub ([0.001]*1024)? False`, `stored == unit-normalized (1/32 per dim)? True`. So the committed stub's constant vector becomes the flat unit vector **at the Qdrant boundary**, not in our code — which is exactly why T-069 found no normalizer in the pipeline, the store, the adapter or the history. T-069's open ambiguity is closed, and the 373's origin (the suites' stubbed embedder, candidate (c)) is confirmed end to end.

CHANGE (my delta = 11 paths: 4 product, 6 test-harness, 1 handoff row; 0 foreign paths touched):
- `packages/core/config.py` (M) — `vector_collection: str = "knowledge"` on `Settings` (env `VECTOR_COLLECTION`) plus a `vector_collection()` accessor that resolves **per call** (never frozen as a module constant). Production behaviour is byte-identical when the variable is unset.
- `apps/worker/jobs/ingest_pipeline.py` (M) — the two hardcoded `"knowledge"` sites now resolve through it: the vector **write** (`:905 coll = vector_collection()`) and the fenced-generation vector cleanup (`:200`).
- `packages/ingestion/dedup.py` (M) — the supersession vector cleanup (`:241`) and the delete-path **qdrant purger** (`:393`) likewise. These had to move together: had only the writer moved, a delete would have purged the live index while writing to the scratch one.
- `packages/answer/factory.py` (M) — the **read** path. `create_orchestrator`/`build_answer_engine` now take `collection: str | None = None` and resolve inside the body (a default argument would freeze the value at import time and pin production forever); `DEFAULT_COLLECTION` stays as the documented default.
- `tests/fixtures/vector_collection_env.py` (NEW) — the test-side seam: `use_scratch_collection(tag)` (exports `knowledge_test_<tag>_<rand8>` **before any app import** — pydantic settings cache on first read), `assert_not_live()` guard, and read-only `live_points_count()` / `collection_points_count()` for the metric proof.
- `tests/integration/{test_dedup_versions,test_crash_recovery,test_ingestion_jobs,test_knowledge_reads,test_security_placeholders}.py` (M ×5) — each exports a scratch name + `INGEST_QUEUE_PREFIX` (so the **compose worker** can no longer claim the suite's jobs and write live from its own env) + `assert_not_live(...)`; the 17 hardcoded `"knowledge"` literals in the dedup/crash suites' own Qdrant reads now use the same name.
- `tests/conftest.py` (M) — `pytest_sessionfinish` drops the run's `knowledge_test_*` collection so the shared Qdrant does not accumulate one index per run. It only ever deletes a name this harness generated (a run pointed at `knowledge` is untouched) and never fails a green session over cleanup.
- `tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py` (M) — `_ensure_scratch_collection()` at import: that suite SIGKILLs jobs **before** the vector stage, so with a fresh name nothing ever created the collection and its reads 404'd (invisible before, because `knowledge` always pre-existed). Creating it up-front is what took that suite from 8 failed to 7 passed.
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row).

WHY: the writer, the purgers and the reader must agree on one collection or the product tears itself apart (writes scratch, purges live). Routing all four through a single setting is what makes the seam safe to hand to suites; the queue-prefix isolation closes the second door, because a suite's *enqueued* jobs are otherwise processed by the long-lived compose worker whose env this ticket cannot change. With that, an integration run cannot write the production index by any route that exists today.

REUSED: nothing new. `Settings`/pydantic env handling and `get_settings()` (already `lru_cache`d), `QdrantVectorStore.ensure_collection`/`upsert`/`delete`/`delete_versions`, the `INGEST_QUEUE_PREFIX` isolation convention already used by `test_crash_recovery`/`test_originals_object_store`/`test_worker_liveness_live`, and the `tests/fixtures/` sys.path convention. No new dependency, no schema change, no API/contract change, no REUSE_MAP/NOTICES delta.

BUILT: one setting + accessor, four call-site rewrites (2 of which were latent hazards: the purger and the reader), one test helper module, five suite wirings, one session finalizer, one suite-side scratch bootstrap. No new behaviour when `VECTOR_COLLECTION` is unset.

True`, and `scratch collection t1071_probe_* deleted; exists=False`. `knowledge` untouched throughout.

Unchanged again after the three other suites and after crash-recovery: **2041**. The scratch index the suite used (`knowledge_test_dedup_*`) appeared during the run and was dropped at session end. Earlier in this ticket the count moved 2039 → 2041 during the first run — that was the compose worker claiming the suite's enqueued jobs; the `INGEST_QUEUE_PREFIX` isolation is what stopped it.

(4) The session finalizer deletes by HTTP; if Qdrant is unreachable it prints and continues — cleanup never turns a green run red. ## 2026-10-13 T-V1-072 — superseded-version vectors now die durably (bounded in-process retry + reconcile sweep)

TASK: T-V1-072 (solo, ~30 min box, no subagents). My T-069 forensics follow-up: `supersede_other_versions` deletes a superseded version's chunks + FTS rows **in the transaction**, then calls a best-effort `_cleanup_superseded_vectors` whose only recourse was a queued job retry that could never arrive — measured residue 66 points on already-superseded versions. Fix rides the T-071 collection seam, hunks confined to `packages/ingestion/dedup.py`.

CHANGE (my delta = 4 paths, 0 foreign paths touched):
- `packages/ingestion/dedup.py` (M — allowed, cleanup path only) — two mechanisms, no new dependency:
  1. **`_delete_superseded_points(*, document_id, version_ids)`** — the Qdrant `delete_versions` call now runs under a **bounded** in-process retry (`_VECTOR_CLEANUP_ATTEMPTS = 3`, `_VECTOR_CLEANUP_BACKOFF_SECONDS = (1.0, 2.0)`), raising the last failure once the budget is spent. A one-off store failure no longer outlives the job that caused it; a store that stays down still falls through to T-V1-017's visible marker + queued retry (unchanged contract, `_cleanup_superseded_vectors` still returns `True`/`False`).
  2. **`reconcile_superseded_vectors(session, *, document_id=None)`** — public sweep that deletes points for every version row with `superseded_at IS NOT NULL`. Postgres is the authority for "superseded", so anything the store still holds for such a version is an orphan by definition, whatever became of the original cleanup. Wired into `supersede_other_versions` after the targeted cleanup (per-document, cheap, idempotent, failures logged not raised) and callable on its own by a scheduled reconcile or an operator. Resolves the collection through T-071's `vector_collection()`, so it is scratch-safe.
- `tests/unit/test_supersession_vector_cleanup.py` (?? NEW — 6 service-free unit tests, fake store with injectable failures + a session double that answers the two version queries).
- `tests/integration/test_dedup_versions.py` (M — the **directly covering** test; its expectation is exactly what this ticket changes) — `test_supersession_cleanup_failure_is_retried_visibly` now fails the store **persistently** (it previously failed once, which the in-process retry now absorbs), so its T-V1-017 checklist — marker, retry queued, stale points still VISIBLE pre-retry, re-run clears marker and points — is preserved as written. Added `test_supersession_cleanup_single_blip_is_retried_in_process` for the transient case.
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row).

WHY: the failure mode is silent stale evidence. A superseded generation's points stay retrievable, so an answer can cite text the operator already replaced, and the only signal is an `error_code` on a job nobody reads. Postgres already knows the truth (`superseded_at`); the vector store just isn't forced to agree. That mismatch is now repairable on its own, without a rebuild.

REUSED: `vector_collection()` (T-071's seam), `QdrantVectorStore.delete_versions` (T-017, already raises `VectorStoreError` when it cannot count what it removed, so "deleted" is never a guess), T-017's marker + `schedule_retry` failure surface, `latest_job_for_version`, the SQLAlchemy `DocumentVersion` model, and the fake-session/fake-store pattern from my T-060 unit tests. No new dependency, config, collection, schema or service.

BUILT: the bounded retry helper, the reconcile sweep, its supersede-time wiring, and the fault-injection tests. Deliberately NOT built: reordering the vector delete *before* the chunk/FTS delete. That would be the literal "same transaction" reading, but Qdrant is not transactional with Postgres, so it only trades orphan points for missing vectors (recall loss on a generation the pipeline would then leave un-superseded), and it would change documented T-V1-017 behaviour — the FTS half commits regardless — inside a ticket whose hunks must stay in the cleanup path.

VERIFY (exact, from repo root; HEAD `d007d8e` at the end — T-071 already committed by museR; nothing committed by me):
- **The metric, live, against a SCRATCH collection** (`/tmp/opencode/tv1072-live.py`, `VECTOR_COLLECTION=t1072_scratch_*`, deleted afterwards): v1 published with 2 points, v2 with 1; `delete_versions` monkeypatched to raise on its **first** call only; `supersede_other_versions` → **3 delete attempts, v1 points = 0 (0 orphans), v2 points = 1 (live generation survives)**; the follow-up `reconcile_superseded_vectors` then removed 0 — i.e. the bounded retry is what did the work and the reconcile was genuinely a backstop, not the actor. The "cleanup fails entirely, later reconcile repairs the orphan" half is proven separately in the unit test.
- **Fault-injection unit tests** — `pytest tests/unit/test_supersession_vector_cleanup.py -q` → **6 passed**: single failure → retried, 0 orphans, **no** failure marker; persistent failure → budget spent, marker `SUPERSESSION_CLEANUP_RETRY` + retry queued, points still present (visible, not reported clean); reconcile removes a lost cleanup's orphan; reconcile with no superseded rows makes **no store call**; reconcile scoping and the budget constants.
- **Supersession/dedup suites** — `pytest tests/integration/test_dedup_versions.py tests/unit/test_supersession_vector_cleanup.py -q` → **24 passed** (18 dedup + 6 new; the 3-assertion re-run count in the existing test is now `>= 1` because the retry budget legitimately issues more calls); `pytest tests/unit tests/contracts -q` → **1350 passed**; `pytest tests/unit/test_purge_default_stores.py tests/unit/test_purge_originals.py tests/unit/test_workspace_policy.py tests/unit/test_originals_store.py -q` → **68 passed**.
- **Lint/type** — `ruff check packages/ingestion/dedup.py tests/unit/test_supersession_vector_cleanup.py tests/integration/test_dedup_versions.py` → **All checks passed**; `mypy packages/ingestion/dedup.py tests/unit/test_supersession_vector_cleanup.py` → **Success: no issues found in 2 source files**.
- **Live `knowledge` untouched, as required** — 2041 points before and after every run. Orphans re-measured now: **114 total** (T-069: 112), of which **0 on superseded versions** (T-069: 66) — already cleared by dev2's T-073 prune, and any residue would now be swept by this ticket's reconcile. The 114 are chunkless points on *non*-superseded generations, outside this ticket's scope and outside my allowlist.

RISKS: (1) The reconcile sweep costs one extra bounded-delete call per supersede; it is a single Qdrant call on already-superseded ids and issues no store call at all when nothing is superseded. (2) `reconcile_superseded_vectors` is public but nothing schedules it yet — a lost cleanup is repaired on the next supersede of that document or on an explicit call, not on a timer. dev2's T-073 reconciliation is the natural caller; deliberately not wired from here to stay inside the allowlist and avoid the collision. (3) The persistent-failure path still leaves points until a retry or reconcile; the change makes that state *visible and repairable*, not impossible — Qdrant and Postgres cannot be atomic. (4) I changed one existing integration test's stub (fail-once → fail-persistently) and one of its call-count assertions; the T-V1-017 behaviour it exists to protect (marker + queued retry + no false "clean") is asserted unchanged, and the new test covers the case it used to. (5) Collision check passed: `packages/ingestion/dedup.py` was **clean** at START and stayed mine, consistent with T-073 being read-only; I did not touch its prune file or any other product path. (6) No commit, no deploy, no restart, no compose command, no writes to live `knowledge` (every live-path run used the T-071 scratch seam, deleted afterwards), no subagent by me.

Start→end `git status --short`: **24 → 27 entries**. **My delta: 3 paths + this row** — ` M packages/ingestion/dedup.py`, ` M tests/integration/test_dedup_versions.py`, `?? tests/unit/test_supersession_vector_cleanup.py`. **Foreign paths touched by me: 0** — `packages/workflow_engine/adapters/langflow_adapter.py`, `tests/unit/test_workflow_langflow_adapter.py`, `tests/unit/test_workflow_langflow_fixture.py`, `tests/fixtures/workflows/` and every other `handoffs/*` file are exactly as found (`M handoffs/{COORDINATOR,DEV21-V4,MUSER-LOOP-STATE,REW1-REVIEW,REW2-REVIEW}-HANDOFF.md` plus 13 untracked handoff files, all foreign).

## 2026-10-13 T-V1-072 REWORK — fixture debris removed; probe fixture routed through the real upload path

TASK: T-V1-072 REWORK (solo, one item, museR). Product fix accepted; merge blocked because **4 fixture rows my T-072 live probe left in `document_versions`** (mime NULL, 11:38Z, `storage_key` NULL) tripped the originals suite's global invariant `count(*) == count(storage_key)` (4533 vs 4529) and would fail T-036's run phase. Fix condition: route the fixture through the real upload path (preferred) or delete its rows in a teardown (acceptable) — fixture debris must not outlive the run. **No product changes; fixture/teardown only.**

CHANGE (my delta this rework: **0 repo files** + this row — the fixture lives outside the repo at `/tmp/opencode/tv1072-live.py`; the dirty set is byte-identical to START):
- **Debris removed from the live DB** (all rows were provably mine: `source_filename='t1072.txt'`, workspace name `t1072`): 2 × `document_versions`, 4 × `chunks`, 2 × `documents`, **4 × `workspaces`** — the first sweep only caught the 2 workspaces from the runs that got past my FK bugs; 2 more from earlier failed runs survived a first pass and were found and removed on the second. No `lexical_docs`/`ingestion_jobs` rows were ever created, and no original objects existed for those rows (their `storage_key` was NULL).
- **`/tmp/opencode/tv1072-live.py` — the fixture itself**, both of museR's conditions, because one of them is not sufficient:
  1. **Routes through the real upload path** (preferred): each fixture version now writes its bytes with `storage.originals.put_original(...)` and the `DocumentVersion` row is created with the returned `storage_key` + real `checksum` + `size_bytes` + `mime_type` — the same storage step the production upload uses. A row that outlives the run can therefore never break the invariant, and it still round-trips through the object store.
  2. **Teardown in `finally`**: objects, `lexical_docs`, chunks, versions, documents and workspaces are removed from a tracked `FIXTURE` id list, so a normal run (or one that raises mid-way) leaves zero residue — including the scratch Qdrant collection. The script prints its own residue count rather than exiting quietly.
- Repo product/test files untouched this rework. For the record, the committed suites were never the offender: `tests/unit/test_supersession_vector_cleanup.py` is service-free (no DB), and the `DocumentVersion(...)` at `tests/integration/test_dedup_versions.py:319` is the suite's own upload helper, which already calls `put_original` and sets `storage_key`.

WHY: a `finally` teardown cannot cover process death, and I proved it rather than assuming it. With the teardown-only version, a `SIGKILL` 4 s into the probe **leaked 2 more NULL-`storage_key` rows** (invariant 4531 vs 4529 — the same failure a second time). Teardown protects against exceptions; only routing through the real path makes surviving debris *correct*. Both are now in place, so the failure mode is closed rather than merely narrowed.

REUSED: nothing new — `storage.originals.put_original` (the same function the upload path uses, with its SHA-mismatch refusal and read-after-write verify), `put_original`'s teardown counterpart `StorageAdapter.delete_object`, `originals_bucket()`, the T-071 scratch-collection seam, and the existing ORM models. No new dependency, config, schema, or product code.

BUILT: fixture hygiene in an evidence script — a tracked-id `FIXTURE` ledger, a `finally` teardown covering object store + all five PG tables + the scratch collection, a residue self-check printed at the end, and an object-store-aware version row. No product code, no test-expectation changes.

VERIFY (exact, from repo root; HEAD `d007d8e`, nothing committed by me):
- **Both suites green in ONE session** — `pytest tests/integration/test_dedup_versions.py "tests/integration/test_originals_object_store.py::test_backfilled_rows_round_trip_through_the_object_store" -q` → **19 passed** (18 dedup + the backfill test) in 53.5 s. Run twice, both green.
- **NULL-`storage_key` rows after: 0** — invariant `count(*) == count(storage_key)` measured immediately before the suites (4529/4529) and immediately after (4589/4589 — the suites' own uploads, every row keyed); the backfill test's own assertion (`totals[0] == totals[1]`) is what would have failed, and it passes.
- **The rework's key experiment, crash path**: probe with teardown-only, `SIGKILL` at 4 s → **leaked 2 NULL rows, invariant BROKEN (4531 vs 4529)**. Probe with the real-upload-path fix, `SIGKILL` at 4 s → **invariant HOLDS (4531 vs 4531), both surviving rows carry a real `storage_key`, and both `load_original()` round-trip (size + SHA-256 verified)**. Then a clean full probe run: metric still proven (`1 injected delete failure → 3 attempts → v1 points = 0, v2 points = 1`), scratch collection deleted (`exists=False`), **teardown residue (0, 0, 0)**.
- **Qdrant debris also removed**: 5 leftover `t1072_scratch_*` collections from the crash-path runs (0–3 points each) deleted; live `knowledge` **2041 points, unchanged** throughout. Remaining collections (`contract-tests-m004`, `review1_m3001_probe`, `review1_m3001_r2`, `test_ghost_78`, `test_qdrant_hybrid_68{,b,c}`) are other agents' and untouched by me.
- **No repo diff**: `git status --short` **28 → 28, byte-identical** (`diff` clean). Nothing to lint or type-check this rework — no repo file was edited. No commit, no deploy, no restart, no compose command, no write to live `knowledge`, no subagent.

RISKS: (1) The fixed fixture is an **evidence script outside the repo**, so the guarantee is not enforced by CI — it is enforced by construction (any surviving row is correct) and by the residue print, not by a test. If a future probe in this repo needs raw fixture rows, it should use `put_original` the same way; I flagged rather than added a repo-wide fixture guard, as that is product/test change beyond this rework's allowance. (2) The originals store accumulated two objects during the SIGKILL experiment; both were deleted with their rows (verified `delete_object` returned for 2 keys). (3) `test_backfilled_rows_round_trip_through_the_object_store` samples `ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 25` and round-trips through the object store — so a fixture row with a *bogus* key would have broken it non-deterministically, not just the count invariant. That is why the fix stores real bytes rather than a plausible-looking key. (4) The 114 chunkless non-superseded points in live `knowledge` (from the earlier row) are untouched by this rework and remain outside scope. (5) I briefly left a second leak in place while testing the crash path (2 rows, since removed) — recorded so the DB's history is explainable: all `t1072` residue is now zero, rows and objects and collections alike.
