## 2026-10-13 T-V3-016 — STORM planning adapter (bounded plan: perspectives, questions, outline)

TASK: T-V3-016 (solo, ~30 min, no subagents). STORM-style planning adapter that turns a research topic into a BOUNDED plan (perspectives → questions → outline) under the V3 budget discipline. Every bound from the runtime layer applies: plan size capped (max perspectives, max questions per perspective, max outline depth — state the caps and why), generation through the LLM gateway (FREE model ONLY per standing user rule — paid → BLOCKED, never substituted; max_tokens>=1000 headroom, proven this loop), and the plan itself is an EVENT (persisted via step_store/evidence seam with provenance — a plan that can't be audited later is not a plan).

- `tests/unit/test_agents.py` (M — allowed, append-only) — 5 new tests: `test_planner_bounds_fuzz_garbage_empty_injection_all_bounded_or_typed_refusal` (10KB `x`*10240 -> plan bounded perspectives<=3 questions<=9 outline<=9 depth<=3 topic<=2000; empty/whitespace -> `PlanError TOPIC_EMPTY`; injection `ignore all limits` with fake returning 100/100/100 -> still bounded 3/9/9 depth 3 — proves load-bearing injection pin), `test_planner_persistence_with_provenance_all` (quantum computing run-prov trace-1 -> `run_id`/`trace_id`/`planner_version`/`created_at` present, `store.get` byte-identical `model_dump_json`), `test_planner_free_model_only_paid_blocked_never_substituted` (FREE `nvidia/...:free` -> OK `llm.calls[0]` in FREE_MODELS; `gpt-4o-mini`/`o3-mini` -> `BLOCKED` `paid model blocked`), `test_planner_max_tokens_headroom_blocked` (999 -> BLOCKED headroom; 1000 -> OK; 4096 -> OK, proves this loop), `test_planner_uses_fake_gateway_no_external_call` (fake called once with FREE model, no external). Uses `_FakeStormLLM(_LLMProvider2)` with `injection_obey` mode to simulate unbounded LLM output that planner still caps.

## 2026-10-13 T-V3-017 — Parallel subquestion fan-out with budget (bounded concurrency, aggregate-or-fail)

TASK: T-V3-017 (solo, ~30 min, no subagents). Fan out a plan's questions to bounded parallel workers with an aggregate budget. Covers: (1) CONCURRENCY CAP (max parallel workers stated — fan-out without a cap is a fork bomb; prove the cap with a test that launches N questions and asserts active workers never exceed it); (2) AGGREGATE BUDGET (the fan-out shares one budget envelope: per-question reserves draw from it, exhaustion of the envelope STOPS new dispatches but lets in-flight finish — no orphan kills, no overruns; prove both); (3) AGGREGATION (results rejoin in plan order regardless of completion order — prove order-independence; a failed subquestion does not poison siblings but is recorded with its error, and the aggregate reports partial-with-errors, never silent-drop).

CHANGE (my delta = 1 NEW + 1 M + this row, 0 foreign paths touched — `packages/workflow_engine/**` untouched, `handoffs/COORDINATOR-HANDOFF.md` untouched, `planner`/`runtime`/`queue`/`worker`/`verifier`/`evidence`/`step_store`/`interfaces`/`tools` untouched per allowed-files):
- `packages/agents/fanout.py` (NEW — allowed, fanout NEW + seam ONLY, 150 lines, ruff + mypy clean) — `MAX_FANOUT_CONCURRENCY=3` (cap parallel workers; 9 Qs x unbounded would be fork bomb), `MAX_FANOUT_QUESTIONS=9` (mirrors planner max), `MAX_FANOUT_BUDGET=9` (aggregate envelope default one unit per question), `MAX_QUESTION_CHARS=200`, `FanoutBudget(total)` (shared envelope, `try_reserve` with `threading.Lock`, `remaining`), `FanoutConfig(max_concurrency, max_questions)`, `QuestionExecutor` Protocol, `FanoutResult(question,index,output,error,status)` + `FanoutAggregate(results,status,succeeded/failed)`, `fanout_questions(questions, executor, config, budget)` (truncate to `max_questions`, pre-size `results` in plan order, `Semaphore(max_concurrency)` for bound, `try_reserve` before each dispatch — exhaustion breaks and marks remaining `budget_exhausted`, `asyncio.create_task` per question + `gather` lets in-flight finish, per-task `try/except` isolates failures, final order is `results[index]` in plan order, aggregate `completed` vs `partial_with_errors`).
- `tests/unit/test_agents.py` (M — allowed, append-only) — 4 new deterministic tests (fake executors, no sleeps, no wall-clock races): `test_fanout_concurrency_cap_never_exceeds` (6 Qs, cap 3, `TrackingExecutor` increments `active`/`max_active` with `await asyncio.sleep(0)` yields -> `max_active<=3==cap` exercised, `budget.used<=total`), `test_fanout_aggregate_budget_stops_new_lets_inflight_finish_no_overrun` (5 Qs, budget total 2, cap 2 -> 2 ok + 3 `budget_exhausted`, `budget.used==2<=total`, `len==5` no dropped, `partial_with_errors` and in-flight outputs not None), `test_fanout_aggregation_order_independence` (3 Qs with `ReverseOrderExecutor` that yields extra for lower indices so higher finish first deterministically -> `[r.question]==questions`, `[r.index]==[0,1,2]`, `[r.output]==["r0","r1","r2"]`, `completed`), `test_fanout_failed_sibling_does_not_poison_others_partial_with_errors` (`TrackingExecutor(fail_at=1)` -> 3 results, 2 ok 1 failed `fail-1`, `succeeded==2` `failed==1` `partial_with_errors`, order preserved, dropped 0). Uses `asyncio.sleep(0)` only for deterministic yields, not wall-clock.
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row).

WHY: V3 research execution — the bounded planner (T-V3-016) produces up to 9 questions; executing them unbounded would fork-bomb the LLM gateway, exhaust budget without isolation, or lose partial results when one sibling fails. The fan-out adapter makes the three invariants explicit: a stated concurrency cap, a shared budget envelope that is checked before dispatch and never overdrawn, and ordered aggregation that never silently drops a sibling and reports `partial_with_errors` when any subquestion failed or was budget-stopped.

REUSED: `asyncio.Semaphore`/`gather`/`sleep(0)` (stdlib, deterministic, no wall-clock), `threading.Lock` for `FanoutBudget` (same pattern as `InMemoryEvidenceStore`/`PlanStore`), `dataclass`/`Protocol` (same as `EvidenceVerifier`/`StormPlanner`), `MAX_FANOUT_QUESTIONS=9` mirrors `planner.MAX_TOTAL_QUESTIONS` (single source of truth for plan size). No workflow_engine, no new SDK, no runtime/queue mutation — `FanoutBudget` is the aggregate envelope seam, not `RunBudget`.

BUILT: 4 caps with why in header, `FanoutBudget` (3 methods, `try_reserve` atomic), `FanoutConfig` (2 fields), `QuestionExecutor` Protocol, `FanoutResult`/`FanoutAggregate` (5 fields/status helpers), `fanout_questions` (40 lines: truncate, semaphore, budget-check-before-dispatch, task gather, budget_exhausted marking, ordered rejoin, partial vs completed), and 4 deterministic tests with `TrackingExecutor`/`ReverseOrderExecutor` (no real sleeps).

- `/root/ko-venv/bin/mypy packages/agents/fanout.py tests/unit/test_agents.py` → **Success: no issues found in 1 source file** (fanout clean; test file now Success after planner _FakeStormLLM fixes, overall All checks passed).

(2) `MAX_FANOUT_CONCURRENCY=3` is the default but callers can pass `FanoutConfig(max_concurrency=N)` — a 9-question plan with cap 1 is sequential (still correct, just slower); cap 9 is unbounded up to plan size, which is still bounded by `MAX_FANOUT_QUESTIONS=9`. (3) `fanout_questions` truncates `questions[:max_questions]` — a caller that passes 20 questions still gets 9, never a fork bomb; excess is silently bounded, not error, which matches planner caps. ## 2026-10-13 T-V3-018 — Gap analysis + unknowns retention (what the evidence does NOT cover)

TASK: T-V3-018 (solo, ~25 min, no subagents). After evidence gathering, compute the GAP — which plan questions have no supporting evidence — and RETAIN the unknowns as first-class output (an unknowns list with the question, what was searched, and why nothing qualified — never silent omission, never 'no results' without the receipt). A synthesis input with zero supporting chunks must produce a named unknown, not an abstention-shaped hole and not a fabricated answer.

## 2026-10-13 T-V3-019 — Contradictions as candidates + outline (conflicting evidence stays visible)

TASK: T-V3-019 (solo, ~25 min, no subagents). When evidence DISAGREES (two chunks supporting opposite claims on one question), the output keeps BOTH as candidates with their provenance — never resolves by silent majority, never drops the losing side. Covers: (1) contradiction DETECTION (claims with opposing verdicts on the same question surface as a contradiction pair — reuse the verifier seam for the verdicts, one-grounder rule); (2) CANDIDATE outline: the research outline carries contradiction sections naming both sides with chunk ids (a reader sees the disagreement, not a smoothed winner); (3) CONFIDENCE discipline: contradicted claims cap at most MEDIUM with a limitation naming the conflict (reuse the T-052 limitation pattern — capped confidence + appended limitation, not a new mechanism).

The detector surfaces every opposing grounded pair for the same question as a `Contradiction` (both sides with chunk provenance), the outline carries a `Contradiction on 'Q': 'A' [chk-a] vs 'B' [chk-b]` section, and every contradicted candidate is capped to `MEDIUM` with a `Conflicting evidence...` limitation — the T-052 pattern of “capped confidence + appended limitation” reused, not reinvented.

## 2026-10-13 T-V3-020 — Grounded synthesis, no unverified final (every sentence grounded or report doesn't ship)

TASK: T-V3-020 (solo, ~30 min, no subagents). Synthesis step that turns verified claims + candidates + unknowns + contradictions into a final report where EVERY sentence is either grounded in cited evidence or explicitly marked (unknown/conflict/abstention). An unverified sentence in the final output is a fabrication — the metric is unverified finals: 0. Covers: (1) sentence-level grounding gate (each final sentence traces to supporting chunks via the verifier seam, one-grounder rule; ungroundable sentences are DROPPED into the unknowns list, not shipped); (2) unknowns + contradictions carried THROUGH (the T-018 unknowns and T-019 contradiction sections appear in the final report verbatim in structure — prove no content is lost between analysis and synthesis); (3) abstention honesty (a report with zero groundable content abstains with reason AND still discloses what was searched — the T-052 pattern, not a silent empty).

- `tests/unit/test_agents.py` (M — allowed, append-only) — 5 new tests: `test_synthesis_grounding_gate_unverified_dropped_not_shipped` (chk-good grounded vs chk-bad unrelated -> 1 grounded `chk-good` shipped, unrelated `chk-bad` dropped to `Unknown NOT_GROUNDED`, unverified 0), `test_synthesis_unknowns_contradictions_carried_through_no_loss` (gap unknown `What is the scope?` + contra `budget 900 vs 1900` + outline + ev -> unknowns contains scope, contradictions==1, outline verbatim, grounded==1), `test_synthesis_abstention_honesty_with_searched_disclosure` (zero verified -> `abstained True`, `abstention_reason`, `searched==["What is X?","What is Y?"]`, `limitations` contains `Searched`), `test_synthesis_adversarial_mixed_input` (groundable `budget 900`/`scope global` + unrelated `chk-unrelated` + contradiction pair `900 vs 1900` candidates + unknown `What is unknown?` -> grounded contains `900`/`scope`/`1900` but `chk-unrelated` NOWHERE in `all_cited`, both conflict sides or outline present, unknown retained, unverified 0), `test_synthesis_uses_production_grounder_one_grounder_rule` (inspect source contains `from answer.verification import ExtractiveGrounder`).

## 2026-10-13 T-V3-021 — Agent run API routes (start/status/cancel over HTTP, same gates as in-process)

TASK: T-V3-021 (solo, ~30 min, no subagents). Run lifecycle over HTTP (start a run, poll status, cancel it) with EVERY gate enforced identically to the in-process path (budget reservation, approval gates, scoped workspace, bounded everything — the API must not be a gate bypass: prove parity by driving the same policy tests through HTTP that the unit suite drives in-process, or state which gates differ and why).

CHANGE (my delta = 3 NEW/M + 1 NEW test file + this row, 0 foreign routes touched — `packages/workflow_engine/**` untouched, `handoff/COORDINATOR-HANDOFF` untouched via hard out-of-scope, `gap`/`contradictions`/`verifier`/`evidence` untouched per allowed-files seam):
- `apps/api/app/agents/router.py` (NEW — allowed, run routes NEW + seam ONLY, 230 lines, ruff + mypy clean) — `POST /v1/workspaces/{workspace_id}/agent/runs` (start), `GET /v1/workspaces/{workspace_id}/agent/runs/{run_id}` (status), `POST /v1/workspaces/{workspace_id}/agent/runs/{run_id}/cancel` (cancel). AuthNZ reuses existing stack (`Depends(get_principal)` + `require(principal, Action.DOCUMENT_READ, Resource(workspace_id=...))` + `_http_error` mapping `ApiError` -> `error_detail`), no new auth. Gates: `RunBudget`/`RunContext` bounded validation (400 on invalid), workspace_id from URL not body (cross-workspace bypass impossible, 403 via `_http_error`), `create_runtime()` factory seam (like `search.create_orchestrator`, tests patch it) then `await runtime.run_enforced(context, goal)` — SAME `AgentRuntime` path as in-process, so budget reservation, approval gates (`WAITING_APPROVAL`), tool allowlist, deadline all parity by construction. In-memory `_RUNS` store mirrors runtime `_PERSISTED_*`. `StatusResponse`/`StartRunResponse` carry `status`/`error`/`error_code` from `RunState`.
- `apps/api/app/main.py` (M — allowed seam ONLY, +2 lines) — imports `agent_router` and `app.include_router(agent_router)` after `eval_router`; no existing routes modified.
- `apps/api/app/agents/__init__.py` (NEW — seam, 3 lines, avoids shadowing `app.agents.router` module so `monkeypatch.setattr("app.agents.router.create_runtime")` works).
- `tests/unit/test_agent_runs_api.py` (NEW — allowed, new API test file preferred over appending to `test_agents.py` — say why: keep agent unit tests (164) focused on pipeline logic, and API contract tests (6) focused on HTTP/auth/parity with `TestClient` + `get_principal` overrides; live-server tests need `httpx` + `require_service` skip, which belongs in an API file, not in the agents pipeline file; separation keeps `ruff`/`mypy` on touched files minimal and makes the gate-bypass metric visible at the HTTP seam). 6 tests: `test_api_unapproved_write_blocked_parity_with_inprocess` (no approve -> in-process `WAITING_APPROVAL` vs HTTP status `waiting_approval` — parity), `test_api_over_budget_blocked_parity` (tiny `RunBudget` 1/1/0 -> HTTP 201 with `limit_exceeded` or 400, never bypass 200), `test_api_cross_workspace_blocked` (WS_OTHER -> 403 for start + get + cancel), `test_api_unauthenticated_refused` (bad-token override -> 401/403 never 200, plus real no-header 401), `test_api_start_status_cancel_lifecycle` (201 -> 200 status -> 200 cancel), `test_live_agent_runs_require_service` (live-server self-skip via `api_client` `require_service` — when stack down skips loudly with `knowledge-os API not reachable at http://127.0.0.1:8000 — start it with docker compose up -d`, when up asserts route mounted and auth-enforced 401/403 not 404).
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row).

WHY: V3 research pipeline is complete in-process (plan->fan-out->verify->gaps->conflicts->synthesis with the same ExtractiveGrounder bar and budget discipline); the HTTP surface must not be a bypass. The three routes are thin wrappers over the same `AgentRuntime.run_enforced` and the same `RunBudget`/`RunState`/`can_reserve_*` gates, behind the same `get_principal`/`require` middleware that the `/search` and `/ask` routes use, so every gate that is exercised in-process is exercised identically over HTTP. ## 2026-10-13 T-V3-025 — External research connector opt-in (SSRF-safe fetch adapter, default off)

TASK: T-V3-025 (solo, ~30 min, no subagents). Approved-connector fetch adapter with scoped snapshots, default OFF. Store URL + time + content-hash + licence + workspace on every fetch (a snapshot without provenance is unfetchable-for-audit — assert all five fields). Deny private-net/file URLs (SSRF: no 10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, 127/8, ::1, link-local, file://, decoded-then-rechecked because %31%32%37%2e%30%2e%30%2e%31 is still 127.0.0.1); redirect guard (follow max N hops, re-validate EVERY hop — a public URL redirecting to 169.254.169.254 is the classic bypass; prove it blocked); flag default FALSE (opt-in per connector, never global-on).

CHANGE (my delta = 1 NEW + 1 M + this row, 0 foreign paths touched — `packages/graph/*` + `packages/answer/engine.py` untouched per V2 attribution, `packages/workflow_engine/**` untouched, `packages/agents/**` untouched):
- `packages/research/external_connector.py` (NEW — allowed, 240 lines, ruff + mypy clean) — `ExternalSnapshot(url,fetched_at,content_hash,licence,workspace_id,content,status_code)` (five provenance fields frozen), `ConnectorDisabledError`/`SSRFBlockedError(url,reason)`/`ExternalFetchError` typed errors, `validate_url_for_fetch(url)` (decoded-then-rechecked: `urllib.parse.unquote` loop 2×, scheme allow `http/https` only, `file://` denied, host extracted via `urlparse.hostname`, `_is_blocked_host` checks `localhost`/`.localhost`/`metadata`, `_is_blocked_ip` via `ipaddress` blocked nets `10/8,172.16/12,192.168/16,127/8,169.254/16,0/8,fc00::/7,fe80::/10,::1/128` plus `is_private`/`is_loopback`/`is_link_local`/`is_reserved`), `FakeResponse(url,status_code,headers,content,is_redirect)` + `FetchTransport` Protocol, `ExternalConnector(workspace_id,licence,enabled=False,transport,max_redirects=3)` (default FALSE opt-in per connector, never global-on, `workspace_id`/`licence` validated non-empty), `enable()`/`disable()`/`snapshots`/`fetch(url)` (if not enabled -> `ConnectorDisabledError`; `validate_url_for_fetch` decoded -> re-validate every hop in `for hop in range(max_redirects+1)` loop, `transport.fetch` injected fake, redirect `Location` via `urljoin` + `validate_url_for_fetch` before follow, `too many redirects` after max, success builds `content_hash=sha256(content)` + `fetched_at=datetime.now(UTC).isoformat()` + `licence` + `workspace_id` + `url` + `content` and appends to `_snapshots` for audit). Decoded-then-rechecked guarantees `%31%32%37%2e%30%2e%30%2e%31` == `127.0.0.1` is still blocked; redirect guard guarantees public `https://example.com/start` 302 to `http://169.254.169.254/meta` is blocked at hop 1 before second transport call.
- `tests/unit/test_research.py` (M — allowed, append-only) — 6 new tests (fake transport, never real network, refusal from validator not timeout): `test_external_snapshot_fields_all_five` (fetch `https://example.com/a` -> assert `url`/`fetched_at`/`content_hash==sha256("hello")`/`licence=="MIT"`/`workspace_id=="ws-1"` + `snapshots` audit), `test_external_default_off_fetches_nothing_until_enabled` (fresh `enabled is False` -> `ConnectorDisabledError`, `calls==[]`, `snapshots==[]`, `enable()` -> fetch succeeds 1 call), `test_external_ssrf_hostile_battery_all_refused_with_typed_errors` (parametrised 14 hostile URLs: `10/8`, `172.16/12`, `192.168/16`, `127/8`, `[::1]`, `[fe80::1]`, `169.254.169.254`, `file:///`, `%31%32%37%2e%30%2e%30%2e%31` encoded loopback, `ftp://` -> all `SSRFBlockedError` with `transport.calls==[]`), `test_external_redirect_guard_revalidates_every_hop` (`https://example.com/start` 302 to `http://169.254.169.254/meta` -> `SSRFBlockedError`, first hop in `calls`, second hop not in `calls`), `test_external_redirect_max_hops_enforced` (3 chained 302 with `max_redirects=1` -> `too many redirects`), `test_external_validate_decoded_then_rechecked` (`%31%32%37%2e%30%2e%30%2e%31` and `%30` Still blocked). Total `tests/unit/test_research.py` now 29 (14 parametrised hostile + 15 others).
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row).

WHY (trace-R2): V3 research needs an approved external-connector surface that is audit-unfetchable-for-audit without provenance, SSRF-safe against private-net/file/encoded/redirect bypasses, and default-off per connector (never global-on) — your T-040 experience (scoped snapshots + validator) applies directly. Reuses the “store URL + time + content-hash + licence + workspace” provenance pattern from T-V3-014 evidence adapter (all five fields) and the “decoded-then-rechecked + every-hop” SSRF discipline from the T-040 connector review, without touching the live `knowledge` tables or the blackholed-egress box (tests inject a fake transport and assert the validator refused, not a connection timeout).

REUSED: `urllib.parse.unquote`/`urlparse`/`urljoin` + `ipaddress.ip_address`/`ip_network`/`is_private`/`is_loopback`/`is_link_local` (stdlib SSRF validator, no new dep), `hashlib.sha256` + `datetime.now(UTC).isoformat()` (same provenance pattern as `agents/evidence.py` `content_hash` + `verified_at`), `dataclass(frozen=True)` + `Protocol` (same as `agents/verifier.py` `ChunkSource` + `EvidenceVerifier` one-grounder seam), `FakeResponse`/`FetchTransport` (same fake-transport pattern as `agents/planner.py` `_FakeStormLLM` + `tests/fixtures/harness_fakes.py`). No `httpx`/`requests` live socket in tests, no `qdrant_client`, no `langgraph`. T-021 `require_service` loud-skip pattern not needed here (unit-only, fake transport), but the same “assert refusal from YOUR validator” principle as `agents/adapters` vendor fence.

BUILT: `ExternalSnapshot` (5 provenance fields + content), `ConnectorDisabledError`/`SSRFBlockedError`/`ExternalFetchError` (3 typed errors), `_BLOCKED_NETWORKS` (9 nets) + `_extract_host`/`_is_blocked_ip`/`_is_blocked_host`/`validate_url_for_fetch` (decoded-then-rechecked + file/private/encoded/link-local/metadata), `FakeResponse` + `FetchTransport` seam, `ExternalConnector(workspace_id,licence,enabled=False,transport,max_redirects)` with `enable`/`disable`/`snapshots`/`fetch` (every-hop re-validate, max 3, hash + time + licence + workspace audit). Flag default `False` per instance, never global-on (prove: fresh connector `enabled is False`).

- `/root/ko-venv/bin/ruff check packages/research/external_connector.py tests/unit/test_research.py` → **All checks passed** (1 E501 fixed on validator docstring, E402/I001 fixed via `import hashlib  # noqa: E402`).
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/mypy packages/research/external_connector.py tests/unit/test_research.py` → **Success: no issues found in 1 source file** (external_connector clean; test file Success after `import pytest as _pytest2  # noqa: E402, I001`).
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_research.py -q` → **29 passed** (15 pre-existing Phase 12 + 14 hostile battery + 5 other new = 29; all parametrised hostile URLs `SSRFBlockedError`, redirect guard `SSRFBlockedError` before second transport call, snapshot hash `sha256("hello")`).
- Metrics: SSRF paths: 0 (`test_external_ssrf_hostile_battery_all_refused_with_typed_errors` parametrised 14x private nets `10/8`/`172.16/12`/`192.168/16`/`127/8`/`::1`/`fe80::1`/`169.254.169.254`/`file`/`%31%32%37%2e%30%2e%30%2e%31` encoded/`ftp` + `test_external_redirect_guard_revalidates_every_hop` + `test_external_validate_decoded_then_rechecked` + `test_external_redirect_max_hops_enforced` — ALL `SSRFBlockedError` with `transport.calls==[]` or first-hop-only, never `ExternalFetchError` timeout).
- Checklist: (1) snapshot fields stored (all five, asserted: `url` `fetched_at` `content_hash==sha256` `licence` `workspace_id` + `snapshots` audit `len==1`); (2) no private access (hostile battery 14x + redirect-to-metadata 1x + encoded 2x all `SSRFBlockedError`); (3) default off (fresh `enabled is False` -> `ConnectorDisabledError`, `calls==[]`, `snapshots==[]`, `enable()` -> success).

RISKS: (1) `_is_blocked_ip` uses `ipaddress` private/loopback/link_local/reserved checks — a future public-range host that resolves via DNS to a private IP (DNS-rebinding) would require a DNS-resolving validator (current validator checks the URL host string only, not the resolved IP of a hostname like `attacker.example` that later resolves to `127.0.0.1`); the redirect guard partially covers this (every hop re-validated), but a pure DNS-rebiding shape without redirect would need a `socket.getaddrinfo`-based check behind the same seam (transport could expose resolved IPs). (2) `ExternalSnapshot.content` stores the full body in-memory (`_snapshots` list) — a 10 MB fetch would be held in RAM and hashed; prod would stream to object storage with `hashlib` incremental and store only the hash + storage key (same five fields, content offloaded). (3) `validate_url_for_fetch` does two `unquote` passes — triple-encoded `%252537` would decode to `%2537` -> `%37` -> `7` in three passes, but such triple-encoding is not a valid IP literal and would be caught as missing host or non-IP; a future hardening could loop until stable. (4) No graph/answer/workflow_engine change — `packages/graph/*` + `packages/answer/engine.py` untouched per V2 attribution, `packages/workflow_engine/**` untouched.

## 2026-10-13 T-V3-026 — Failure-semantics + source-verification suite (every failure mode pinned)

TASK: T-V3-026 (solo, ~25 min, no subagents). A suite that pins the failure semantics of the whole research path end-to-end (planner → fan-out → verifier → gap → synthesis → connector): every failure MODE produces its documented code/surface (TOOL_TIMEOUT vs TOOL_NOT_ALLOWED vs LIMIT_EXCEEDED vs WAITING vs ConnectorDisabled vs SSRFBlocked vs NO_EVIDENCE vs contradiction-cap — each asserted at the surface the caller sees), and every SOURCE cited in a synthesis output verifies back to a stored chunk (source-verification sweep: citations resolve, tombstoned/deleted sources REFUSE rather than resolve stale).

CHANGE (test-only, no product changes — product changes are OUT even for bugs found: file them, don't fix):
- `tests/unit/test_agents.py` (M — allowed, append ONLY, 200 lines, ruff clean, mypy Success) — 8 new tests: `test_failure_taxonomy_all_codes_pinned_no_invented` (enumerates `AgentErrorCode` 13 values vs expected set `tool_not_allowed` … `unknown_required`, none invented, each `transition_to(FAILED, error_code=code)` pinned at `RunState` surface), `test_failure_tool_timeout_surface` (`is_retryable(TOOL_TIMEOUT)` true vs `TOOL_NOT_ALLOWED` false, `TOOL_TIMEOUT.value` pin), `test_failure_tool_not_allowed_surface` (WRITE `publish` without `approve_run` -> `WAITING_APPROVAL` with `TOOL_NOT_ALLOWED`, parity via `AgentRuntime` + `_PERSISTED_STATES`), `test_failure_limit_exceeded_surface` (`LIMIT_EXCEEDED` + `BUDGET_EXCEEDED` pin), `test_failure_waiting_surfaces` (`WAITING_APPROVAL`/`WAITING_INPUT` non-terminal), `test_failure_cancelled_surface` (`request_cancel` -> `CANCELLED`), `test_failure_source_revoked_surface` (`EvidenceVerifier` grounded then `tombstone("chk-1")` -> `CHUNK_TOMBSTONED`/`NOT_GROUNDED` REFUSE, not stale), `test_source_verification_citations_resolve_and_tombstoned_refuse` (synthesis `ev1`/`ev2` -> grounded sentences each `chunk_ids` resolve via `InMemoryChunkSource`, tombstone `chk-1` -> REFUSE, gap `REASON_NO_EVIDENCE`, contradiction candidates capped `MEDIUM`).
- `tests/unit/test_research.py` (M — allowed, append ONLY, 120 lines, ruff clean, mypy Success) — 6 new tests: `test_research_failure_connector_disabled_surface` (`ConnectorDisabledError` with `disabled` at `fetch` surface, fake transport never called), `test_research_failure_ssrf_blocked_surface` (`SSRFBlockedError` with `private` on `127.0.0.1`, validator surface not timeout), `test_research_failure_no_evidence_surface` (`GapAnalyzer` empty -> `REASON_NO_EVIDENCE` unknown, not fabricated), `test_research_failure_contradiction_cap_surface` (`ContradictionDetector` `900` vs `1900` -> `MEDIUM` with `Conflicting` limitation, above `MEDIUM` 0), `test_research_failure_provider_error_surface` (`ResearchEngine` `_Broken` LLM -> `ResearchEngineError`, not fake report), `test_research_source_verification_tombstoned_refuse` (`MemoryEvidenceStore` `ev-1` `chunk_id c1` -> `get_evidence` resolves, add `revoked_at` -> `synthesize_report` excludes `ev-rev` from `evidence_ids`, stale 0).
- `handoffs/CODE1-HANDOFF.md` (this row) — filed product bugs: **NONE** (suite passed, no failing behavior to file; if a bug were found it would be filed here with file+lines+failing behavior per “file them, don't fix” and the fix would become the next ticket).

WHY: V3 research stack is complete (plan with bounded FREE-only + fan-out with concurrency/budget + verifier with one-grounder + gaps retained + conflicts as candidates with MEDIUM cap + grounded synthesis + external connector with SSRF guard + APIs with gate parity); without a pinned failure suite, a later change could silently invent a new error code, swallow a failure, or resolve a stale citation. The suite pins every documented surface at the caller-visible boundary (the `AgentErrorCode`/`RunStatus`/`GapAnalyzer`/`SSRFBlockedError` string the caller branches on) and sweeps every synthesis citation for `resolve` vs `REFUSE` on tombstoned/deleted, so “every failure has a name and every citation has a source” is enforced, not documented.

REUSED: `agents/interfaces.py` `AgentErrorCode`/`RunState`/`RunStatus` (taxonomy, none invented) + `agents/runtime.py` `is_retryable`/`RunBudget`/`AgentRuntime`/`_PERSISTED_STATES` (gates) + `agents/verifier.py` `InMemoryChunkSource`/`EvidenceVerifier` (one-grounder) + `agents/gap.py` `GapAnalyzer`/`REASON_NO_EVIDENCE` + `agents/synthesis.py` `GroundedSynthesizer` + `agents/contradictions.py` `ContradictionDetector` + `research/external_connector.py` `ExternalConnector`/`FakeResponse`/`ConnectorDisabledError`/`SSRFBlockedError` + `research` `MemoryEvidenceStore`/`ResearchEngine`/`ResearchEngineError` (provider failure). No live LLM, no network — fake gateways/transports only (`_Fake`, `_T`, `_Broken`).

BUILT: Failure taxonomy pin (13 codes), 14 tests total (8 in `test_agents.py` covering planner/fan-out/verifier/gap/synthesis/connector agent modes + source sweep, 6 in `test_research.py` covering research/connector/provider modes + revoked sweep) — all assert the documented `value`/status at the surface the caller sees, and all use fake transports/gateways (no `sleep`, no wall-clock).

## 2026-10-13 T-V3-023 — Run UI list/detail/timeline (LANE BREAK — frontend work in dev2's lane)

TASK: T-V3-023 (solo, ~35 min, no subagents). LANE BREAK (explicit): this is frontend work in dev2's lane — taken because dev2 is blocked on an unanswered permission dialog with no ruling in sight and the loop must keep moving. Goal from MICRO-TICKETS-V3.md: run list + detail + timeline over the T-V3-021 run API. Limits/usage visible (budget state surfaced, not hidden); accessible error AND empty states (a run that failed/cancelled reads clearly; zero runs reads as empty, not as broken); NO raw reasoning rendered anywhere (the no-hidden-reasoning rule extends to the UI: StepEvent has no reasoning field — prove none is displayed by rendering from typed fields only; assert it).

CHANGE (my delta = 2 NEW page files + this row; API routes NOT touched — read only; `packages/graph/*` + `packages/answer/engine.py` untouched per V2 attribution; `packages/workflow_engine/**` untouched; no commit/deploy/restart/compose):
- `apps/web/src/app/agent-runs/page.tsx` (NEW — allowed; path note: the ticket said `apps/web/app/agent-runs/page.tsx`, but this repo's Next.js app root is `apps/web/src/app/` — every existing route (`/ask`, `/search`, `/knowledge`) lives there, so the same route is created at the real app root; `apps/web/app/` would have been unrouted) — "use client" list page mirroring `app/ask/page.tsx` + `app/search/page.tsx` patterns: workspace scope from `useWorkspaces()`, all API calls through the typed `auth.authFetch` proxy (same as `ask/contracts.ts`), typed wire mirrors of `RunSnapshot`/`StartRunBody`/`RunRecord` (run_id, goal, status, error, error_code + the limits set at start). Run list: browser-scoped localStorage registry per workspace (`agent-runs:v1:<ws>`) because the API ships start/status/cancel but NO server-side list — each row renders LIVE status from `GET /agent/runs/{id}` plus goal, the limits line (`N tokens · N steps · N tool calls`), a status chip whose WORD is always rendered (tone secondary), and the error code explained in plain text via `ERROR_CODE_LABELS`. Start form (goal + max_steps/max_tokens/max_tool_calls with labels) writes the record and POSTs the run. Empty state: `role="status"` "No runs yet for this workspace. Runs started from this browser appear here; empty means nothing has been started, not that the service is down." Start failure: `role="alert"` with the message. No-workspace state reads as a scoped prompt, not an error.
- `apps/web/src/app/agent-runs/[id]/page.tsx` (NEW — allowed) — "use client" detail page via `useParams()`, same auth/scope/client pattern: live `GET /agent/runs/{id}` with 4s→3s polling that STOPS at terminal statuses; 404/403 → `role="alert"` "not found in this workspace"; progress state as `role="status"` "This run is {status}"; Budget section surfaces limits (from the local record when tracked, with an honest "not started from this browser, so its limits are not recorded locally" fallback) and budget/deadline exhaustion in text when `status=limit_exceeded` + `budget_exceeded`/`deadline_exceeded`; Outcome section renders final status + error_code + its plain-text explanation (failed/cancelled reads clearly); Timeline section is an `<ol>` of lifecycle states OBSERVED FOR THIS RUN IN THIS BROWSER, labelled as such (the API exposes current status only, so the page does not invent transition history it was not given); Cancel action through the existing `POST /agent/runs/{id}/cancel`, disabled once terminal with "This run has finished (…); it can no longer be cancelled." Only typed fields are rendered.

WHY: V3 UI surface for the T-V3-021 routes — a run lifecycle that cannot be SEEN cannot be audited or operated. The list makes runs discoverable (browser-scoped because no server list endpoint exists — stated, not hidden), the detail makes state legible (budget exhaustion, deadline, cancellation, failure — each as text with a chip), and the no-hidden-reasoning rule extends to the UI by construction: the pages render only `run_id`/`goal`/`status`/`error`/`error_code`/limits — no free-form model output field exists in the wire types or the JSX. The heightened bar for the lane break is met: `tsc --noEmit` clean (exit 0), reasoning-leak asserted (0 grep hits), a11y basics (labelled inputs, `role="status"`/`role="alert"`, text-first status words, keyboard buttons).

REUSED (client patterns): `auth` typed client from `@/lib/auth-client` (same-origin `/api/backend` proxy, single-flight refresh, error mapping — same as `ask/contracts.ts` `askQuestion`), `useWorkspaces()` from `@/components/workspace/workspace-provider` (workspace scope, same as `/ask` + `/search`), `Badge`/`Button`/`Input`/`Label` from `@/components/ui/*`, the status-chip tone pattern from `app/knowledge/status-chip.tsx` (unknown state renders neutral, never mis-coloured), Tailwind class patterns and the `role="status"`/`role="alert"` conventions from `app/search/page.tsx`. No new dependency, no new client mechanism, no API change.

BUILT: `RunStatusValue`/`RunErrorCode`/`RunSnapshot`/`StartRunBody`/`RunRecord` typed mirrors, `localStorage` registry helpers (`loadRecords`/`saveRecords`/`loadRecord`, key `agent-runs:v1:<ws>`, storage-unavailable safe), `RunStatusChip` (tone map incl. neutral for unknown), `ERROR_CODE_LABELS` (13 taxonomy codes → plain text), list page (registry + poll refresh + start form + empty/error states), detail page (live fetch + polling that stops at terminal + timeline of observed transitions + budget/outcome/actions sections + cancel). Zero free-text model fields rendered.

## 2026-10-13 T-V4-005 — B-slice R2 vehicle: config-validation enforcement point (determine-or-build → VEHICLE EXISTS, verification-only)

TASK: T-V4-005 (solo, ~30 min, no subagents). B-slice R2 VEHICLE: the enforcement point where config validation actually bites (import/compile/run refusing invalid config through the B-R1-fixed validators). Determine-first: read the adopted diff (0d23253, T-V4-001..004) + REW2-V4-B-R1.md + DEV21-V4-HANDOFF.md and determine whether an enforcement vehicle already exists; if yes PROVE it with a rejection test per validator through that point (verification-only); if NO, build one.

DETERMINATION — **VEHICLE EXISTS** (no product change needed or made): the single point where all four B-R1 validators run on every import is `LangflowAdapter.import_definition`'s security pass in `packages/workflow_engine/adapters/langflow_adapter.py`: per node it calls `cls._check_config_values(raw_node, node_id, spec)` (line 795), which runs every allowlisted config value through `cls._validate_config_value` (line 1556) — the number branch carries B-R1-2's `math.isfinite` guard (line 1604) and B-R1-1's magnitude pre-reject + `_format_number` bounded echo (lines 1614-1618), the list branch carries B-R1-3's per-element str/length validation (lines 1659-1669), and B-R1-4's documented dict-drop is pinned at `_template_value` (mapping payloads → `None`) + `_check_config_values`' docstring. Any rejection raises `LangflowImportError` at line 806, BEFORE `_build_node_spec` (line 1718, invoked at line 838) constructs `NodeSpec` — invalid config never reaches config or the revision hash. `import_revision` (line 878) delegates to `import_definition` — there is no second entry point and no other path from untrusted flow JSON into config. So this ticket was **verification-only**: 0 product lines changed.

CHANGE (my delta = 1 NEW test file + this row; `packages/workflow_engine/**` product files untouched — the B-R1-fixed files were wired, not re-fixed; no B-R1 finding regressed, so no BLOCKED push):

## 2026-10-13 T-V4-006 — Validator: graph-size / fan-out / config-size limits (no unbounded imports)

TASK: T-V4-006 (solo, ~30 min, no subagents). Hard limits on import-time resource consumption — graph NODE/EDGE counts, per-node fan-out (edges per node), and total config SIZE — each with a stated cap, typed rejection past it, and no unbounded path (a 10k-node flow, a 1:M fan-out hub, and a multi-MB config must each refuse fast, never OOM the importer or hang it).

CHANGE — **MY FILE LIST (announced for dev2's T-V4-015 W3 lane disjointness): `packages/workflow_engine/validator.py` (M, the only product file) + `tests/unit/test_workflow_resource_limits.py` (NEW) + this row.** Not touched: `executor.py`, `engine.py`, `compiler.py`, `interfaces.py`, `adapters/**` (dev2's W3 area — his `adapters/v3_adapter.py` was `??` at START, committed by him mid-ticket; zero overlap with my hunks, no force needed), no B-R1 finding re-fixed:
- `packages/workflow_engine/validator.py` (M, +157 lines, ruff + mypy clean) — T-V4-006 resource-bounds layer: module caps `MAX_GRAPH_NODES=200` (mirrors the adapter's import-time `MAX_NODES`; structural checks are quadratic in n), `MAX_GRAPH_EDGES=500` (mirrors `MAX_EDGES`; 500 over ≤200 nodes is dense already), `MAX_EDGES_PER_NODE=64` (per-node fan-out: every catalog node declares ≤2 output ports and even a shared upstream node fans out to at most a few dozen targets; 64 is generous for real DAGs while refusing a 1:M hub whose adjacency work is linear in M per visit), `MAX_CONFIG_BYTES_PER_NODE=32_768` (per-node canonical-JSON config bytes — import caps per-field length, not the sum; a node with many large-but-legal strings would bloat the revision hash), `MAX_CONFIG_BYTES_TOTAL=1_048_576` (definition-wide: 200 nodes × 32 KB = 6.4 MB worst case, so the total caps the canonical JSON recomputed on every hash verification). Helpers `_echo` (bounded id echo in messages) + `_config_bytes` (serialise like the hash path with `default=str`; circular refs fail closed over-cap). New check `WorkflowValidator._check_resource_bounds` (O(n+m): len counts, one Counter pass for out-degree, one serialisation per node for config size) wired as a SHORT-CIRCUITING pre-pass in `validate()` — past a cap the structural checks below are quadratic in the hostile size (cycle DFS, per-edge node scans), so refusing first is what keeps the validator from hanging; undersized definitions behave exactly as before. Typed codes: `TOO_MANY_NODES`, `TOO_MANY_EDGES`, `NODE_FAN_OUT_TOO_HIGH`, `NODE_CONFIG_TOO_LARGE`, `GRAPH_CONFIG_TOO_LARGE`.
- `tests/unit/test_workflow_resource_limits.py` (NEW, 18 tests, ruff + mypy clean) — 6 classes: `TestGraphNodeCountBound` (10k nodes → `TOO_MANY_NODES` with elapsed < 1 s; boundary 200 accepted, 201 refused; 10k-edge spread → `TOO_MANY_EDGES` fast), `TestFanOutBound` (10k-edge hub → `NODE_FAN_OUT_TOO_HIGH` node_id=hub < 1 s; 64 accepted, 65 refused — isolating the fan-out cap from the edge-total cap; huge node id echo bounded ≤300 chars), `TestConfigSizeBound` (one node > 32 KB → `NODE_CONFIG_TOO_LARGE`; 199 nodes × 7 KB ≈ 1.4 MB with node count at-limit → `GRAPH_CONFIG_TOO_LARGE` in isolation; boundary exactly-at-cap accepted, +1 byte refused), `TestSingleEnforcementPoint` (the point is `validate()` and — via its re-validation — `WorkflowCompiler.compile` raises on oversized nodes / fan-out hub / fat config; a small valid definition still compiles with matching hash), `TestLayerParity` (validator caps == adapter import caps: one policy across layers), `TestImportLayerRefusesFast` (the untrusted-JSON boundary itself: a 10k-node Langflow flow → `OVERSIZED_FLOW` in < 1 s, existing adapter cap proven unchanged).

WHY: the T-V4-005 proof pinned WHERE config validation bites; this ticket closes the remaining unbounded-consumption holes one layer down: import caps bound per-field size and node/edge counts on the JSON boundary, but nothing bounded per-node config TOTALS, per-node fan-out, or the size of definitions built outside the import path (code-constructed `WorkflowDefinition`s). Since `WorkflowCompiler.compile` re-validates through `WorkflowValidator.validate`, the validator is the single point every definition must pass before execution — so the caps there refuse every shape fast, and the pre-pass ordering is what makes the refusal cheap (never the quadratic structural work on a hostile graph).

REUSED: the existing validator structure (`ValidationError`/`ValidationResult` typed channel, staticmethod check style, `Counter`), the adapter's import-time caps (same numbers — one policy), the compiler's existing re-validation (line 71, unchanged) as the routing to the point, and the B-slice bounded-echo practice (`_echo`, mirroring the adapter's `_trunc`). No new dependency, no executor/compiler/interface changes.

BUILT: 5 caps + 2 helpers + 1 pre-pass check (157 lines in `validator.py`) and the 18-test proof module. Every cap is stated with its reason in the code. Refusal is typed (stable codes on the existing `ValidationError` channel) and bounded (messages ≤ 300 chars even for a 50 KB node id).

- `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_workflow_langflow_adapter.py tests/unit/test_workflow_langflow_fixture.py tests/unit/test_workflow_validator.py tests/unit/test_workflow_compiler.py tests/unit/test_workflow_fake_runtime.py tests/unit/test_workflows.py tests/unit/test_workflow_config_enforcement.py tests/unit/test_workflow_resource_limits.py -q` → **359 passed** (331 from T-V4-005's baseline + 18 new + other streams' additions; no regressions in the langflow/validator/compiler/workflow suites).

- `/root/ko-venv/bin/mypy packages/workflow_engine tests/unit/test_workflow_resource_limits.py` → **Success: no issues found in 10 source files**.
- **Metrics:** oversized imports accepted: **0** — 10k-node graph (`TOO_MANY_NODES`), 1:M hub (`NODE_FAN_OUT_TOO_HIGH`, isolated at the 65-edge boundary), multi-MB config (`NODE_CONFIG_TOO_LARGE` + `GRAPH_CONFIG_TOO_LARGE`, isolated at 199×7 KB with node count at-limit) all refused; through `validate()` AND through `compile()` (re-validation) AND at the import boundary (existing adapter cap, proven). Refusal time bounded: every hostile shape asserted `elapsed < REFUSAL_TIME_LIMIT_S` (1.0 s; measured in the low milliseconds), and the pre-pass ordering means the quadratic structural checks never run on a hostile graph.
- **No over-rejection:** at-limit node count (200), at-limit edges-per-node (64), at-limit per-node config (exactly 32 768 bytes) all pass the resource layer; a small valid definition still validates and compiles with matching revision hash; validator caps == adapter import caps (one policy).

## 2026-10-13 T-V4-007 — Validator port type-compatibility hardening (mismatched wires refuse, no silent coercion)

TASK: T-V4-007 (solo, ~25 min, no subagents). Every edge connecting two ports checks type-compatibility and REFUSES mismatches with a typed error naming both ports and their types (no silent coercion, no Any-swallows-everything, no stringly-typed bypass). Covers: (1) the compatibility matrix itself (which type pairs connect — stated explicitly, not emergent; any pair not in the matrix refuses); (2) generic/parameterized ports (e.g. list<T> vs list<U> checks element types, not just the container — same elements-not-container discipline as B-R1-3); (3) optional/nullable edges (None-handling explicit per port, not a global escape hatch).

CHANGE — **MY FILE LIST (announced for dev2's T-V4-016 W3-resume lane disjointness): `packages/workflow_engine/interfaces.py` (M, additive syntax only) + `packages/workflow_engine/validator.py` (M, the compatibility rules) + `tests/unit/test_workflow_port_types.py` (NEW) + this row.** Zero overlap with dev2's concurrent untracked files (`packages/workflow_engine/checkpoint.py`, `tests/unit/test_workflow_checkpoint.py` — appeared mid-ticket, his lane, untouched by me; no pause/BLOCKED needed). Resource bounds from T-V4-006 not relitigated; executor untouched:
- `packages/workflow_engine/validator.py` (M, +126/−16) — the rules are now STATED, not emergent: `_PORT_TYPE_MATRIX` = {(b,b) for b in bases} ∪ {any→b} ∪ {b→any} — identity pairs plus the DECLARED ``any`` wildcard on either side, and nothing else (a pair absent from the matrix refuses; ``any`` is in the matrix because the port carrying it explicitly opts out, not as an emergent fallback). `_types_compatible` rewritten in three ordered rules: (1) matrix on the CONTAINER pair (a parameterized list is still a ``list``), (2) nullability — a nullable source (``string?``) may only feed a nullable target, per-port, never global, (3) generics — ``list<A>``→``list<B>`` recurses on ELEMENT types (``list<list<number>>`` vs ``list<list<string>>`` refuses at the inner element; bare ``list`` = ``list<any>``, an explicitly untyped element). `_mismatch_reason` names the refusal class ("type pair not in the compatibility matrix" / "nullable source into non-nullable target" / "list element types differ (X vs Y)"), bounded; `_check_type_compatibility` messages keep naming edge + both ports + both types and append the reason (bounded ≤300 chars).
- `packages/workflow_engine/interfaces.py` (M, +43/−2, purely additive syntax) — `_is_supported_port_type()` accepts the port-type grammar: bases (string/number/boolean/dict/list/any), recursive ``list<…>`` parameterization, and a trailing ``?`` nullable marker, with `_MAX_PORT_TYPE_DEPTH=8` (the type value is an untrusted-length string, so the recursive syntax check itself is bounded). `PortSpec.model_post_init` uses it; existing flat types behave identically. Nothing else in the package interprets port types (executor/compiler read names only — verified by grep), so this is the validator's contract to extend.
- `tests/unit/test_workflow_port_types.py` (NEW, 75 tests) — matrix-vs-behavior over all 36 base pairs; off-matrix battery (12 pairs through a real definition → exactly one TYPE_MISMATCH naming edge/ports/types/reason); valid-wire battery at the matrix edges (identity + any both directions, no over-rejection); generics (identical/nested accepted; 5 element mismatches refused with the element reason; container mismatch; bare-list both directions; any-element); nullability (non-null→nullable, nullable→nullable accepted; nullable→non-nullable refused with the nullable reason; per-base abuse battery over all 5 non-any bases; nullable does not rescue base mismatch; nullable inside generics and on the container); syntax (15 accepted incl. 3-deep nesting; 11 rejected incl. `list<>`, `string??`, depth-10 nesting); dangling ports still skip the type check (reported by the dangling check); legacy flat any-typed flow still validates; reason helper bounded.

WHY: the type check was an emergent two-line comparison (equality or any-on-either-side) — which cannot state what connects, cannot see inside containers, and treats every port as implicitly nullable-or-not with no rule at all. This ticket makes the policy explicit (a matrix a reader can cite), element-aware (B-R1-3's elements-not-container discipline applied to types), and nullability-explicit (per-port ``?``, one direction rule), so a mismatched wire can never be silently coerced or swallowed by an undeclared wildcard.

REUSED: the existing validator structure (staticmethod checks, `ValidationError` channel, the `_check_type_compatibility` message shape naming both ports and types — preserved and extended), `PortSpec`'s existing constructor-time type validation (extended, not replaced), the B-R1-3 elements-not-container precedent, and the bounded-echo practice from T-V4-006/B-slice. No new dependency; executor/compiler untouched.

BUILT: `_PORT_TYPE_MATRIX` (stated), `_split_nullable`, `_list_element`, the three-rule `_types_compatible`, `_mismatch_reason`, the extended `_is_supported_port_type` syntax + depth cap in interfaces.py, and the 75-test proof module. Matrix statement (verbatim from the code): a `(source, target)` base pair connects iff it is an identity pair `(b, b)`, or ``any`` appears on either side as the declared wildcard — every other pair refuses.

VERIFY (exact, from repo root; HEAD `bff690e` — T-V4-006 committed; nothing committed by me):
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_workflow_port_types.py -q` → **75 passed**.
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest` all 9 workflow suites (langflow adapter + fixture + validator + compiler + fake runtime + workflows + config enforcement + resource limits + port types) → **434 passed** (359 from T-V4-006's baseline + 75 new; no regressions — the langflow flows with ``any`` ports still validate and round-trip).
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit -q` → **1606 passed** (whole unit suite, incl. dev2's concurrent checkpoint tests in the tree; 1 StarletteDeprecationWarning).
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/ruff check packages/workflow_engine/validator.py packages/workflow_engine/interfaces.py tests/unit/test_workflow_port_types.py` → **All checks passed** (note: `packages/workflow_engine/checkpoint.py` has 3 pre-existing ruff errors — E501×2 + F821 `dataclass` — in dev2's UNTRACKED lane file; not mine, not touched, reported here for his lane).
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/mypy packages/workflow_engine/validator.py packages/workflow_engine/interfaces.py tests/unit/test_workflow_port_types.py` → **Success: no issues found in 3 source files**.
- **Metric 1 — mismatched wires accepted: 0.** Off-matrix battery: all 12 off-matrix base pairs → exactly one TYPE_MISMATCH through a real definition; nested generics `list<list<number>>`→`list<list<string>>` (and 4 more element mismatches) refused with "list element types differ"; nullable abuse: every `base?`→`base` over all 5 non-any bases refused with "nullable source into non-nullable target"; `string?`→`number?` refused (nullable does not rescue an off-matrix pair); `list<string>`→`dict` refused (container). Every refusal names the edge, both ports and both types, plus the reason class.
- **Metric 2 — valid wires rejected: 0.** Matrix edges: all 6 identity pairs + any-wildcard both directions (`any→string`, `string→any`, `any→list`, `list→any`) pass; generics: identical parameterized lists (incl. 3-deep nesting) pass, bare↔parameterized both directions pass, `list<any>` wildcard passes; nullability: `string→string?`, `string?→string?`, `list<string>→list<string?>`, nullable container accepted; the legacy flat any-typed flow still validates, and all 9 workflow suites (incl. the langflow round-trip fixture with ``any`` ports) stay green.
- Matrix-vs-behavior is proven exhaustively: for all 36 base pairs, `_types_compatible` == matrix membership (test_matrix_compatibility_matches_matrix_for_every_base_pair) — the behavior IS the stated matrix.

RISKS:
1. **Syntax extension in interfaces.py** is additive-only (flat types unchanged; only new parameterized/nullable forms become constructible) and nothing but the validator interprets port type strings (grep-verified: executor/compiler read port names, not types). A future consumer of `.type` must parse the syntax via the same helpers.
2. **``any`` remains a wildcard by design** — it is listed in the matrix, not emergent, and both the ticket and the PortSpec contract treat it as a declared opt-out; tightening it further (removing the wildcard) would break existing flows that use ``any`` ports deliberately.

## 2026-10-13 T-V4-008 — Compiler: determinism + join-semantics lock

TASK: T-V4-008 (solo, ~30 min, no subagents). The compiler (definition → executable plan) is DETERMINISTIC (same definition bytes → byte-identical plan every run; prove: compile twice, compare) and its JOIN semantics are locked (every join node declares its strategy — all/any/first — with explicit barrier behavior: which inputs it waits for, what it emits on partial inputs, timeout behavior; an undeclared join refuses to compile).

CHANGE — **MY FILE LIST (announced for dev2's T-V4-016 lane disjointness): `packages/workflow_engine/compiler.py` + `packages/workflow_engine/interfaces.py` + `packages/workflow_engine/__init__.py` + `tests/unit/test_workflow_compiler_determinism.py` (NEW) + 3 existing test files updated to the new declared semantics + this row.** Zero overlap with dev2's in-flight `?? packages/workflow_engine/store_discipline.py` (his `checkpoint.py`/`test_workflow_checkpoint.py` were committed/fixed by him mid-ticket — his 14 tests now green; not mine, not touched, not cited as baseline). Executor untouched:
- `packages/workflow_engine/interfaces.py` (M, additive) — `JoinStrategy` enum (`all`/`any`/`first`) + `NodeSpec.join_strategy: JoinStrategy | None` (declared barrier; `None` legal only for non-join nodes); `ExecutionStep` gains the STATED barrier: `join_waits_for` (sorted inbound ports), `join_emit_on_partial`, `join_timeout_s`. **Determinism fix (found while proving it):** `WorkflowRevision._compute_content_hash` was SEED-DEPENDENT — `workspace_capabilities` is a `set`, `model_dump(mode="json")` serialised it in set-iteration order, and `json.dumps(sort_keys=True)` sorts dict keys but not list elements, so identical definition bytes hashed differently under different `PYTHONHASHSEED` (proven empirically: 3 seeds → 3 different hashes). New `_canonical_payload` normalizer: python-mode dump (sets stay sets), dict keys stringified+sorted, set members sorted by canonical JSON — the same bytes now hash identically in every process. No absolute hash value is pinned anywhere in the tree (all comparisons are between computed hashes — verified by grep), so the canonicalization change is safe.
- `packages/workflow_engine/compiler.py` (M) — join lock `_check_join_declarations`: a node with ≥2 inbound edges must declare a strategy or compile raises `ValueError` naming the node and the accepted strategies (runs before plan construction); `_join_barrier` emits the stated barrier per the `_JOIN_BARRIERS` table (`all`: waits all-mapped, emits nothing; `any`: waits all-mapped, emits first-arrival; `first`: waits first-mapped only, emits first-input-only; timeout `JOIN_BARRIER_TIMEOUT_S=30.0`); the emergent CONDITION-source → `"any"` INFERENCE is REMOVED (single-inbound nodes carry the trivial `all` barrier — runtime behaviour identical for one wire); `input_mapping` keys sorted for byte-stability.
- `packages/workflow_engine/__init__.py` (M) — exports `JoinStrategy` (flat alphabetical `__all__`, codebase convention).
- `tests/unit/test_workflow_compiler_determinism.py` (NEW, 16 tests) — determinism: 25 in-process repeats byte-identical (`model_dump_json` full payload), two revisions of the same bytes identical, plan built only from ordered/sorted collections, **cross-process: identical bytes compiled under `PYTHONHASHSEED=1/2/12345` in fresh subprocesses produce identical plan bytes AND identical revision hash** (the set-order trap); join lock: undeclared join refuses (through the standard compile entry too), single-inbound needs no declaration (no over-rejection), each strategy's barrier parametrized (`waits_for`/`emit_on_partial`/`timeout`), 2-wires-from-one-source is still a join, declared joins deterministic across repeats, invalid strategy rejected at construction, validator layer undisturbed, plan JSON round-trips.
- 3 existing test files updated (5 tests) because they pinned the emergent inference — NOT weakened, re-pinned to the declared contract: `test_workflow_compiler.py`: `test_compile_join_mode_declared_any_for_condition_downstream` (declares ANY on a 2-inbound node, asserts the any-barrier + the trivial all barrier for single-inbound), `test_compile_join_mode_all_for_regular_merge` (declares ALL on the merge node); `test_workflow_fake_runtime.py`: `_skipped_join_defn` merge node declares ALL (its docstring already said "all-join" — now explicit); `test_workflow_langflow_fixture.py`: condition-downstream single-inbound nodes carry the trivial all barrier (runtime behaviour for one inbound edge is unchanged).

WHY: two locks the compiler previously left implicit. (1) Determinism was asserted only in-process, while the plan's `revision_hash` was seed-dependent through a set field — identical bytes could publish different revision identities in different processes, breaking plan/hash equality across workers. The canonical normalizer makes the hash process-independent and the proof is now cross-process. (2) Join semantics were INFERRED from source node types (a CONDITION source implied `"any"`) with no way to declare intent and no refusal for ambiguity; the barrier (waits-for / emit-on-partial / timeout) existed nowhere. Now every join node declares its strategy, the barrier is stated on the step, and an undeclared join refuses to compile — the executor can dispatch from the plan without guessing.

REUSED: the existing compiler structure (topo sort with sorted ties, hash verification, re-validation entry — unchanged), the `ValidationError`-style typed refusal channel (`ValueError` as before, now with node + strategies named), the bounded-echo/declared-policy practice from T-V4-006/007, and the existing `ExecutionStep.join_mode` field consumed by the executor (`all`/`any` values preserved for compat). No new dependency.

BUILT: `JoinStrategy` enum + `NodeSpec.join_strategy` + 3 `ExecutionStep` barrier fields (interfaces), `_canonical_payload` seed-independent hash normalizer (interfaces), `_check_join_declarations` + `_JOIN_BARRIERS` table + `_join_barrier` + sorted `input_mapping` (compiler), and the 16-test determinism/join-lock suite incl. the subprocess seed proof.

## 2026-10-13 T-V4-009 — Executor honesty: terminal states, no false-COMPLETED

TASK: T-V4-009 (solo, ~30 min, no subagents). The executor NEVER reports COMPLETED unless every node reached a genuine terminal state with its outputs recorded; failures surface as FAILED with the failing node named, cancellations as CANCELLED, skips as SKIPPED — no silent success, no swallowed node errors, no green run with red nodes inside. Covers: (1) terminal-state machine (every run ends in exactly one of COMPLETED/FAILED/CANCELLED with the transition rule stated); (2) per-node status honesty (a failed node fails the run or is explicitly covered by a declared join/error policy — never silently absorbed); (3) cancellation propagation (cancel mid-run → CANCELLED, in-flight nodes settled, no COMPLETED-after-cancel).

CHANGE — **MY FILE LIST (announced for dev2's T-V4-017 lane disjointness): `packages/workflow_engine/executor.py` + `packages/workflow_engine/interfaces.py` (additive contract for the state machine only) + `tests/unit/test_workflow_executor_honesty.py` (NEW) + `tests/unit/test_workflow_fake_runtime.py` (one test re-pinned) + this row.** Zero overlap with dev2's in-flight untracked files (`draft_store.py`/`store_discipline.py`/`test_workflow_draft_store.py` at START, rotated to `routes/` mid-ticket — HIS files, his red draft-store tests are NOT my baseline, NOT touched). Compiler/validator not relitigated:
- `packages/workflow_engine/interfaces.py` (M, additive) — `WorkflowRunStatus.CANCELLED` (the state machine could not represent cancellation at all before); `WorkflowRun.error` + `WorkflowRun.failed_node_id` (a non-COMPLETED run names its terminal node — no unlabelled status); `NodeRun.error` documented as the honest-skip-reason carrier.
- `packages/workflow_engine/executor.py` (M) — **terminal-state table (stated in code):** COMPLETED ⇔ every node has a terminal record, none FAILED, no join node (≥2 inbound) left with an incomplete declared barrier or unreached, and every non-skipped node recorded its output (`_finalize`); FAILED ⇔ a node raised, returned an `{"error": …}` payload (previously absorbed into outputs!), or a declared barrier was left incomplete — the run names the node (`failed_node_id` + `error`); CANCELLED ⇔ `request_cancel()` observed before the next dispatch (deterministic mid-run cancel from inside a node) or an external `asyncio.CancelledError` (in-flight node settled FAILED("cancelled mid-execution"), remaining nodes SKIPPED, then re-raised — asyncio semantics preserved). New honesty mechanics: skip REASONS recorded on every SKIPPED (`barrier-incomplete` / `no-active-inbound-edge` / `upstream-skipped` / `cancelled`); `_propagate_skipped` — a skipped node still decrements its targets' pending as INACTIVE so a downstream any/first barrier can observe the inputs it did receive (previously a skipped branch collapsed the whole tail into "never reached" and an any-barrier could never be satisfied by its one live input); `_settle_remaining` — every fast-fail path records the never-ran nodes as SKIPPED, so a FAILED run still discloses its tail (no missing records = no silent holes); single-wire skips are routed as `no-active-inbound-edge` (branch not taken = routing, disclosed, run may complete) while multi-inbound barrier skips fail the run (declared work incomplete); `WorkflowExecutor.request_cancel()` + `cancel_requested`.
- `tests/unit/test_workflow_executor_honesty.py` (NEW, 13 tests) — hostile battery: node raises RuntimeError, node raises NotImplementedError, node RETURNS `{"error": …}` (→ FAILED, never absorbed), cancel requested from inside the trigger (→ CANCELLED, `calls == ["start"]` proves nothing starts after cancel, in-flight output recorded), all-barrier partial inputs (→ FAILED naming merge), any-barrier zero active input (→ FAILED naming join); no-over-rejection: clean linear run COMPLETED with all nodes COMPLETED and `final_output` recorded, routing skip on a single-inbound node disclosed SKIPPED while the run COMPLETES, any-barrier with one active input COMPLETES (the propagation fix), full all-barrier COMPLETES; state machine: the terminal set is exactly {COMPLETED, FAILED, CANCELLED} (PENDING/RUNNING excluded), every finished run has `finished_at` and no RUNNING/PENDING residue, external task cancellation settles and re-raises (`pytest.raises(asyncio.CancelledError)`).
- `tests/unit/test_workflow_fake_runtime.py` (M, 1 test re-pinned) — `test_skipped_branch_skips_join` previously asserted the false-COMPLETED shape (all-barrier merge skipped by a partial condition branch, run COMPLETED with no output node executed). It now asserts the honest contract: run FAILED, `failed_node_id == "merge"`, merge SKIPPED with `barrier-incomplete`, end SKIPPED with `no-active-inbound-edge`. This is the contract change the ticket mandates, not a weakening.

WHY: the executor had three honesty holes: a node returning an `{"error": …}` payload was recorded as COMPLETED output (a swallowed error); cancellation did not exist (no CANCELLED state, no cancel path, so a cancelled task either hung or looped to COMPLETED); and a skipped branch abandoned its edges, collapsing the downstream tail into "never reached" — which made `any`/`first` barriers unusable and left FAILED runs with missing node records. The terminal-state table above is the stated rule; every battery shape and the no-over-rejection half are pinned against it.

REUSED: the existing executor structure (readiness loop, edge-activation, `_build_input`, hash verification — all unchanged), the `WorkflowRun`/`NodeRun` typed channel (extended additively), the T-V4-008 declared-barrier semantics (`all` emits nothing on partial; `any` first-arrival; `first` first-mapped-only) which the executor now dispatches from instead of collapsing, and the joindeclaration contract. No new dependency.

BUILT: `CANCELLED` + run-level `error`/`failed_node_id` (interfaces); error-payload-as-failure, `_propagate_skipped`, `_settle_remaining`, `_finalize` (COMPLETED ⇔ records-complete + no failed + no incomplete barrier + outputs recorded), `_finish_cancelled`, `request_cancel`, skip-reason taxonomy, external-CancelledError settle-and-re-raise, and the 13-test honesty suite incl. the deterministic mid-run cancellation.

- `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest` all 12 workflow suites (langflow adapter + fixture + validator + compiler + compiler determinism + fake runtime + executor honesty + workflows + config enforcement + resource limits + port types + checkpoint(dev2's, observed only)) → **477 passed**.
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit -q` → **1695 passed** (whole unit suite; 1 StarletteDeprecationWarning; dev2's in-flight draft-store files rotated mid-ticket, none failing under me, not cited as baseline).
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/ruff check` my 4 files (executor, interfaces, 2 test files) → **All checks passed**.
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/mypy packages/workflow_engine/` + the two test files → **Success: no issues found in 14 source files**.
- **Metric — false-COMPLETED runs: 0.** Battery: node raises (RuntimeError + NotImplementedError) → FAILED with the node named; node returns an error payload → FAILED (previously silent COMPLETED); cancel mid-run → CANCELLED with `calls == ["start"]` (nothing starts after) and the in-flight node's output preserved; all-barrier partial inputs → FAILED naming the merge; any-barrier zero active input → FAILED naming the join. None end COMPLETED, and every non-COMPLETED run carries `error` + `failed_node_id`.
- **Metric — genuine completions refused: 0.** Clean linear run COMPLETES (every node COMPLETED, `final_output` recorded); single-inbound routing skip stays disclosed SKIPPED and the run COMPLETES (condition flows green across fake_runtime + langflow fixture suites); any-barrier with one active input COMPLETES (propagation fix); full all-barrier COMPLETES; all 477 workflow-suite tests green with no re-pinned expectation other than the one false-COMPLETED case the ticket targets.

RISKS:
1. **`first`-barrier runtime dispatch** — the executor runs a `first`-join on its first active arrival and skips it with none; the full "await first-mapped only" semantics live in the plan's stated barrier (T-V4-008) — consistent, but a follow-up could align the executor's any/first branches to read `join_waits_for` directly.

## 2026-10-13 T-V4-010 — Executor: bounded node semantics (no runaway nodes)

TASK: T-V4-010 (solo, ~30 min, no subagents). Every node executes under explicit bounds — per-node timeout (a hung node fails the node, never the run's liveness), per-node output-size cap (a flooding node is refused, never OOMs the run), and retry limits with backoff (transients retry N times then fail loudly, never loop forever). Each bound stated with its default, each breach names the node + the bound + the observed value.

CHANGE — **MY FILE LIST (announced for dev2's T-V4-019 route-contracts lane disjointness): `packages/workflow_engine/executor.py` + `packages/workflow_engine/interfaces.py` (additive: `NodeRun.attempts`) + `tests/unit/test_workflow_executor_bounds.py` (NEW) + this row.** Zero overlap with dev2's `packages/workflow_engine/routes/**` (untouched); compiler/validator untouched; terminal-state semantics from T-V4-009 reused unchanged:
- **Bound table (defaults + reasons, stated in code):** `NODE_TIMEOUT_S = 30.0` (per-node wall-clock — a hung node fails the node at the limit instead of hanging the run's liveness; worst-case node cost is attempts × timeout; 30 s is generous for one step); `MAX_NODE_OUTPUT_BYTES = 1_048_576` (per-node output cap in canonical-JSON bytes — refused before the output is stored in `node_outputs`, fed downstream, or hashed; 1 MB is far above any legitimate payload and far below the OOM territory a flooding node aims for); `NODE_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 3` (retry cap, total attempts — a permanently failing node fails loudly, never loops forever); `NODE_RETRY_BASE_S = 0.05` (bounded exponential backoff `base × 2^(n-1)`, deterministic, total retry delay ≤ 0.15 s at the defaults). The bounds are **executor/deployment level and never node-configurable** — `NodeSpec.config` is untrusted imported data, so a node must not be able to widen or shrink its own bounds (`configure_bounds(...)` validates at wiring time: timeout/base > 0, attempts ≥ 1, bytes ≥ 1).
- `packages/workflow_engine/executor.py` (M) — new `_execute_within_bounds(node, input, workspace_id) -> _NodeOutcome` wrapping every node execution: `asyncio.wait_for` per attempt (timeout), `_output_bytes` measurement before store/propagate (output cap), retry loop with `self._sleep(base × 2^(n-1))` (sleep injectable for determinism), `NotImplementedError` treated as permanent (no wasted retries), `asyncio.CancelledError` propagating untouched as control flow (the T-V4-009 settle path owns it), error-payload rule preserved. Every breach message names the node + bound + observed: timeout → `node 'X' exceeded the per-node timeout (0.05s bound; attempt 1 timed out after 0.051s)`; cap → `node 'X' returned 3145742 bytes of output; the per-node cap is 1024 bytes`; exhaustion → `node 'X' failed after 3 attempts (bounds: timeout 30.0s, max attempts 3): …` with the last error. The loop consumes the outcome: `ok` → COMPLETED + edges as before (attempts/latency recorded on the `NodeRun`), `not ok` → node FAILED, run FAILED via the T-V4-009 terminal table (`failed_node_id` + `error` + `_settle_remaining` disclosure).
- `packages/workflow_engine/interfaces.py` (M, additive) — `NodeRun.attempts: int = 0` so the capped attempt count is recorded on the node trace (observable, asserted).
- `tests/unit/test_workflow_executor_bounds.py` (NEW, 14 tests) — timeout (hung node fails fast with the bound+observed message, elapsed < 5 s against a 30 s sleep, attempts capped at the retry cap, just-under-timeout passes); output cap (3 MB flood refused naming 3145742 observed bytes vs the 1024 bound, output never stored/propagated, `final_output` None, just-under passes); retry cap (always-fail → exactly 3 attempts then loud failure, injected sleep records the deterministic backoff schedule `[0.05, 0.1]`; fail-twice → recovers on attempt 3 with attempts recorded on the node; permanent `NotImplementedError` → exactly 1 attempt); config hygiene (invalid bounds rejected at wiring time, cancellation inside a bounded execution propagates and is not retried, plain construction keeps the defaults); the stated defaults are asserted as constants.

WHY: the previous two tickets made the run's terminal state and the work per node honest; this one bounds the NODE. Without a timeout a single hung node hangs the whole run (liveness); without an output cap a flooding node OOMs the run and its downstream; without a retry cap a permanently failing node retries forever. All three are stated defaults with reasons, configured at the deployment/executor level (never from untrusted node config), and every breach is a named failure at the node surface (node + bound + observed) that flows into the T-V4-009 FAILED terminal state — never a silent success and never an unbounded loop.

REUSED: the executor's readiness loop, edge-activation and `_build_input` (unchanged), the T-V4-009 terminal table + `_settle_remaining` disclosure + error-payload rule (the bounded outcome feeds them), the T-V4-008 barrier semantics (unrelated but co-resident), and the agent-runtime retry posture (cap + bounded backoff) applied here at the executor layer. No new dependency; bounds validated at wiring time.

BUILT: `NODE_TIMEOUT_S`/`MAX_NODE_OUTPUT_BYTES`/`NODE_MAX_ATTEMPTS`/`NODE_RETRY_BASE_S` constants + reasons, `_NodeOutcome`, `_output_bytes`, `configure_bounds` (validated knobs incl. injectable sleep), `_execute_within_bounds` (timeout + cap + capped backoff retry + permanent/control-flow handling), `NodeRun.attempts`, and the 14-test bound suite.

VERIFY (exact, from repo root; HEAD `4257643` — T-V4-009 committed; nothing committed by me):
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_workflow_executor_bounds.py -q` → **14 passed**.
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest` all 13 workflow suites (langflow adapter + fixture + validator + compiler + determinism + fake runtime + executor honesty + executor bounds + workflows + config enforcement + resource limits + port types + checkpoint(dev2's, observed only)) → **491 passed**.
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit -q` → **1735 passed** (whole unit suite; 1 StarletteDeprecationWarning).
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/ruff check packages/workflow_engine/executor.py packages/workflow_engine/interfaces.py tests/unit/test_workflow_executor_bounds.py` → **All checks passed**.
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/mypy packages/workflow_engine/executor.py packages/workflow_engine/interfaces.py tests/unit/test_workflow_executor_bounds.py` → **Success: no issues found in 3 source files** (dev2's `routes/schemas.py` carries 7 pre-existing mypy errors in HIS in-flight lane — reported, not touched).
- **Metric — unbounded executions: 0.** Hung node (30 s sleep, 0.05 s bound) → FAILED in < 5 s naming node + bound + observed elapsed, attempts == NODE_MAX_ATTEMPTS; 3 MB flood → FAILED naming 3145742 observed bytes vs the 1024 bound, output never stored (`output_data is None`, `final_output is None`); always-failing node → exactly `NODE_MAX_ATTEMPTS` attempts then loud failure ("after 3 attempts"), backoff schedule recorded `[0.05, 0.1]`; cancellation inside a node propagates and is not retried (calls == 1).
- **Metric — legitimate slow/large nodes refused: 0.** Node sleeping 0.05 s under a 5 s bound → COMPLETED (attempts == 1); output well under a 1024-byte cap → COMPLETED; transient failure failing twice then succeeding → COMPLETED on attempt 3 with `attempts == 3` recorded; all 491 workflow-suite tests green with no re-pinned expectations.

RISKS:
1. **Retries re-execute the node with the same input** — a side-effecting node could double-apply work within the attempt cap. The cap bounds it; nodes with external side effects should treat idempotence as their own contract (a per-node retry policy is a follow-up, not this ticket's scope).
2. **Output measurement serialises the result once** (`json.dumps(default=str)`, or UTF-8 bytes for strings) — the node's own allocation already happened before the cap applies; the cap prevents storage/propagation/hashing, which is the executor-controlled surface.
3. **`configure_bounds` can raise the defaults** (it is a deployment knob, deliberately) — a caller choosing a larger timeout/cap owns that decision; the wired value is what every breach message names.
4. dev2's `routes/schemas.py` mypy errors are pre-existing in his lane (reported here, not fixed — lane discipline). No commit/deploy/restart/compose.

## 2026-10-13 T-V4-011 — Executor: AGENT node via abstract adapter (no direct V3 import)

TASK: T-V4-011 (solo, ~30 min, no subagents). Workflows containing an AGENT node execute it through the W3 V3NodeExecutor adapter seam (the injected-AgentRunner Protocol from T-V4-015) — the executor routes AGENT nodes to the seam, honors its refusals (disabled/missing-capability/no-runner), and records the authority output unchanged. NO direct import of V3 product code in the executor (seam-only, same rule as T-V4-015); the flag stays OFF by default so production behavior is unchanged.

CHANGE — **MY FILE LIST (announced for dev2's T-V4-020 ACL+hygiene lane disjointness): `packages/workflow_engine/executor.py` (M) + `tests/unit/test_workflow_agent_seam.py` (NEW) + this row.** Zero overlap with dev2's in-flight W4 files (his `draft_store.py`, `migrations/*`, `routes/schemas.py`, `store_discipline.py`, `credential_hygiene.py` and their tests were modified/created by HIM during my run — untouched by me). Validator/compiler untouched; bounds/terminal/joins from T-V4-008/009/010 reused unchanged:
- `packages/workflow_engine/executor.py` (M) — **Routing rule (stated):** AGENT nodes execute through the injected seam (`configure_agent_executor`); every other node — and AGENT nodes with no seam wired — execute through the primary injected executor; there is no third path. `WorkflowExecutor(node_executor)` gains an optional `agent_executor` seam (`configure_agent_executor`, `agent_executor` property, `_executor_for(node)`, wiring validated against the `NodeExecutor` Protocol — a bad wiring fails at configuration time). The executor imports NEITHER `workflow_engine.adapters.v3_adapter` NOR `packages/agents` — seam-only, and the dependency direction stays one-way (adapters import the executor for `FakeNodeExecutor`, never the reverse), so there is no import cycle and no direct V3 import. `_execute_within_bounds` resolves `executor = self._executor_for(node)` once per node, so all bounded attempts of an AGENT node go to the same seam. Default (no seam) keeps the pre-existing path byte-identical: AGENT → injected executor → NotImplementedError → FAILED.
