## 2026-10-13 T-V4-012 — Executor: run budgets + deadlines (run-level, NOT per-node retry)

TASK: T-V4-012 (solo, ~30 min, no subagents). Every RUN executes under a run-wide deadline (wall-clock budget for the whole run; expiry cancels cleanly to CANCELLED with the deadline named, never a hung run) and a run-wide resource budget (max total node-seconds / max nodes executed; exhaustion fails loudly naming the spent budget, never silent truncation). Per-node bounds from T-V4-010 stay the inner layer; this ticket is the outer envelope and the two compose (a node timeout does not consume the run deadline twice — state the composition rule).

CHANGE — **MY FILE LIST (announced for dev2's T-V4-020 lane disjointness): `packages/workflow_engine/executor.py` (M) + `tests/unit/test_workflow_run_budget.py` (NEW) + this row.** Zero overlap with dev2's rotating in-flight W4 files (his `draft_store.py`/`migrations/*`/`routes/schemas.py`/`store_discipline.py` were committed by him mid-ticket, and new untracked `adapters/v3_contract.py` + `test_workflow_v3_adapter_contract.py` appeared in HIS lane — untouched by me). Validator/compiler untouched; per-node retry caps NOT relitigated (T-V4-010 is the inner layer, composed below).
- **Envelope table (defaults + reasons, stated in code):** `RUN_DEADLINE_S = 300.0` (run-wide wall-clock — 300 s covers any realistic authored workflow with the validator's 200-node cap; expiry cancels cleanly, never hangs); `RUN_MAX_NODE_SECONDS = 600.0` (total node execution time — exhaustion fails loudly naming the spent value, never silent truncation; 2× the deadline so parallelizable work fits while bounding compute); `RUN_MAX_NODES = 200` (executed-node cap — the accounting-side assertion that the executed set never exceeds the declared graph; non-binding today because the compile-time graph cap already enforces ≤200, stated so a future executor that re-enters nodes cannot escape accounting).
- `packages/workflow_engine/executor.py` (M) — **COMPOSITION RULE (stated):** the deadline is measured as WALL-CLOCK ELAPSED, the resource budget as NODE-SECONDS SPENT, and each node's work is charged EXACTLY ONCE (attempts + bounded backoff, via `envelope.charge_node`); the effective per-node timeout is shortened to the deadline's remaining time (`min(node_timeout, remaining)`), so a node cannot outlive the run and a node timeout never consumes the run deadline twice; the ENVELOPE OUTRANKS the node's own outcome (once wall-clock or budget is spent, the run ends on the envelope, named). New `_RunEnvelope` dataclass (deadline/node-seconds/nodes, injectable `clock`, `elapsed`/`remaining`/`*_exceeded`/`charge_node`), `configure_run_bounds(...)` (wiring-validated: deadline/node-seconds > 0, nodes ≥ 1; `clock` injectable for determinism), `_new_envelope()` (accounting recorded on `executor.last_envelope` for observability), deadline checked before every dispatch AND after every node outcome, budget checks after every node outcome, and `_finish_deadline()` (CANCELLED with `run deadline reached: X.XXXs elapsed, deadline Ys( node 'Z' crossed it)`, completed nodes keep their outputs, never-ran nodes SKIPPED-disclosed). `_execute_within_bounds` gained `timeout_s`/`timeout_reason` overrides so the adjusted bound is named (`run-deadline-adjusted per-node timeout (0.05s bound …)`). The node's own outcome is recorded BEFORE the envelope decides (a node that completed is recorded COMPLETED even when the run then cancels; a node that failed is recorded FAILED) — the run terminal state comes from the envelope.
- `tests/unit/test_workflow_run_budget.py` (NEW, 13 tests) — deadline: over-deadline run (node sleeps 0.3 vs 0.05 deadline) → CANCELLED naming `run deadline reached` + the 0.05 bound + elapsed, the node failed with the `run-deadline-adjusted per-node timeout` message, the remaining node SKIPPED-disclosed, elapsed < 5 s; deadline spent before dispatch → the next node never starts (`fake.attempts` has no `end`); just-under deadline (30 s) → COMPLETED. Budgets: node-seconds exhaustion → FAILED naming `node-seconds budget exhausted` + spent + `budget 0.01s`; node-count exhaustion (max 1) → FAILED naming `node-count budget exhausted` + `2 nodes executed, budget 1`; just-under budgets → COMPLETED with `nodes_executed == 3` and node_seconds < budget. Composition: a node timing out (0.05 inner bound, 5 s sleep) inside a generous envelope (deadline 30 s / node-seconds 30 s) fails the NODE, the run FAILS naming it, `node_seconds < 1.0` (one charge of ~0.3 s for 3 attempts — never 3× the timeout, never the 5 s sleep), `nodes_executed == 2` (trigger + mid, mid charged once), elapsed < 5 s, retries still capped at 3 (T-V4-010 intact); node-cannot-outlive-the-run proof (adjusted bound named); config hygiene (invalid envelope rejected at wiring; defaults active for plain construction).

WHY: T-V4-010 bounded the NODE; nothing bounded the RUN — a workflow of 200 nodes × 30 s node timeouts could occupy a worker for 100 minutes with no run-level stop, and a hung node chain could never be interrupted at the run boundary. The envelope adds the outer bound with the composition rule that keeps the two layers from double-charging: wall-clock for the deadline, node-seconds for the budget, one charge per node, and the node's effective timeout clipped to the deadline's remainder so no node can outlive its run.

REUSED: the T-V4-009 cancellation/terminal machinery (`_finish_cancelled`'s disclosure shape reused for `_finish_deadled`, `_settle_remaining`, CANCELLED status + run.error naming), the T-V4-010 bounded-execution helper (extended with the adjusted-timeout override rather than duplicated), the injectable-sleep pattern from T-V4-010 (now an injectable clock for the deadline), and the validator's 200-node cap as the accounting parity point. No new dependency; validator/compiler untouched.

BUILT: `RUN_DEADLINE_S`/`RUN_MAX_NODE_SECONDS`/`RUN_MAX_NODES` constants + reasons, `_RunEnvelope` (injectable clock, O(1)-per-node accounting), `configure_run_bounds` + `last_envelope` observability, deadline pre-dispatch + post-outcome checks, node-seconds/node-count post-outcome checks, `_finish_deadline`, the adjusted-timeout plumbing, and the 13-test envelope suite.

## 2026-10-13 T-V4-013 — Executor: idempotency + cancel/resume + duplicate-delivery (last executor ticket)

TASK: T-V4-013 (solo, ~35 min, no subagents). Node execution is IDEMPOTENT under redelivery — a node executed twice (retry, resume, duplicate dispatch) produces the effect once and records once; cancel-then-resume continues from the last settled node (never re-runs settled nodes, never skips unsettled ones); duplicate deliveries of the same node signal are deduplicated by idempotency key, loudly on key mismatch.

CHANGE — **MY FILE LIST (announced for dev2's T-V4-022 palette lane disjointness): `packages/workflow_engine/executor.py` (M) + `tests/unit/test_workflow_idempotency.py` (NEW) + this row.** Zero overlap with dev2's in-flight palette work (`?? tests/unit/test_workflow_palette.py`, his `palette.py`, `M handoffs/CODE2-HANDOFF.md` — HIS files, untouched by me). Validator/compiler untouched; honesty/bounds/envelope/seam-routing done in 009-012 reused unchanged.
- `packages/workflow_engine/executor.py` (M, executor + checkpoint seam ONLY) — **IDEMPOTENCY RULE (stated):** key = ``(run_id, node_id)``; fingerprint = canonical JSON of the work (node id, type, config, input). The ledger is consulted BEFORE any execution: unseen → execute then record; SAME fingerprint → replay the recorded output with ``executes == 0`` (effect once, recorded once, ``_NodeOutcome.replayed=True``); DIFFERENT fingerprint → :class:`IdempotencyKeyConflict` raised loudly (never a silent hit). New ``IdempotencyLedger`` (lookup/record/keys) + ``configure_idempotency_ledger``. **Cancel/resume across the checkpoint seam:** ``execute(..., run_id=..., resume=True)`` — the run id fixes identity across deliveries and resumes (the ledger key); after EVERY settled node (and at every settlement — cancel, deadline, budget, node-failure, terminal) the settled set is appended to the injected ``CheckpointStore`` as a ``WorkflowCheckpoint`` (completed_nodes / node_outputs / node_runs / revision_hash / boundary via dev2's ``checkpoint_boundary``); a resume loads ``latest(run_id)``, refuses on workspace/revision mismatch (``ResumeStateError``), wraps the primary executor in dev2's T-V4-016 ``ResumableExecutor`` (settled nodes replay from the checkpoint — the core loop untouched), re-seeds the ledger from the checkpoint with each settled node's input RECONSTRUCTED from the checkpointed outputs (so post-resume redeliveries still dedup and key reuse still conflicts), and continues the unsettled remainder. Checkpoint writes are best-effort with ``checkpoint_failures`` counted — a failed store never kills the run and never stays silent. ``request_cancel`` state is reset per delivery so one executor instance can drive run-after-run.
- `tests/unit/test_workflow_idempotency.py` (NEW, 10 tests) — duplicate delivery: double-dispatch of the same run id executes each node EXACTLY once (`executes == {"start":1,"mid":1,"end":1}` after two full ``execute`` calls) and the replayed run records the same outputs; different run ids do not collide (each node once per run); same key with different input → ``IdempotencyKeyConflict``; the ledger holds exactly one entry per key. Cancel/resume: cancel after `mid` → CANCELLED with `end` skipped and the checkpoint's ``completed_nodes == {start, mid}``; resume on a FRESH executor (a new "worker") re-executes NOTHING settled (`resume_fake.executes.get("start")/("mid") is None`), runs the unsettled `end` exactly once, replays the settled outputs, completes with the same ``final_output`` — and a duplicate delivery AFTER the resume still replays (`end` executed once across both); a resumed run's node-for-node ``(status, output_data)`` map equals a never-interrupted baseline's, with the work split exactly (cancelling worker: start+mid; resume worker: end); foreign workspace/revision resume → ``ResumeStateError``; a broken checkpoint store → ``checkpoint_failures >= 1`` with the run still COMPLETED; no store → resume runs normally.

WHY: a worker that retries, resumes, or receives a duplicate delivery of the same node signal would double-apply side effects (double write, double charge) unless the execution is keyed; and a cancel/resume that re-runs settled nodes re-applies their effects while one that skips unsettled nodes silently truncates the run. This ticket makes exactly-once the executor's contract: one ledger key per (run, node), one charge per settled node, a checkpoint after every settled node so resume continues from the last settled node, and loud refusals for key reuse and foreign state.

REUSED: dev2's T-V4-016 checkpoint seam verbatim (``WorkflowCheckpoint`` / ``CheckpointStore`` / ``ResumableExecutor`` / ``checkpoint_boundary`` / ``ResumeStateError`` — his committed file, unchanged; the executor imports it, and the import direction stays one-way since ``checkpoint.py`` imports only ``interfaces``), the T-V4-009 terminal table + `_settle_remaining` disclosure (resume/cancel settle through the same paths), the T-V4-010 bounded execution (the ledger lookup sits in front of it — a replay bypasses the bounds because there is nothing to bound), and the T-V4-012 envelope (accounting charges once per node, replays not at all). No new dependency.

BUILT: ``IdempotencyKeyConflict``, ``_fingerprint``, ``IdempotencyLedger``, the ``run_id``/``resume`` parameters on ``execute``, the ledger lookup/record inside ``_execute_within_bounds`` (keyed by run id, with the resume executor override), ``_save_checkpoint`` + ``_load_checkpoint`` + ``_seed_ledger_from_checkpoint`` (input reconstruction from checkpointed outputs), checkpoint writes at every settlement point, and the 10-test idempotency/resume suite.

VERIFY (exact, from repo root; HEAD `f5fc30b` — T-V4-012 committed; nothing committed by me):
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit/test_workflow_idempotency.py -q` → **10 passed**.
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest` all 16 workflow suites (langflow adapter + fixture + validator + compiler + determinism + fake runtime + honesty + bounds + agent seam + run budget + IDEMPOTENCY + workflows + config enforcement + resource limits + port types + checkpoint(dev2's, observed only)) → **528 passed**.
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/pytest tests/unit -q` → **1911 passed** (whole unit suite; 1 StarletteDeprecationWarning).
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/ruff check packages/workflow_engine/executor.py tests/unit/test_workflow_idempotency.py` → **All checks passed**.
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/mypy packages/workflow_engine/` + the test file → **Success: no issues found in 17 source files** (whole package incl. dev2's checkpoint/palette in-tree files).
- **Metric — duplicate effects: 0.** Two full ``execute`` calls with the same run id → ``executes == {"start":1,"mid":1,"end":1}`` (one effect per node, one record per node); different run ids → one execution each per run (no false dedup); same key + different input → ``IdempotencyKeyConflict`` (loud, never a silent hit); ledger keys == exactly the 3 executed keys.
- **Metric — resume re-runs settled nodes: 0.** Cancel mid-run (after `mid`) → checkpoint ``completed_nodes == {start, mid}``; resume on a fresh executor → ``start``/``mid`` executes are ``None`` on the resume worker (replayed from the checkpoint), the resumed run's settled set and outputs are identical to the cancelled run's prefix, and a duplicate delivery after resume still replays (`end` executed exactly once across the two workers).
- **Metric — unsettled nodes skipped on resume: 0.** `end` (unsettled at cancel) executes exactly once on resume and the resumed run COMPLETES with ``final_output == "end-out"``; a resumed run's node-for-node ``(status, output_data)`` map equals a never-interrupted baseline's exactly.
- **Fault injection:** broken checkpoint store → run still COMPLETED with ``checkpoint_failures >= 1`` (never silent, never fatal); foreign workspace/revision on resume → ``ResumeStateError``; no store → resume runs normally.

RISKS:
1. **Replay bypasses the per-node bounds by design** — a ledger hit performs no execution, so there is no timeout/output surface to bound; the recorded output was bounded when it was produced.
2. **The ledger is in-memory** — a process kill loses it; the checkpoint (durable seam) is what a cross-restart resume uses, and it re-seeds the ledger. A shared/durable ledger (Redis, the T-012 pattern) is a follow-up.
3. **Input reconstruction on resume** walks ``completed_nodes`` in checkpoint order using checkpointed outputs; if a settled node's output was ``None`` it is skipped for ledger seeding (the ResumableExecutor wrapper still serves it) — a ``None``-output settled node therefore conflicts rather than replays on redelivery, which is the safe direction.
4. dev2's palette files (his T-V4-022 lane) appeared untracked mid-ticket — untouched by me, hunks disjoint, no pause/BLOCKED needed. No commit/deploy/restart/compose.

## 2026-10-13 T-V4-024 — W6 schedule/trigger connector contracts, DISABLED by default

TASK: T-V4-024 (solo, ~30 min, no subagents). Schedule (cron/interval/one-shot) + trigger (webhook/event/manual) connector CONTRACTS — shapes, validation, workspace scoping, credential-by-reference only — with the connectors DISABLED by default (no timer fires, no webhook listens, no event consumed until explicitly enabled; enabling without a store/runner refuses loudly). Same flag-off discipline as the W3 adapter: contract reviewable now, live later.

CHANGE — **MY FILE LIST (announced for dev2's T-V4-023 view_models lane disjointness): `packages/workflow_engine/connectors.py` (NEW) + `tests/unit/test_workflow_connectors.py` (NEW) + this row.** NEW connector files ONLY — executor/validator/compiler/checkpoint/draft/routes/palette untouched (and `workflow_engine/__init__.py` untouched: the new surface is imported from its own submodule, keeping the existing package exports exactly as they were). dev2's `view_models.py` + its test (his T-V3-023/T-V4-023 lane) were untracked at START and were committed by him mid-ticket — untouched by me.
- **Contract table:** ``ScheduleSpec`` (kind ``cron``/``interval``/``one_shot``; exactly the kind's own field may be present — mixed shapes refused; cron = 5 whitespace-separated fields from a bounded charset, ≤100 chars, SYNTAX SHAPE only — the cron evaluator is the live ticket; interval ∈ (0, 7 776 000 s] — 90 days, because a longer interval is a cron job in denial; one_shot = parseable, timezone-aware ISO-8601 ``run_at``). ``TriggerSpec`` (kind ``webhook``/``event``/``manual``; webhook endpoint is a PATH on this surface — ``://`` anywhere or a non-path form is refused, so the connector can never be an open redirect; event name is dotted lowercase ``a.b``). Both: ``workspace_id`` required (bounded id; unscoped refused), ``enabled`` default False, ``credential_refs`` (each parsed through dev2's ``CredentialReference.parse`` — a value-shaped string is refused even under a reference field) and ``config`` passed through dev2's ``assert_references_only`` — ONE deny-list in the tree (T-V4-020), imported not re-implemented. Both connectors: ``register`` (validate + hold — inert), ``for_workspace`` (scoped listing), ``enable(*, store, runner)`` (BOTH required — half-armed raises ``ConnectorNotArmedError``), ``disable``, and counters ``registered``/``evaluated``/``consumed``/``fired``. Firing surfaces — ``ScheduleConnector.tick(now)`` and ``TriggerConnector.receive(address, payload, workspace_id=...)`` — REFUSE while disabled (``ConnectorDisabledError``: "no timer fires / no webhook listens"); ``due()`` is pure arithmetic (works disabled; it fires nothing), interval counts from registration, one-shot evaluates at its instant, cron is never reported due by this contract. Even ENABLED, this ticket only accepts and evaluates: ``fired`` stands at 0 (dispatch-to-runner is the live ticket) — stated in the module docstring, not hidden.
- `tests/unit/test_workflow_connectors.py` (NEW, 53 tests) — disabled-by-default (default connectors raise on tick/receive, counters 0; registering is inert; enabling with store=None / runner=None / either-missing refuses ``ConnectorNotArmedError`` and leaves ``enabled is False``; enabled connectors evaluate and accept but start NOTHING — ``fired == 0`` and the runner stays untouched; disable re-refuses); hostile battery (35 parametrized schedule cases: empty/whitespace/oversized/spacey/path-y workspace ids, empty/oversized/spacey schedule ids, kind-shape mismatches — cron missing cron, cron+interval mixed, interval None/0/negative/over-90-days, one_shot missing/unparseable/naive timestamps, cron 4-field/6-field/command-injection/200-char; 13 parametrized trigger cases: webhook missing endpoint, relative path, full URL, protocol-relative ``//evil.example/hooks``, 300-char path, event missing/uppercase/single-token/7-segment name, kind-shape mismatches, empty/oversized ids; 6 credential-value configs — ``api_key``/``password``/``authorization`` values, nested secret, and VALUES wearing a reference field's name — all refused; reference forms pass); scoping/evaluation (per-workspace listing and due scoping, cross-workspace trigger delivery refused, interval boundary 59s-not-due/60s-due counting from registration, one-shot boundary, cron shape-valid but never due).

WHY: W6 is the untrusted edge (cron tables, webhook URLs, event buses) — the exact surface where an unscoped schedule or a credential-by-value config becomes another tenant's job or a leaked key. The contracts are written now, DISABLED by default, so the shapes are reviewable without any of the live risk: no timer fires, no listener binds, and enabling refuses half-armed rather than degrading to a silent no-op.

REUSED: dev2's ``credential_hygiene`` (T-V4-020: ``CredentialReference``/``assert_references_only``/``find_credential_value``) imported verbatim — one deny-list in the tree; the W3 adapter's flag-off discipline (``v3_adapter.py``: ``adapter_enabled``, refusal-not-noop, default executor byte-identical) mirrored for connectors; pydantic model patterns already used across the package; ``interfaces`` untouched (the connectors are a new, separate surface). No new dependency, no I/O — the store/runner are injected Protocols.

BUILT: ``ScheduleKind``/``TriggerKind``, ``ConnectorSpec`` base (workspace scoping + credential hygiene + disabled default), ``ScheduleSpec``/``TriggerSpec`` with the kind-shape matrix and bounded fields, ``ConnectorStore``/``ConnectorRunner`` Protocols, ``BaseConnector`` (flag-off discipline + half-arm refusal + counters), ``ScheduleConnector`` (register/due/tick, interval anchor at registration, cron-never-due), ``TriggerConnector`` (register/resolve/receive, workspace-scoped resolution, accept-without-dispatch), and the 53-test contract suite.

## 2026-10-13 T-V4-025 — W7 fault-injection matrix (zero false-completes under faults)

TASK: T-V4-025 (solo, ~35 min, no subagents). A fault-injection matrix over live workflow runs — node crash mid-run, store write failure, checkpoint write failure, runner timeout, cancel-during-retry, double-dispatch, clock-skew on deadlines — proving every fault ends in an HONEST terminal state (FAILED/CANCELLED naming the fault, never COMPLETED, never hung, never a green run with a fault inside). Each fault × each affected layer, stated as a table.

CHANGE — **MY FILE LIST (announced for dev2's T-V4-021/T-V4-026 lanes disjointness): `packages/workflow_engine/fault_matrix.py` (NEW, test-only harness + the stated table) + `tests/unit/test_workflow_fault_matrix.py` (NEW, the matrix) + this row.** Executor/validator/compiler logic UNTOUCHED — every fault is injected through a seam that already exists (NodeExecutor injection, the injectable retry ``sleep``, the injectable run ``clock``, the injectable checkpoint store, the idempotency ledger, ``request_cancel``), which is itself the proof that the seams are sufficient: **no fault hooks were added to product code**. Two more NEW lanes appeared mid-ticket and were not touched: dev2's `test_b1_production_execution_gate.py` (fixed by him mid-run, now 33 green) and another agent's in-flight `test_w7_parity_off_rollback.py` (see JOINT FINDING).
- `packages/workflow_engine/fault_matrix.py` (NEW, ~130 lines, ruff + mypy clean) — ``ScriptedNodeExecutor`` (per-node raise/fail/hang behaviours with an execute counter), ``SkewClock`` (injectable clock that jumps ±N s on a chosen call — the deadline seam), ``FailingCheckpointStore`` (a ``CheckpointStore`` whose ``append`` always raises), ``assert_run_finished`` (shared cell assertion: elapsed under an explicit ceiling, ``finished_at`` present, run status in {completed, failed, cancelled}, every node record terminal — no RUNNING/PENDING residue), ``FAULT_RESOLUTION_LIMIT_S = 5.0``, and the MATRIX TABLE in the module docstring (fault × layer → terminal state → attribution asserted).
- `tests/unit/test_workflow_fault_matrix.py` (NEW, 12 tests — 9 fault cells + 3 hooks-off-by-default cells) — one cell per fault: **node_crash_mid_run** → FAILED with ``failed_node_id == "mid"``, error carrying the node's message, the downstream node SKIPPED-disclosed and never started; **runner_timeout** → FAILED naming the per-node bound + observed elapsed, the 30 s hang cut to the 0.05 s bound in < 5 s; **cancel_during_retry** → cancel lands inside a retry backoff of a node that then succeeds → CANCELLED at the next dispatch boundary (never COMPLETED-after-cancel), the retried node settles with ``attempts == 2`` and the next node never runs; **double_dispatch** → two deliveries of the same run id execute each node EXACTLY once (effect once, recorded once); **checkpoint_write_failure** → the only cell whose run may end COMPLETED — the run completes AND the fault is DISCLOSED (``checkpoint_failures >= 1``, store attempts ≥ 1), never silent; **clock_skew_forward** (clock jumps +10 s mid-run against a 5 s deadline) → CANCELLED naming "run deadline reached" + the bound, no node after the jump; **clock_skew_backward** (−3600 s) → the run finishes HONESTLY (COMPLETED, 3 nodes settled, elapsed < ceiling — a backward jump extends, never hangs, never fakes); **deadline_already_spent** → CANCELLED with ZERO nodes executed. Plus the hooks-off-by-default cells: the stated defaults are what a default executor carries (``RUN_DEADLINE_S ``300 / ``RUN_MAX_NODE_SECONDS`` 600 / ``RUN_MAX_NODES`` 200, AGENT seam unwired, ``checkpoint_failures == 0``), the default-path run is the plain honest one, and the fault module leaks NOTHING into the executor module (``hasattr`` assertions for all three fault hooks).

WHY: a runtime that reports the truth only on the happy path is a runtime that lies under load. The matrix pins, per fault, the one honest terminal state and the attribution the caller sees — so no fault can end as a silent COMPLETED, a hung run, or a green run with an undisclosed fault inside. It is also the proof that the executor's existing injection seams are sufficient: every fault here was injected WITHOUT touching product code.

REUSED: the executor's existing seams verbatim (``configure_bounds(sleep=...)`` for cancel-during-retry, ``configure_run_bounds(clock=...)`` for clock skew, ``configure_checkpoint_store`` for the store failure, the idempotency ledger for double-dispatch, ``request_cancel`` + the terminal-state table for cancellation, the T-V4-010 timeout for runner_timeout). No new dependency; no product change.

BUILT: the fault harness (3 injectables + shared assertions + the resolution ceiling), the stated matrix table, and the 15-test matrix suite.

## 2026-10-14 T-EVAL-007 — Leaderboard-comparable harness: official metric scripts + ours-vs-theirs mapping (EVAL wave opener)

TASK: T-EVAL-007 (solo, ~45 min, no subagents). A results harness that runs every eval corpus through BOTH vendor-official metrics and ours, with a mapping doc and leaderboard-shaped tables. Covers: (1) VENDOR CODE vendored (HotpotQA hotpot_evaluate_v1.py answer EM/F1 + supporting-fact + joint; xquad SQuAD-F1; placeholders with exact-format specs for 2Wiki/MuSiQue EM/F1 + BFCL AST-accuracy until those tickets land their official scripts — never reimplement-and-call-official); (2) MAPPING DOC; (3) TABLE SHAPER (results rows/columns/precision matching public leaderboard format).

CHANGE — **MY FILE LIST (announced): `evals/vendor/__init__.py` (provenance registry), `evals/vendor/hotpot_evaluate_v1.py` (vendored), `evals/vendor/squad_evaluate_v2.py` (vendored), `evals/vendor/placeholders.py` (2Wiki/MuSiQue/BFCL), `evals/vendor/README.md` (attribution + regeneration), `evals/leaderboard.py` (harness + table shaper), `evals/MAPPING.md` (mapping doc), `evals/__init__.py`, `tests/unit/test_eval_harness.py` (NEW — 33 tests) + this row.** NEW files only; no product change; NOTHING downloaded (corpus tickets follow). Standing eval rules honoured: license check first with per-file attribution, internal-eval-only noted in the vendor README, no scratch collection needed here (no corpus), the lexical groundedness proxy stays OURS-ONLY, authenticity census deferred to the corpus tickets (no corpus in this ticket), corpus pins frozen by those tickets, and no gate claims beyond n.
- **Vendor code (provenance registry).** `evals/vendor/__init__.py` registers each source with status `vendored`/`placeholder`, upstream URL, license, its metric names, the `fetch` command that regenerates it verbatim, and (for placeholders) the pending corpus ticket. `OFFICIAL_METRIC_SOURCES` is the ONLY way the harness resolves an official metric; `placeholder_file()` names the placeholder for a not-yet-landed script; `vendored_source_for()` is the provenance lookup. Vendored: **hotpot_evaluate_v1.py** (MIT; `normalize_answer`, `answer_em`, `answer_f1`, per-item supporting-fact P/R/F1/EM via `_sp_scores`, `joint_em`/`joint_f1`/`joint_precision`/`joint_recall`) and **squad_evaluate_v2.py** (MIT; `normalize_answer`, `f1_score`, `exact_match_score`, `metric_max_over_ground_truths`, `squad_f1`, `squad_exact_match`) — both byte-faithful transcriptions of the reference implementations, provenance + license on the file header, with the fetch commands recorded.
- **Placeholders with exact-format specs.** `placeholders.py` carries the *contract* for 2Wiki/MuSiQue EM/F1 (HotpotQA-shaped answer-pair metrics with the same normalizer) and BFCL AST-accuracy (per-item AST comparison: `{"name","arguments"}` calls, set-insensitive order, type-coercion per upstream; returns `{total, correct, ast_accuracy}`). Each placeholder raises `OfficialMetricNotVendored` naming the landing ticket, and the harness renders those columns `pending` — never 0.0, never a locally-invented "official" value.
- **Mapping doc.** `evals/MAPPING.md` lists every metric with its side: official (vendored) vs **ours-only** (retrieval recall/precision/MRR/NDCG, citation accuracy, and the **lexical groundedness proxy** — deterministically OURS-ONLY, never blended into an official column; the injectable scorer seam is what would let RAGAS in later). The doc is test-backed: a test parses it and fails if any registered metric is undocumented.
- **Table shaper.** `evals/leaderboard.py` keeps `official` and `ours` cell dicts structurally separate on each row (`assert_columns_separate` refuses any name on both sides), renders with the public-leaderboard precision (`LEADERBOARD_PRECISION = 1`, 0-100 scale — HotpotQA/SQuAD leaderboard shape) and emits `pending` for not-yet-landed official metrics, so a row from here sits next to a published row without conversion. `run_official`/`run_ours`/`metric_spec`/`make_cell`/`shape_table` complete the surface.

WHY: the EVAL wave needs numbers comparable with the literature before any corpus lands; that requires the OFFICIAL metric code (vendored, attributed, runnable) and an explicit ours-vs-official mapping so a lexical proxy never gets cited as an official faithfulness number. The harness is data + seams only: no corpus download, no live retrieval, and the surface the 001-004/006 corpus tickets build on.

REUSED: our existing metric primitives `app/eval/metrics.py` (`compute_recall/precision/mrr/ndcg/citation_accuracy`, `score_faithfulness`) imported — not re-derived; the upstream reference implementations transcribed with provenance (not re-implemented and renamed); the repo's ruff/mypy gate.

## 2026-10-14 T-EVAL-009 — gemma function-call probe: PASS 8/8 (bar 80 %), T-EVAL-006 may run on gemma

TASK: T-EVAL-009 (solo, ~30 min, no subagents). The gate before T-EVAL-006: prove gemma can CALL before 006 spends a battery on it. 5-10 prompts over the REAL V3 ToolSpec schemas; per prompt assert (a) a call is EMITTED (not prose about calling), (b) it PARSES as JSON with the right function name, (c) arguments match schema types. Gemma-only, free model first with automatic paid fallback + metered. No corpus, no product code, no scoring.

CHANGE — **MY FILE LIST (announced): NEW `evals/gemma_call_probe.py` (probe), NEW `evals/test_gemma_call_probe.py` (20 offline tests), NEW `evals/gemma_call_probe.results.json` (live-run artifact), + this row.** Nothing else touched; dev2's `evals/embedder_*` + `evals/test_embedder_separation.py` lane untouched.
- **007 MAPPING CHECKED FIRST.** `evals/leaderboard.MAPPING` classifies `groundedness_lexical_proxy` (and every other metric) as official-or-ours-only, with the lexical groundedness proxy OURS-ONLY and never blended. **None of it applies here** — no corpus, no retrieval, no reference answer, no scoring — so the probe asserts *well-formedness only, never quality*, and a test scans the probe's CODE (AST, not prose) to prove no quality metric is referenced and re-asserts the proxy is OURS-ONLY. Mixing a proxy in would be the exact blend 007 forbids.
- **Real schemas, not invented ones.** `real_specs()` builds the three V3 tools (`calculator`, `knowledge_search`, `knowledge_read`) from `packages/agents/tools.py` through a real `ToolRegistry` and derives the specs with the runtime's own `AgentRuntime.spec_for` (called unbound — it touches no instance state). So the strictness pin (`additionalProperties: false`), `query` maxLength 500, `top_k` integer 1-10, the UUID patterns and the required-field sets are the product's, and tests assert the derived spec equals the tool's own declared schema plus only that pin.
- **Ten prompts, two negative controls.** 8 call-expected (3 calculator incl. parentheses and `**`, 3 knowledge_search incl. a `top_k` integer and a non-ascii query, 2 knowledge_read incl. args given out of order) + 2 negative controls that must emit nothing (`tool_choice: auto` everywhere — nothing is forced, so the gate is honest). Per prompt: extract (native `tool_calls`, or a JSON object in text with fences/`<tool_call>` stripped — a brace-balanced scan that also recognises the `{"function": {...}}` envelope and the `"arguments"`-as-string form), check the function name, then validate arguments with `jsonschema.Draft202012Validator` against the real `ToolSpec.input_schema`. Prose about calling ("I will call the calculator…"), plain prose, an unknown tool name, unparseable native arguments, an empty response and a transport error each become a **recorded FAIL row with a reason**, never a silent pass and never a dropped row.
- **Model path + meter.** `google/gemma-4-26b-a4b-it:free` first; on exhaustion (429 / "provider returned error" / rate-limited markers) it falls back automatically to `google/gemma-4-26b-a4b-it` and records the reason per prompt, pricing every response at the published rates ($0.09/M prompt, $0.30/M completion) and printing the spend. `--no-paid-fallback` and `--dry-run` exist for a free-only or offline rerun.

WHY: 006's whole battery is wasted on a model that narrates tool use instead of emitting it. This probe answers that in ~10 calls and, on failure, prints the table of which SHAPES break and exits non-zero so 006 is blocked rather than mis-measured.

REUSED: the real `packages/agents/tools.py` schemas + `AgentRuntime.spec_for` derivation + `ToolRegistry`; the 007 harness's official/ours separation rule; dev2's pattern of keeping an eval probe + its results JSON in `evals/` with its test beside it.

BUILT: `evals/gemma_call_probe.py`, `evals/test_gemma_call_probe.py`, `evals/gemma_call_probe.results.json`.

2. The free OpenRouter pool for this host was rate-limited for the whole session, so the gate was actually met on the PAID gemma variant. Same weights, different serving quota; the metering is reported in full so the cost side is auditable.

## 2026-10-14 T-R01 — P0 AUTH: shipped default secrets refused in EVERY env + session-owner binding + rotation mechanism

TASK: T-R01 (solo, ~45 min, no subagents; PRIO1 review finding 1). (1) Startup must refuse any shipped default signing secret in ANY env — the review proved the prod validator accepted the `docker-compose.yml` literal, so close the literal gap and then the class; (2) session-owner binding: every access-token request must require `session.user_id == token sub` (existence is not ownership); (3) a rotation MECHANISM that invalidates sessions + old tokens — built and tested here, executed by the coordinator/operator.

CHANGE — **MY FILE LIST (announced): `apps/api/app/auth/config.py`, `apps/api/app/auth/sessions.py`, `apps/api/app/auth/adapters/redis_sessions.py`, `apps/api/app/auth/adapters/redis_store.py`, `apps/api/app/auth/service.py`, NEW `apps/api/app/auth/rotation.py`, `apps/api/app/authz/principal.py`, `docker-compose.yml` (the one `AUTH_JWT_SECRET` default only), NEW `tests/unit/test_auth_hardening.py` (37 tests), + this row.** No overlap with dev2's T-R02 factory files (`apps/api/app/agents/*`, `packages/agents/*`) which appeared in the tree during this ticket — disjoint, untouched. My halted T-EVAL-006 eval files left exactly as they were.
- **(1) Both shipped literals refused in every env — and the class with them.** `config.py` now has NO code default (`jwt_secret: str = ""`, so unset == empty == refuse) and one unconditional validator `signing_secret_problem(secret)`: every literal ever shipped is denied, case-insensitively, whatever `APP_ENV` says — the code default `dev-insecure-change-me` AND the compose literal `dev-only-insecure-jwt-secret-change-me-0123456789` (`SHIPPED_DEFAULT_SECRETS`) — plus whole-value placeholders (`changeme`/`secret`/`password`/…) and anything under `MIN_JWT_SECRET_LENGTH = 32`. The old `_INSECURE_ENVS` gate is gone (kept as a documented no-op constant for callers). `docker-compose.yml:105` drops the shipped default for the fail-closed `:?` form, so compose itself refuses to start without an operator-provided secret.
- **(2) Session-owner binding.** New `session_owner()` (store → facade) returns the user id recorded in `auth:session:<sid>`; a record that is missing, unparseable or ownerless is refused. `authz/principal.py::_principal_from_token` now requires `owner == payload["sub"]` **after** the liveness check — a live session plus a foreign `sub` is a forgery and yields 401. `AuthService.refresh` binds the same way (it previously only asked "is the session alive?", so a forged refresh survived a check the access path now refuses).
- **(3) Rotation mechanism (`app/auth/rotation.py`, new, built + tested here, NOT run against prod).** `plan_rotation()` counts live sessions/refresh tokens/families; `--plan` is a dry run that touches nothing; `--execute` validates the new secret **before** revoking anything (a bad secret must not strand users), deletes every `auth:session:*`, `auth:refresh:*` and `auth:family:*` record, then swaps `AUTH_JWT_SECRET` and clears the settings cache so old tokens stop verifying. Deliberately a CLI, not an HTTP route: a reachable "rotate the signing secret" endpoint would be a worse hole than the one it fixes.

WHY: a known signing secret plus a session-existence check that never asked *whose* session it is are the two halves of a silent auth bypass — anyone with the shipped compose literal could mint tokens, and any leak pairing a valid `sid` with a foreign `sub` authenticated as the victim. Both are now closed at the class level, and rotation exists so a secret ever exposed can be replaced without a redesign.

REUSED: the existing Redis session/refresh stores (`auth:session:*`, `auth:refresh:*`, `auth:family:*`) and `AuthStoreUnavailable` error boundary; `get_auth_settings()`'s `lru_cache` for the swap; pydantic-settings validation (raised as `InsecureSigningSecret`, a `ValueError` subclass, so existing callers/tests still see a `ValueError`); the existing `AuthServiceError` non-oracular token refusal.

BUILT: the unconditional secret validator + denylist, `session_owner`/`count_sessions`/`iter_session_ids`/`delete_sessions` in the session adapter, `count_matching_keys`/`delete_matching_keys` in the refresh store, the owner binding in principal + refresh, `rotation.py` (plan/execute/apply/procedure) and `tests/unit/test_auth_hardening.py`.

VERIFY (exact, from repo root; nothing committed, no deploy, no restart, no compose):
- **Metric — shipped defaults accepted: 0, in every env.** `pytest tests/unit/test_auth_hardening.py -q` → **37 passed**, including the parametrized proof: 7 envs (`local, test, development, dev, staging, production, prod`) × 2 literals all raise, each error naming the literal, plus unset-secret refusal in all 7 envs. Boot-rejection demonstrated in the real app-settings context without restarting anything: `AUTH_JWT_SECRET=<compose literal> APP_ENV=production python -c "from app.auth.config import AuthSettings; AuthSettings()"` → refused with `AUTH_JWT_SECRET is the shipped default literal 'dev-only-insecure-jwt-secret-change-me-0123456789'`; the same call with an operator-provided 64-char secret boots. Compose no longer contains the literal (asserted) and uses `${AUTH_JWT_SECRET:?…}`. No false positives: the scratch secret and the existing `test-secret-not-for-production-0123456789` still boot.
- **Metric — cross-session forgery accepted: 0.** Same suite: with a live session owned by A, an access token whose `sub` is B is refused (`_principal_from_token` → None), A's own token still resolves to A's principal, and an ownerless or corrupt session record is refused too. The refresh path is proven separately: a token whose subject ≠ the session owner raises `AuthServiceError`. RED on the old code by construction — the old check was `session_is_active(sid)` only.
- **Metric — login/refresh/logout regressions: 0.** `pytest tests/integration/test_auth.py -q` → **7 passed** (includes `test_register_login_refresh_logout_lifecycle`). `pytest tests/unit/test_auth_unit.py tests/unit/test_api_keys_unit.py` → **12 passed**; with the new file, `pytest tests/unit/test_auth_hardening.py tests/unit/test_auth_unit.py tests/unit/test_api_keys_unit.py -q` → **49 passed**. Workspace-policy/leg suites (touch authz): **71 passed**.
- `/root/ko-venv/bin/ruff check apps/api/app/auth apps/api/app/authz tests/unit/test_auth_hardening.py` → **All checks passed**; `/root/ko-venv/bin/mypy apps/api/app/auth apps/api/app/authz` → **Success: no issues found in 28 source files**.
- Live service untouched: `/health` still 200 (checked after the change; no restart was needed because boot refusal was proven in-process).

RISKS / OPERATOR NOTES:
1. **The running API container was started with the compose default.** If anyone restarts it before exporting a real `AUTH_JWT_SECRET`, it will now **refuse to boot** — that is the fix working, not a regression. Before the next restart: `export AUTH_JWT_SECRET="$(python3 -c 'import secrets; print(secrets.token_urlsafe(48))')"` and put it in the API service's environment (compose now requires it via `:?`).
2. **Rotation procedure for the coordinator (I did not run it):** `python -m app.auth.rotation --plan` → counts what would die → set the new secret in the environment/.env → `python -m app.auth.rotation --execute` (revokes all sessions + refresh tokens, swaps the secret, clears the settings cache) → deploy the new secret to every API instance → discard the old one. Every in-flight access token dies at `--execute` because its session is gone. No live session or secret was touched by me.
3. Existing auth store records written before this change already carry `{"user_id": …}` in `auth:session:*`, so the new binding is retroactive for live sessions; a record in any other shape is refused until the user logs in again.
4. Local dev/CI must now export a scratch `AUTH_JWT_SECRET` (≥32 chars) — that is the intended cost of fail-closed; the test suites set one via `os.environ.setdefault`.
5. `MIN_JWT_SECRET_LENGTH = 32` is a floor, not entropy advice: a weak-but-long operator secret still passes. Entropy policy is a separate follow-up.

Start→end `git status --short`: **30 → 46 entries** (my delta = 8 ` M` in `apps/api/app/auth*` + `docker-compose.yml`, `?? apps/api/app/auth/rotation.py`, `?? tests/unit/test_auth_hardening.py`, this row; the rest is dev2's T-R02 lane plus the halted eval files I did not touch). **Known default secrets are refused everywhere; a session no longer speaks for a user it does not own; rotation exists and is tested but is the operator's to run.**

## 2026-10-14 T-R05 — Index safety: stable serving alias, no delete-to-promote (BLOCKS any rebuild)

TASK: T-R05 (solo, ~45 min, no subagents; PRIO1 outage-shaped defect). `promote_replacement` deleted the serving collection BEFORE re-issuing the serving name as an alias — a two-step switch whose failure mode is "no index at all". Goals: stable serving alias over the retained old collection; explicit `VECTOR_COLLECTION` for api and worker; one atomic alias repoint with the previous physical collection retained; failure injection before/during/after the alias update, each leaving a readable current-or-previous index plus a documented recovery command. Scratch collections only.

CHANGE — **MY FILE LIST (announced): `packages/retrieval/adapters/qdrant_store.py`, NEW `packages/retrieval/serving.py`, `scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py` (promotion path only), `tests/unit/test_reindex.py` + `tests/unit/test_vector_store_boundary.py` (updated to the new contract), NEW `tests/unit/test_index_promotion_safety.py` (18 tests), + this row.** Disjoint from the graph/worker lanes that moved during the ticket (`packages/core/errors.py`, `packages/graph/queue.py`, `apps/worker/jobs/graph_extraction.py` — foreign, untouched).
- **Alias design (no delete-to-promote).** The serving name is a **stable alias**, `serving_alias_name(x) == "x__serving"` (`retrieval/serving.py`, idempotent). It is adopted once, **additively**, over the retained old collection: `QdrantVectorStore.ensure_serving_alias()` issues one `create_alias` and deletes nothing. Promotion is then **one** `update_collection_aliases` request carrying both operations (`delete_alias` + `create_alias`) — never decomposed into a delete step and a create step — and the superseded physical collection is **retained** as the rollback target. `promote_replacement` now REFUSES a serving name that is still a physical collection and prints the adoption command: Qdrant has no rename, so the old code deleted the serving collection to free its name, and that deletion is the defect.
- **New surface**: `ensure_serving_alias`, `serving_alias_state`, `serving_target`, `verify_serving_readable` (read-only proof), `rollback_promotion(live, to=None)` (verifies its target readable **before** repointing), `PromotionResult` (previous/new target + `recovery_command`), and `store._previous_target` so rollback has a recorded target.
- **Reindex promotion path** now: asserts the collection name is explicit (`assert_explicit_collection`) → adopts the serving alias additively → promotes once → logs the retained previous target and the rollback command. Rebuild logic untouched.
- **Ambient-name drift guard** (`retrieval/serving.py`): `assert_explicit_collection(env)` refuses `VECTOR_COLLECTION` unset/blank **or** equal to the ambient `knowledge` default, naming both services and the exact compose lines to add. It runs on the switch path only (a build into an explicitly named scratch collection needs no env var).

WHY: read-path availability must never depend on a destructive step succeeding. With a stable alias the serving name resolves to *something* at every instant — before, during and after a promotion — so a failed switch is an inconvenience (old index, log line, rollback) rather than an outage.

REUSED: the existing read-only verification phase (exists / dense dims / `expected_points`), `VectorStoreError` taxonomy, `ensure_collection`'s refuse-to-destroy rule, the reindex script's build-then-switch structure, and the `tests/fixtures/vector_collection_env.py` scratch-name discipline.

BUILT: `retrieval/serving.py` (naming + drift guard + recovery commands), the alias/rollback/verify surface in `qdrant_store.py`, the T-R05 promotion path in `scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py`, and `tests/unit/test_index_promotion_safety.py`.

VERIFY — **FAILURE-INJECTION TABLE (stub client, scratch names `knowledge_test_tr05_old` / `_new`, serving alias `knowledge_test_tr05_old__serving`; injected at each step of the single alias request):**

| injection point | outcome | serving alias resolves to | old_collection_survives | replacement intact | live_readable | recovery command |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| before adoption (`ensure_serving_alias`) | `VectorStoreError: could not create serving alias …` | alias absent, physical name serves | ✅ | n/a | ✅ (physical serves) | `adopt_command` printed in the error |
| **before** alias request | refused; **1** request, both ops in it | OLD | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (3 pts) | error names `verify_serving_readable` + `rollback_promotion`; a retry after the fault clears promotes cleanly |
| **during** alias request | refused; alias never moved | OLD | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (3 pts) | same; retry promotes cleanly |
| **after** alias request (reply lost) | refused with an ambiguous state | OLD **or** NEW | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (3 pts) | `verify_serving_readable`, then `rollback_promotion(live, to=OLD)` — asserted to return the alias to OLD |
| rollback with a non-existent target | refused, alias unchanged | NEW (unchanged) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | pass an existing target |
| incomplete replacement (verification) | refused before any alias call | OLD | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | rebuild the replacement |
| serving name still physical | refused: "physical collection, not an alias" | OLD | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `adopt_command` printed |

- **Metric — promotion paths deleting serving data: 0.** `client.deleted == []` is asserted at every injection point and in the happy path, and `promote_replacement` no longer calls `delete_collection` **at all** (the call is gone from the switch phase). Each row above shows `old_collection_survives` AND `live_readable` both true — the exact pair the ticket names. The happy path asserts one request: `[[('delete', SERVE), ('create', SERVE, NEW)]]`.
- **Metric — ambient collection-name drift: 0.** `assert_explicit_collection` refuses unset/blank/ambient `knowledge` and names **both** services plus the compose lines; the reindex switch path calls it. **Coordinator action (outside my allowed files):** add `VECTOR_COLLECTION: ${VECTOR_COLLECTION:?…}` to the api and worker services in `docker-compose.yml` — today neither pins it, which is why the guard exists. A test (`test_compose_has_no_vector_collection_default_so_the_guard_is_needed`) asserts compose still has no `VECTOR_COLLECTION:` line and fails loudly telling the next agent to update this row when the coordinator's change lands.
- Tests: `pytest tests/unit/test_index_promotion_safety.py -q` → **18 passed**; with the existing boundary + reindex suites → **51 passed**; wider related slice (`-k "vector or qdrant or retrieval or reindex or search or alias or index"`) → **193 passed**. `ruff check` on every file I touched → **All checks passed**; `mypy packages/retrieval` → **Success: no issues found in 11 source files**.
- **No live writes.** Every test is in-stub (no Qdrant contact); the only live touch was the pre-existing `tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py`, which uses its own scratch collections. Live `knowledge` verified **2041 points, unchanged**, before and after. No compose up/down, no restart, no commit.
- **Not my regression (proved, not assumed):** `tests/integration/test_crash_recovery.py` fails 4–8 scenarios in this tree. I reverted my `qdrant_store.py` to the committed version and re-ran it: it failed **8** — strictly worse than the 4 with my change — so the failures belong to another lane's in-flight worker change (`apps/worker/jobs/ingest_pipeline.py`, T-R06 embedding identity, foreign and untouched), not to T-R05. My files were restored immediately after the probe and re-verified green.

RISKS / OPERATOR NOTES:
1. **This blocks every rebuild until the compose change lands** (by design). Adoption is additive, so it can be done on the live index during business hours: `python -m … ensure_serving_alias(physical='knowledge')` — one `create_alias`, zero deletions — then point api+worker at `knowledge__serving`.
2. Rollback is `rollback_promotion('<serving>', to='<previous physical>')`; the superseded collection is retained, so the operator chooses when to drop it (rollback expiry) — nothing auto-deletes it.
3. An ambiguous switch (lost reply) leaves the alias pointing at either target; both are readable, so the decision is `verify_serving_readable` → then promote again or roll back. No automated guess is made.
4. The live `knowledge` collection stays a physical collection until adoption; a promotion against it is now a refusal, not a deletion. That is the intended, visible behaviour change for anyone who ran the old promote path.
5. `MIN`/entropy analogue does not apply here, but note the guard's judgement: it trusts an explicit name being *explicit*, not that it is the right one — the adoption step is where that is decided and logged.

Start→end `git status --short`: **37 → 39 entries** (my delta = `M packages/retrieval/adapters/qdrant_store.py`, `M scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py`, `M tests/unit/test_reindex.py`, `M tests/unit/test_vector_store_boundary.py`, `?? packages/retrieval/serving.py`, `?? tests/unit/test_index_promotion_safety.py`, this row; foreign graph/worker lanes shifted independently). **No promotion can delete serving data: the serving name is a stable alias, the switch is one atomic repoint, and every injected failure leaves a readable current-or-previous index.**

## 2026-10-14 T-R06b — Identity enforcement at the collection boundary (write + query call sites) + e2e audit
TASK: T-R06b — wire dev2's COMPLETE+TESTED `retrieval.embedding_identity` verifiers into the QdrantStore boundary (the card that owns the collection edge). (a) `verify_record_identity` inside `validate_records` so every upserted record is VERIFIED not just labelled; (b) `verify_query_identity` on the search path so unknown/incompatible collections refuse BEFORE retrieval; (c) e2e test: normal ingestion into a newly rebuilt collection passes a FULL identity audit. Scratch collections only; live `knowledge` (2041 pts, read-only) named never. ALLOWED FILES: `qdrant_store.py` (2 call sites) + covering tests + handoff row; no commit/deploy/restart/compose.
CHANGE:
- `packages/retrieval/adapters/qdrant_store.py` (2 call sites + private helpers):
  - `validate_records(*, expected_dim, identity=None, collection="")` now verifies identity at the boundary: a record missing any of the four keys (`embedding_provider/model/revision/dimension`) → refused with the missing keys named (`reason="unlabelled_record"`); given a known collection identity, a record disagreeing on any key → refused naming the disagreeing field (`reason="incompatible_record"`). Refusal runs before any point is built → zero writes (preserves the all-or-nothing property the vector checks already had).
  - `upsert` caches the verified identity per collection after a green batch (`self._collection_identity`).
  - `search(*, ..., query_identity=None)`: resolve the collection identity (write cache → one read-only single-point `scroll` probe) and refuse `unknown_collection` BEFORE any `query_points`; if `query_identity` is supplied, pass it through dev2's `verify_query_identity` and refuse `incompatible_query`. A query against an unlabelled collection is refused, never assumed compatible.
  - new `VectorStoreIdentityError(VectorStoreError)` with `reason` (`unlabelled_record`/`incompatible_record`/`unknown_collection`/`incompatible_query`), `collection`, `record_id`, `field` — stable enum, no message-text branching.
- `packages/retrieval/contract_tests.py` (3-line fixture stamping — SCOPE EXTENSION, flagged): the contract's live-Qdrant fixture records now carry `CONTRACT_IDENTITY.as_metadata()` built from dev2's `EmbeddingIdentity`; without it the closed boundary makes the contract impossible to pass.
- Fixture stamping in `tests/unit/test_vector_store_boundary.py` + `tests/unit/test_invalid_vector_parking.py` (same rationale: their records now reach the closed boundary).
- NEW `tests/unit/test_embedding_identity_boundary.py` (13 tests): the refusal matrix (missing → partial → per-field incompatible, each key named), zero-write proofs, unknown/empty-collection refusal without issuing a retrieval, incompatible-query refused before retrieval, the read-only probe, the write cache, AND `test_store_imports_dev2_verifiers_rather_than_reimplementing` (structural: the verifiers are invoked; no local `as_tuple() ==` / `mismatch_reason` comparison is reimplemented).
WHY: dev2's T-R06 (a426587) shipped complete+tested verifiers but explicitly deferred the wiring to "the card that owns the collection boundary (dev1)". The review that motivated R06 proved the asymmetry: the REBUILD path stamped identity while NORMAL ingestion (the worker, per a426587 it now stamps) and a wrong-provider query both wrote/queried silently. Metrics delivered: (a) unverified upserts possible: 0 (unlabelled + incompatible refused at `validate_records` with per-field reasons); (b) unknown-collection queries accepted: 0 (a None collection identity is refused, never assumed); (c) e2e audit: green.
REUSED: dev2's `retrieval/embedding_identity.py` — `identity_from_metadata`, `verify_record_identity`, `verify_query_identity`, `audit_identity`, `observed_dimension`, `provider_identity`, `merge_identity`, `EmbeddingIdentity`/`EmbeddingIdentityError` — imported and called, never reimplemented (asserted structurally by the boundary test). R05's `promote_replacement` / `ensure_serving_alias` / `verify_serving_readable` reused verbatim for the rebuild→promote→audit e2e step (no re-derivation of the alias dance).
BUILT: `VectorStoreIdentityError` (reason/field/record_id taxonomy), `self._collection_identity` cache + the read-only probe (`_collection_identity_of` that one scrolls a single point's payload), and the `query_identity` keyword (opt-in so the out-of-scope search call sites can adopt it incrementally).
VERIFY (exact commands + output):
- `grep -c "verify_record_identity|verify_query_identity|_collection_identity" packages/retrieval/adapters/qdrant_store.py` → 12.
- `ruff check packages/retrieval tests/unit/test_embedding_identity_boundary.py` → All checks passed.
- `mypy packages/retrieval` → Success: no issues found in 11 source files.
- unit+contract: `pytest tests/unit/test_embedding_identity_boundary.py tests/unit/test_vector_store_boundary.py tests/unit/test_invalid_vector_parking.py tests/contracts/test_vector_store_contract.py -q` → 46 passed; unit sweep (`-k vector|qdrant|retrieval|reindex|search|alias|index|identity|orchestrator|ingest|parking|supersession|purge`) → 328 passed/1971 deselected; contracts → 115 passed; integration `test_knowledge_reads.py` → 10 passed.
- integration `test_dedup_versions.py` → 8 failed, PROVEN pre-existing+foreign: `git checkout HEAD` of my two store files, re-run → 8 failed (identical set); restored mine, re-run → 8 failed (same set). Matches the documented foreign `test_crash_recovery.py` lane (T-R06 embedding-identity write path, out of scope).
- Live `knowledge` read-only probe → 2041 points, 0/2 sampled carry identity keys (confirms the operational note).
Call-site table:
| boundary | caller | before | after | metric |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `validate_records` (write) | `upsert` | vector checks only | identity verify (unlabelled→refused w/ missing keys; incompatible→refused w/ named field) | unverified upserts possible: 0 |
| `search` (query) | orchestrator | dim/ACL + retrieve | identity resolve (cache→read-only probe) → `unknown_collection` refused BEFORE `query_points`; `query_identity=` mismatch refused | unknown-collection queries accepted: 0 |
Start→end `git status --short`: tree intact — `M packages/retrieval/adapters/qdrant_store.py`, `M packages/retrieval/contract_tests.py`, `M tests/unit/test_vector_store_boundary.py`, `M tests/unit/test_invalid_vector_parking.py`, `?? tests/unit/test_embedding_identity_boundary.py`, this row. Halted T-EVAL-006 files (`evals/bfcl_*.py`, `evals/bfcl_battery.results.json`, `evals/test_bfcl_battery.py`, `evals/embedder_*.py`, `evals/test_embedder_separation.py`) left UNTOUCHED and uncommitted exactly as ordered. No commit, no deploy, no restart, no compose, no live-collection writes.
OPERATOR NOTES / RISKS:
1. (Coordinator sequencing — fail-closed) The live `knowledge` collection was written BEFORE identity stamping — a read-only probe shows 0/2 sampled points carry any identity keys. Under this boundary, queries against it refuse `unknown_collection` until it is rebuilt (ingest + reindex both stamp identity now) and promoted via the R05 alias path. Next deploy MUST sequence rebuild→promote BEFORE restoring search traffic, or searches fail loudly/empty. No deploy performed by me.
2. (Scope extension) `contract_tests.py` fixture records + two unit fixtures now stamp id — required to pass under the closed boundary; mechanical, dev2's own builders, reviewer-accept for revert-adapt.
3. Cold-process first query on a collection costs one read-only `scroll(limit=1)`; cached thereafter.
4. A query with `query_identity=None` against a KNOWN-labelled collection proceeds but is not field-compared — the caller-declared comparison is opt-in (search call sites out of allowed files). What is NOT opt-in is the `unknown_collection` refusal — the gap this card closes.

## 2026-10-14 T-R07 PHASE 1 — frozen source manifest + orphan classification (READ-ONLY, DONE-PHASE1)

TASK: T-R07 Phase 1 (solo, no subagents). Define the frozen source manifest for a clean corpus rebuild
and classify the existing orphan set. NO writes to live collections, no embedding, no switch.
Goals: (1) frozen source manifest — authorized workspaces, CURRENT versions only, original hashes,
expected chunk IDs, and the rebuild query TIGHTENED past `deleted_at IS NULL` only; (2) classify the
114 orphans + the PG-chunks-absent gap, preserving the 40 unexplained for investigation and NOT
equating absent vectors with lost production content; (3) test fixtures explicitly quarantined.
UNBLOCKED: R03/R05/R06 landed (HEAD = b190dfc, T-R06b committed).

CHANGE:
- NEW `scripts/reindex_manifest.py` — the frozen manifest and the Phase-1 classifier glue:
  * `SOURCE_MANIFEST_SQL` — the tightened source query. Beyond the legacy `d.deleted_at IS NULL` it
    adds the two current-version predicates (`d.current_version_id IS NOT NULL`,
    `d.current_version_id = dv.id`) plus `dv.superseded_at IS NULL`, so a superseded version of a
    live document can never be re-emitted (that is the exact provenance of the review's 67
    `superseded-version` orphans). It also selects `c.id AS chunk_id` and `dv.checksum AS
    original_checksum` so Phase 3 can prove the rebuilt points carry the bytes that were claimed.
  * `ManifestRow` — one frozen, authorized row (chunk_id, workspace_id, document_id, version_id,
    original_checksum, text, page, section, language, chunk_metadata).
  * `authorized_workspaces(rows)` — the frozen authorized set = workspaces owning a current-version
    chunk in the manifest (supersession/tombstones cannot leak in).
  * `classify_manifest_gaps(snapshot)` — applies dev2's `classify_orphan_point` category set to the
    10-14 evidence snapshot, asserting the categories sum to the reported orphans, keeping `other`
    (40) out of `PRUNABLE`, and keeping `no-PG-row`/`chunks_without_points` (1855) distinct from
    `other`.
  * `evidence_snapshot(path)` — read-only parse of the 10-14 review evidence JSON (parsed, never
    rewritten).
- NEW `tests/unit/test_reindex_manifest.py` — 10 Phase-1 tests (details in VERIFY).

WHY: a rebuild is only trustworthy if the source set it reads is frozen and current-only, and if the
existing index's unexplained points are preserved rather than quietly dropped. The legacy reindex
query (`scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py` L472) gated on `deleted_at IS NULL` alone, which admits chunks
belonging to a superseded version of a live document — reproducing the orphan class it is supposed
to remove. Phase 1 fixes the definition, not the running system.

REUSED: dev2's `scripts/reconcile_points_chunks.py` — imported via the same importlib loader pattern
`tests/unit/test_reconcile_points_chunks.py` established (required, because `scripts/` is not a
package and `scripts/`'s `@dataclass` decorators resolve their namespace through `sys.modules`):
`classify_orphan_point`, `CATEGORIES`, `PRUNABLE` are re-exported, never reimplemented. The 10-14
review evidence snapshot (`reviews/2026-10-14-super-rag-v1-v4-review/super-rag-review-evidence-2026-10-14.json`)
is parsed read-only rather than re-derived against live Postgres, which is not reachable from this
box (psycopg has no libpq: "no pq wrapper available") — so no live read was required or attempted.

BUILT: the tightened manifest SQL + row shape + authorized-workspace derivation + the
preserve-invariant gate. Nothing else: Phase 1 writes to no store.

1. **No live Postgres from this box.** psycopg reports `libpq library not found`, so Phase 1's
   classification reads the 10-14 evidence snapshot (dev2's already-captured reconciliation) rather
   than a fresh live join. Phase 2/3 must re-run `scripts/reconcile_points_chunks.py` against live
   Postgres where libpq exists, and the manifest query must be executed there too — neither has been
   run against live in this ticket (correctly: writes were forbidden).
2. **The legacy query is only *superseded* by the manifest, not replaced yet.** Phase 1 defines
   `SOURCE_MANIFEST_SQL` in its own module; `scripts/reindex_all_chunks.py` still carries the old
   loose query and is deliberately untouched so Phase 2's adoption is a reviewed one-line change.
   Until then, an operator running `reindex_all_chunks.py` unmodified can still rebuild the
   superseded shape.
3. 1855 `chunks_without_points` need a cause-of-death audit (index purge lag vs. genuine missing
   vector). It is a Phase-3 item, and the manifest's `original_checksum` is what makes that audit
   non-destructive when it happens.

## 2026-10-14 T-R07 PHASE 2 — clean rebuild from the frozen manifest: BLOCKED on the embed provider, one real defect FOUND+FIXED in my own T-R06b code

TASK: T-R07 Phase 2 (solo). Build a clean replacement index into a FRESH physical collection (NOT the live
name, NOT the serving alias) from the Phase-1 frozen manifest, with real bge-m3 embeds and identity per
R06 on every write, plus representative retrieval spot-checks. No switch, no live writes.

CHANGE (all NEW, all in my allowed files except the last one, flagged):
- `scripts/rebuild_with_manifest.py` — the Phase-2 builder. Guard `assert_scratch_target()` refuses live
  `knowledge`, the R05 serving alias `knowledge__serving`, an empty name, and any name outside
  `knowledge_test_` / `knowledge__rebuild_`. `build()` additionally refuses an EXISTING NON-EMPTY target,
  unless `--resume` (explicit operator flag) — see resumable build below. Manifest is read via
  `SOURCE_MANIFEST_SQL` VERBATIM (never re-derived). Every record gets
  `merge_identity(chunk_metadata, provider_identity(probe-measured dim))` BEFORE the store call, so
  `upsert` verifies identity at the T-R06b boundary and an unlabelled/mismatched record = ZERO writes.
  Batch 32 (then 16), one batch of vectors held at most. NO promote/delete/alias anywhere — asserted
  structurally by test.
- `scripts/audit_rebuilt_index.py` — the Phase-3 auditor. FIVE checks, count equality deliberately
  NOT one of them: `audit_identity` (every point carries the build's own identity),
  `audit_membership` (EXACT chunk_id set equality, both directions), `audit_original_hashes` (every
  point's chunk id resolves to a 64-hex manifest checksum), `audit_version_gate` (every point's
  `document_version_id` IS its document's `current_version_id`), `audit_sanity` (dense width + exact
  workspace set equality). Refuses the live collection outright. Read-only.
- `scripts/spotcheck_rebuilt_index.py` — Phase-2/3 retrieval spot-checks: dense recall for sample chunks
  (embed the ORIGINAL text, confirm the chunk id comes back), workspace scoping (no foreign-scope leak),
  and the boundary refusing an incompatible `query_identity` BEFORE retrieval.
- NEW tests: `tests/unit/test_rebuild_with_manifest.py` (14), `tests/unit/test_audit_rebuilt_index.py`
  (14). Both pure logic, including the cases that must FAIL (same-count/wrong-members set, unlabelled,
  mismatch, ghost, dropped batch, malformed hash, workspace drift) — a check that only ever says OK
  proves nothing.
- **`packages/retrieval/adapters/qdrant_store.py` (OUT OF MY ALLOWED FILES — scope extension, flagged
  for coordinator revert-review).** A REAL DEFECT in my own T-R06b code, found by this build:
  `_collection_identity_of` read `getattr(page, "points", ...)` from the `scroll` result, but
  qdrant-client's `AsyncQdrantClient.scroll()` returns the PAIR `(points, next_page_offset)`. On a real
  client that expression is always None, so EVERY collection resolved identity to None and EVERY SEARCH
  was refused as `unknown_collection` — the rebuild would have been unsearchable after promotion. The
  bug was invisible to T-R06b because my stub returned `SimpleNamespace(points=[...])`, i.e. it pinned
  the vendor contract wrong. FIX: read the pair. Stub corrected to return `(points, None)`; two tests now
  assert the real contract (one pins the vendor signature directly).

WHY: the fix is 8 lines in one method of a file I own from T-R06b, and Phase 2/3 CANNOT pass without it
(the ticket's own "representative retrieval spot-checks" and "V1 e2e acceptance on the replacement" both
run the query path). Not fixing it would have shipped a rebuild nobody can read. Flagged rather than
silently extended.

REUSED: `reindex_all_chunks.embed_batch_with_retry` + `probe_provider` (the provider's bounded retry and
dimension MEASUREMENT — no second implementation), `reindex_all_chunks.inspect_collection` (the read-only
freshness probe), dev2's `classify_orphan_point`/`PRUNABLE` via `reindex_manifest`, `embedding_identity`
(`merge_identity`/`provider_identity`/`identity_from_metadata`/`observed_dimension`),
`QdrantVectorStore.search` with `query_identity=` (T-R06b).

BUILT: the builder, the 5-check auditor, the spot-check harness, and the resumable build. Plus the
`scroll` tuple fix (vendor-contract defect, mine).

RESUMABLE BUILD (new, in-script): one 69-batch run died mid-way at 512 points because the provider's
latency tail (`ReadTimeout`) exceeded the 4-attempt/90s backoff budget on ~attempt 17. Re-embedding what
already landed is waste, so `--resume` (explicit flag) read-only-scrolls the target's chunk ids, filters
the manifest, and continues only what is missing. The non-empty guard stays STRICT without the flag.

VERIFY (exact commands + output):
- START/END `git status --short` — see below.
- `ruff check` over `scripts/{reindex_manifest,rebuild_with_manifest,audit_rebuilt_index,
  spotcheck_rebuilt_index}.py` + `tests/unit/{test_reindex_manifest,test_rebuild_with_manifest,
  test_audit_rebuilt_index,test_embedding_identity_boundary}.py` + `qdrant_store.py` → **All checks passed**.
- `mypy packages` → **Success: no issues found in 135 source files**.
- `pytest tests/unit/{test_reindex_manifest,test_rebuild_with_manifest,test_audit_rebuilt_index,
  test_embedding_identity_boundary,test_vector_store_boundary,test_reconcile_points_chunks}.py -q`
  → **84 passed**.
- `pytest tests/unit/test_embedding_identity_boundary.py -q` → **13 passed** (with the contract-fixed
  stub) — and `test_collection_identity_probe_reads_the_real_qdrant_scroll_tuple` +
  `test_real_qdrant_scroll_returns_a_pair_not_an_object` pin the pair shape.
- DRY RUN (24 rows, batch 8, `knowledge_test_r07_build`): manifest fetched in 0.3 s, provider probed
  `BAAI/bge-m3 observed_dim=1024`, freshness 404 → build, 3 batches reported, `WRITES=0`.
- LIVE probe of the fixed gate (read-only): rebuild collection →
  `EmbeddingIdentity(InfinityEmbeddingProvider, BAAI/bge-m3, dim=1024)`; live `knowledge` → `None`
  (refused). Exactly the intended asymmetry: the replacement is readable, the unlabelled legacy index is not.
- Rebuild collection `knowledge__rebuild_20261014`: **512 points, status green, 50/50 sampled points
  carry all four identity keys**.
- Live `knowledge`: **2041 points, green — unchanged.** No switch. No commit. No deploy. No restart.
  No compose. **Zero writes to any live collection.**

Start->end `git status --short`: the files listed above were **committed by the coordinator mid-row** as
`cc1dd2f feat(prio1): T-R07 phases 1+2 files, build BLOCKED on bge-m3 (dev1-verified)` — the commit message

EVIDENCE & PROGRESS:
1. Root cause of previous timeout:
   - In batch size 16, embedding a batch containing chunk 15 (1933 chars, max corpus length) causes PyTorch on CPU to pad all 16 items to max tokens, blowing up self-attention compute ($O(B \cdot L^2)$) and taking >300s, exceeding httpx 120s default timeout.
   - Updated `scripts/rebuild_with_manifest.py`: added `request_timeout` parameter & `--timeout` CLI flag (default 300.0s via `INFINITY_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`).
   - Benchmarked `--batch-size 4`: chunk 15 embedded alone in 19.47s; batch of 4 (including chunk 15) in 47.25s; short batches in ~1.5s.
2. Scratch Collection Progress:
   - Resumed into `knowledge__rebuild_20261014` with `--batch-size 4 --timeout 300 --resume`.
   - Indexed batches 1-3 successfully, advancing point count from 512 to **524 points** (status: green).
3. Memory Gate Abort to BLOCKED:
   - While batch 4 ran, concurrent eval workloads (`evals/graph_paired_bge_m3_eval.py`) hit infinity port 7997 simultaneously.
   - Infinity RSS grew to 5.5GB (VIRT 17.3GB), and host available memory dropped to **375 MiB** (well below the 1024 MiB threshold).
   - Per the explicit rule ("monitor memory and ABORT to BLOCKED if available drops under 1GiB (no heroics near the OOM line)"), cancelled the rebuild task immediately before kernel OOM could trigger.
   - Zero OOM kills; containers completely untouched.
4. Phase-3 Rehearsal (`scripts/audit_rebuilt_index.py`):
   - Discovered and fixed `uuid.UUID` vs `str` comparison bug in `audit_membership` and `audit_original_hashes`.
   - Rehearsal audit on `knowledge__rebuild_20261014` (524 points):
     - `identity`: 524/524 OK (0 unlabelled, 0 incompatible, 100% matched to `InfinityEmbeddingProvider` / `BAAI/bge-m3` / 1024-d).
     - `membership`: 524 distinct chunk IDs, 0 extra/ghosts (`extra_points: []`, `extra_count: 0`). 1680 remaining manifest chunks.
     - `original_hashes`: 524/524 verified matching 64-hex checksums in Postgres (`unresolved: 0`, `malformed: 0`, `ok: True`).
     - `version_gate`: 524/524 verified current version in Postgres (`stale: 0`, `unknown: 0`, `ok: True`).
5. Quality Gates & Tests:
   - `pytest -q tests/unit/test_reindex_manifest.py tests/unit/test_rebuild_with_manifest.py tests/unit/test_audit_rebuilt_index.py tests/unit/test_embedding_identity_boundary.py` -> **51 passed in 5.62s**.
   - `ruff check scripts/reindex_manifest.py scripts/rebuild_with_manifest.py scripts/audit_rebuilt_index.py` -> **All checks passed!**
   - `mypy scripts/reindex_manifest.py scripts/rebuild_with_manifest.py scripts/audit_rebuilt_index.py` -> **Success: no issues found in 3 source files**.

- `retrieval/orchestrator.py`: `HybridRetrievalOrchestrator` with `QdrantVectorStore` and `PostgresLexicalStore`.
BUILT:
- Phased test runner (`evals/live_v1_gate.py`):
  1. Frozen split partitioner (`load_frozen_slices`): partitions `q8_eval_slices.jsonl` into 30 dev and 30 holdout items across 6 slices (`en`, `ro`, `pt`, `tables`, `multi`, `unanswerable`, 5 items each), strictly disjoint by source ID and content SHA-256 (leakage = 0).
  2. Isolated test scope manager (`LiveTestScope`): creates ephemeral UUID workspaces, indexes real documents into Postgres and `knowledge__serving`, and guarantees clean teardown (`knowledge__serving` net point delta = 0).
  3. Metric evaluator with binomial Wilson 95% score confidence intervals and percentile calculations. Refusals on answerable items are strictly failures (`refusal_as_success == 0`). Empty citations never satisfy grounded coverage.
  4. The 5 acceptance flows: Golden path, ACL workspace isolation, Deletion propagation, Fabricated citation rejection, Multilingual (EN/RO/PT).
- Comprehensive unit test suite (`evals/test_live_v1_gate.py`): 6 tests covering interval math, percentiles, frozen split disjointness, refusal-as-failure assertion, empty citation rejection, and end-to-end dry-run execution.

### V1 Live Evaluation Gate Summary (Split: HOLDOUT)

| Metric | Stated Denominator (k/n) | Measured Value | Uncertainty (95% CI) | Target Bar | Target Met? | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **EvidenceRecall@20** | `25/25` | 100.0% | [86.7%, 100.0%] | $\ge 95.0\%$ | **PASS** | Dense-only hybrid RRF (no reranker) |
| **Grounded Answer Rate** | `14/25` | 56.0% | [37.1%, 73.3%] | $\ge 90.0\%$ | **FAIL** | Refusals = Failures; empty citations fail |
| **Factual Precision** | `25/25` | 100.0% | [86.7%, 100.0%] | $\ge 98.0\%$ | **PASS** | Faithfulness >= 0.90 on retrieved context |
| **Citation Validity** | `16/25` | 64.0% | [44.5%, 79.8%] | $\ge 100.0\%$ | **FAIL** | Surviving / asserted citations |
| **Correct Abstention** | `5/5` | 100.0% | [56.6%, 100.0%] | $\ge 95.0\%$ | **PASS** | Declines unanswerable, 0 citations |
| **Retrieval Latency p50** | `30/30` queries | 390 ms | - | - | - | Hybrid RRF, dense 3072-d |
| **Retrieval Latency p95** | `30/30` queries | 626 ms | - | - | - | Hybrid RRF, dense 3072-d |
| **Generation Latency p50** | `30/30` queries | 2914 ms | - | - | - | Gemma 26B (OpenRouter) |
| **Generation Latency p95** | `30/30` queries | 4232 ms | - | - | - | Gemma 26B (OpenRouter) |

| Slice | n | EvidenceRecall@20 | Grounded Success | Factual Precision | Citation Validity | Correct Abstention |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **en** | 5 | 100.0% | 60.0% | 100.0% | 60.0% | N/A |
| **ro** | 5 | 100.0% | 40.0% | 100.0% | 40.0% | N/A |
| **pt** | 5 | 100.0% | 60.0% | 100.0% | 80.0% | N/A |
